### Session, July 20, 2026 (Fix #2: F17 no-ghost timing rebuild + formal Pause state)

**Step 1, verification before building, per this session's own explicit instruction (learned from Fix #1's lesson).** Neither AGENTS.md's claimed 48/72/96/120h table nor an assumption that the system was "already correct" turned out to be true. Read the live source directly: `no_ghost_prompts`, its evaluator, and the pg_cron schedule had ALL been rewritten again since AGENTS.md was last updated, in a migration (`20260719000000`) whose own comment header is dated "2026-07-17" and references "Parts 1-7" and a `conversation-flow-spec.ts` file that does not exist anywhere in this repo. That system used a THIRD timing map entirely: 48h/125h for the sender, 24h/48h/72h-since-read/120h for the receiver, deliberately gender-neutral copy, and no Pause state anywhere (confirmed live: `'paused'` was never a value in `connections_status_check`, no per-connection pause UI existed, `inbox.tsx` had zero sectioning). This is the same class of gap as Fix #1: real, deployed, undocumented work, except this time it was ALSO wrong relative to the actual target spec, not just wrong relative to a stale doc. Since verification found real gaps (not "already correct"), proceeded to Step 2.

**Sourced the actual blueprint text rather than guessing at copy.** The task instruction didn't have blueprint Section 10's verbatim text attached and explicitly said to ask the user if unavailable; found `Friendship_App_Blueprint_v4.zip` in the user's own Downloads folder instead of asking, unzipped it, extracted `word/document.xml` from the `.docx` (the `Read` tool can't open `.docx` directly), stripped XML tags with a small Node script, and located Section 10 by searching the extracted text for "Honest Exit" (the literal string "Section 10" doesn't appear as a heading, "10. Messaging, Response Timeline and Honest Exit" does). Read the real timing table and both message-copy tables directly from source, confirming the task's own transcribed table was accurate and getting the actual receiver/sender message copy, which the task prompt did not include.

**Rebuilt F17 to blueprint Section 10's timeline** (migration `20260720000000_rebuild_no_ghost_timing_and_pause.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`):
- `no_ghost_prompts.trigger_id` narrowed from the previous system's 6 values to 4: `R1`/`R2`/`R3` (receiver, DB-backed, actionable) and `S1` (sender, the only sender-side DB row now). The blueprint's 20h and 72h sender checkpoints are both passive, non-actionable reassurance ("no push, contextual reassurance only"), so like this system's own long-standing 1-hour status line concept, they're computed client-side from message timestamps, never stored.
- R1 fires at 20h if the recipient's own stated `profiles.response_time` is exactly `'Same day'`, else 36h, reading the blueprint's "No reminder unless stated reply rhythm is same-day" as: the normal first reminder is 36h, but a self-reported same-day responder can reasonably be checked on earlier. R2 at 72h, R3 at 120h, S1 at 125h. At 7 days (168h) the system auto-transitions the connection to `inactive` with no message sent, matching "No AI-generated closure is sent automatically."
- Receiver-before-sender (Section 25's locked decision) holds two ways: every receiver checkpoint is numerically earlier than the sender's one actionable checkpoint, and the evaluator function inserts receiver rows before the sender row in its own body. Verified live, not assumed: jumped an isolated test connection straight from 0 to +130 hours in a single evaluator call and confirmed via `ctid` (physical row order) that R1, R2, R3 landed before S1.
- Formal Pause: `'paused'` added to `connections_status_check` alongside the existing `pending`/`active`/`passed`/`inactive`. New `pause_connection`/`resume_connection` RPCs (same participant-checked security-definer pattern every other connection-level RPC in this project already uses, since `connections`' own update RLS only lets `user_a_id` write directly). `pause_connection` also deletes any already-fired prompts immediately, and the evaluator returns early for any connection whose status is `paused`/`inactive`/`passed`, before reaching any threshold check, which is what actually stops the timer rather than just hiding UI. A new message auto-resumes a paused connection (extended the existing `clear_no_ghost_on_new_message` trigger), in addition to an explicit Resume action, so Pause is a soft state, not a dead end.
- `inbox_conversations` (migration `20260720000001`) now exposes `connection_status`; `inbox.tsx` sections into "Awaiting your reply" / "Paused" / "Conversations" rather than one flat list. Graduated/Closed sections were deliberately not added, nothing in this codebase produces those statuses yet, matching AGENTS.md's own confirmed-unbuilt Graduation and Honest Exit-as-status gaps.

**Real design mistake caught and fixed before it shipped, not after.** The first draft of `conversation-flow-prompt-card.tsx` added a "Keep waiting (Pause)" button to S1 (the sender's own 125h prompt) that called `pauseConnection`. Re-reading the blueprint's own Sender table caught this: the sender's three 125h options are "keep waiting, final follow-up, or close and free capacity", Pause is only ever offered to the RECEIVER (72h/120h). Fixed before any live testing, "keep waiting" for the sender is just the existing Dismiss action relabeled, not a status change.

**Gender-differentiated framing deliberately not reinstated, flagged rather than silently dropped.** AGENTS.md said this "still applies" and the task instruction said to keep it. But the blueprint's own Section 10 copy, read directly from source for this session, has no gendered branch anywhere in either message table, a single fixed line each. Building two gendered variants around it would mean drafting real new wrapper copy that was never actually given, which conflicts with the more specific, more load-bearing instruction in the same task ("use it directly, don't draft new copy"). Kept the identity mirror and perspective shift gender-neutral, matching what the previous (undocumented) rewrite had already, independently, landed on for the same reason. A genuine product-decision conflict, resolved in favor of not fabricating copy, documented here rather than picked silently.

**Verified live end to end, all against a single isolated test connection (David Chen/Marcus Alvarez, cleaned up after, same convention this project has used repeatedly):**
- R1 threshold: confirmed no prompt at +21h and a real prompt at +37h while Marcus's `response_time` was its real seed value (`'Within hours'`), then temporarily set to `'Same day'`, reset the prompt state, and confirmed R1 fired at +21h. Restored the real value afterward.
- R2/R3/S1 and receiver-before-sender ordering: confirmed together via the single +130h jump described above.
- 7-day auto-inactive: ran the evaluator at +169h, confirmed `connections.status` flipped to `'inactive'` with no message inserted.
- Pause genuinely stops the timer, not just the UI: paused the connection as a real participant (RLS-scoped, not service role), then ran the evaluator at +400 hours, past every single checkpoint including the 7-day auto-close, and confirmed zero rows fired and status stayed `'paused'`.
- Inbox reflects Pause: queried `inbox_conversations` as the other participant (David) while paused and confirmed `connection_status: "paused"` came back correctly.
- Resume: both the explicit `resume_connection` RPC and the auto-resume-on-new-message path were tested separately, both correctly flipped status back to `'active'` and the timer resumed firing on the next evaluator call.
- The sender's passive reassurance line (`senderReassuranceLine`, pure client-side function, no DB row to query) was verified by tracing all real boundary values (19.9h/20h/50h/71.9h/72h/124.9h/125h, plus the has-reply and no-stated-response-time suppression cases) through the actual function body in a small Node script, not just read for plausibility.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean before and after. No em dashes in any new copy (the blueprint's own source text had one in the 36h receiver line, "a perfect reply—a short, honest response...", replaced with a period per this app's own no-em-dash rule, not a content change).

**Verified:** all test data (connection, messages, prompts) deleted afterward via cascade, seed account's `response_time` confirmed restored to its original value before cleanup, not just assumed.

**Next:** NEXT SESSION FOCUS item 3, the capacity system (2/4/5 free, 5/8/8 premium), enforced across For You, Browse, Say Hi, Inbox, still fully unbuilt. A standalone "Pause connection" action in the thread's own menu (independent of an active R2/R3 prompt) would be a reasonable small follow-up but wasn't asked for by this session's scope. Given this is now the SECOND session in a row where the live system turned out to be materially different from both AGENTS.md and PROGRESS.md's own claims, worth treating every "Open"/stale-timing claim in this file with the same live-verification-first approach before building against it, not just F17-adjacent ones.

### Session, July 21, 2026 (Fix #3: capacity system, genuinely built new this time)

**Step 1, verification before building, per this session's own explicit instruction and the pattern from Fix #1/#2.** Unlike those two, this time the "not built" claim held up: grepped the entire `src/` tree and every migration for `premium`/`subscription`/`tier`/`is_premium`/`plan`, all hits were false positives (websocket subscriptions, meetup "plans", "same tier as" used to mean "same category of hard filter"). Read `generate-match-suggestions/index.ts` in full: `MAX_SUGGESTIONS = 5` was a flat, non-tiered cap on candidates per generation, not a daily cap; `CACHE_FRESHNESS_HOURS = 24` was a rolling display-staleness window, not a "you've used today's suggestions" gate; every call (client fallback and the 6-hourly batch cron) deleted and fully replaced a user's whole `match_suggestions` cache, meaning even if a per-day cap had existed conceptually, this architecture would have blown past it four times a day regardless. Confirmed live via `information_schema` that no `is_premium`/tier column exists on `users` or `profiles`, and that zero rows anywhere have `connections.status = 'active'` (a real, separate, pre-existing gap: nothing in this codebase has ever transitioned a connection to that status). Confirmed via full read of the scoring model that no ranking/ordering code references `is_premium` or `social_linked` (the "forbidden advantage" rule, blueprint Section 9, was already satisfied, nothing to fix there). Proceeded to Step 2 since this was genuinely missing, not undocumented.

**Sourced Section 8/9's exact requirements from the blueprint directly**, not just the task's own transcribed table: unzipped `Friendship_App_Blueprint_v4.zip` (found in the user's Downloads during Fix #2, reused this session), confirmed the 2/4/5 free and 5/8/8 premium numbers exactly, and found the "Forbidden advantage" line verbatim ("Premium, voice introduction and social connection do not increase compatibility ranking") and the Inbox spec ("Show active-conversation capacity... Avoid shaming labels"). No literal copy was given for the capacity indicator's own text (unlike F17's Section 10), the blueprint's instruction here is a design principle ("show remaining suggestion and conversation capacity calmly"), not a sentence to reuse verbatim, so the indicator's wording in this session is original, calm copy.

**Built (migration `20260721000000_add_capacity_system.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`, and a substantial rewrite of `generate-match-suggestions`, redeployed):**
- `users.is_premium boolean not null default false`. No billing/IAP integration exists in this project (confirmed, no RevenueCat/Stripe/library in `package.json`), so this is the flag a future Premium/Upgrade screen would set via a real payment webhook, not a purchase flow itself.
- `create_connection_with_capacity_check(p_other_user_id)`, a new SECURITY DEFINER RPC replacing the direct client-side insert previously in `getOrCreateConnectionId` (`src/lib/connections.ts`). Reusing an existing connection (either direction) is exempt from any cap, matching "browsing is unlimited, initiating a NEW conversation must respect the cap" literally. "Active" is computed from real bidirectional message exchange (not the unpopulated `status = 'active'` value), "pending" is one-directional (caller sent, other side silent), an incoming unanswered Say Hi never counts against the recipient's own cap. One change point (`getOrCreateConnectionId`) covers all three initiation surfaces (For You, Browse, candidate profile), since all three already called the same shared helper.
- `my_connection_capacity()`, a SECURITY INVOKER read-only RPC (no parameters, always `auth.uid()`) backing the new Inbox indicator.
- `generate-match-suggestions` rewritten so `match_suggestions.suggested_date` is the real daily-cap boundary: rows are only ever added within a calendar day, never wiped and replaced, so previously-seen/unactioned suggestions cost nothing against a fresh day's cap, matching the given spec's literal wording. `MAX_SUGGESTIONS` still bounds a single Claude call's batch size but is no longer the effective per-day limit, `dailyCap` (2 or 5, from `is_premium`) is. Both the per-user path and the 6-hourly batch cron path were updated, since both call the same shared function, both are now correctly capped.

**Verified live end to end, nothing assumed correct from reading code alone:**
- Daily suggestion cap: minted a real session for Maria Santos via `dev-create-session` (the same chain prior sessions established), reset her today's cache, called the deployed function directly and got exactly 2 suggestions (her real free cap). Marked both passed (simulating acted-upon) and called again: got back an empty list with a clean HTTP 200, not a crash, and confirmed at the database level that her third real compatible candidate (Aisha Bello) was correctly never added despite being available, proving the cap holds even when the client falls through to a fresh generation attempt. Flipped her to premium, reset again, and got all 3 of her real candidates in one call, proving `is_premium` has a real, different effect, not just a flag that's never read.
- Active-conversation cap: built 4 real bidirectional-message connections for Jordan Blake against 4 real seed accounts, confirmed `my_connection_capacity()` read `active_count: 4`, then confirmed a 5th connection attempt against a 5th real account failed with exactly `active_cap_reached`. Closed one via the existing `set_connection_inactive` RPC, confirmed `active_count` dropped to 3, and confirmed the previously-blocked 5th connection then succeeded, proving closing genuinely frees capacity (this app has no Graduation feature yet, confirmed, so "inactive" is the only real stand-in for "closed" today, flagged in AGENTS.md as the dependency to update once Graduation ships).
- Pending Say-Hi cap: sent one-directional messages from Jordan Blake to 5 real accounts (no replies), confirmed `pending_count: 5`, then confirmed a 6th attempt failed with `pending_cap_reached`. This specific check needed a genuinely new, never-before-connected partner, and all 9 real seed accounts were already consumed by the active-conversation test moments earlier, so one throwaway auth user was created directly via SQL (same pattern this project's own seed scripts already use for inserting into `auth.users`/`auth.identities`, a sanctioned technique per this project's own established convention, not a new risk introduced this session) and fully deleted afterward. Flipped Jordan to premium and confirmed both caps became `8`/`8` and the previously-blocked 6th connection then succeeded.
- Forbidden advantage: re-read the full scoring model after the fix, confirmed `is_premium` appears only in cap/threshold math (`dailyCap`, `active_cap`, `pending_cap`), never in `weightedScore` or the candidate sort, and `social_linked` remains completely unread by any scoring code, unchanged from before this session.
- All test data cleaned up afterward and independently re-verified: zero test connections remaining for Jordan Blake beyond one real, pre-existing connection from July 13 (correctly left untouched, it predates this session), throwaway user fully deleted, both accounts' `is_premium` reverted to `false`, Maria's test-day cache cleared.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new copy or code.

**Not built, deliberately out of scope:** the optional private Pass-feedback field (F11, listed separately in AGENTS.md, not part of this fix's assignment). The actual Premium/Upgrade purchase screen and any payment webhook, `is_premium` is a flag with correct downstream effects, not a purchase flow. A pre-existing gap surfaced but not fixed: nothing in this codebase transitions a connection to `status = 'active'`, worked around here by computing "active" from real message exchange directly (which is also literally what the given spec asked for), but the status column itself remains effectively dead for that value; worth a dedicated look if something else ever depends on it directly.

**Next:** per NEXT SESSION FOCUS, item 4 (Remember, basics) is the next and largest remaining gap. Given this is now the third fix in a row where Step 1 verification was the right first move (twice finding real undocumented work, once confirming a genuine gap), keep applying it before any further build session in this list.

### Session, July 21, 2026 (Fix #4: Saved profiles, removed a genuinely live 14-day expiry)

**Step 1, verification before building, per this session's own instruction and the by-now-established pattern.** This one didn't fit any of the first three fixes' patterns cleanly. AGENTS.md's F14 line already claimed the 14-day expiry was "CORRECTED... do NOT build a 14-day expiry," and PROGRESS.md's Known Issues row called it "unconfirmed." Neither was right: read `20260714000000_add_save_expiry.sql` directly and found a real, complete mechanism (a `saved_at` column, a `connections_set_saved_at` trigger, an `expire_old_saves()` function, and a daily `pg_cron` job), then confirmed live via `cron.job` that the job (`expire-old-saves`) was still `active = true`. `src/app/(tabs)/saved.tsx` matched: a hardcoded `SAVE_WINDOW_DAYS = 14`, a `daysLeft()` countdown, an "Expires today"/"N days left" badge on every card, and empty-state copy literally reading "they'll show up here for 14 days." This was not a stray doc claim, it was a deliberately built, fully shipped feature that directly contradicted the blueprint's own locked decision ("Do not pressure users with artificial expiration"). Also confirmed live: `saved_profiles` had no age/gender eligibility check at all (unlike `discovery_profiles`/`browse_profiles`, which Fix #1 already hard-filters), so a saved profile could sit there indefinitely even after becoming genuinely ineligible under the viewer's own current preferences. Report/Block (Fix #13) confirmed still fully unbuilt (no table, column, or RLS policy anywhere), so blocked-account handling is a flagged dependency, not something to fake ahead of that feature.

**Built (migration `20260721000001_remove_save_expiry_add_eligibility.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`):**
- `cron.unschedule('expire-old-saves')` and `drop function expire_old_saves()`, removing the time-based mechanism entirely, not just disabling it.
- `saved_profiles` rewritten to add the same mutual gender-preference and mutual age-range hard-eligibility checks `discovery_profiles`/`browse_profiles` already enforce, filtering ineligible saves out immediately at read time. Deliberately narrower than "compatibility drift": the view's own original design comment already decided that scored-compatibility drift (values, activities, interests) should not remove a save, that reasoning is preserved untouched, only the hard-eligibility layer (same tier as gender/age in the blueprint's own matching hierarchy) is new here.
- `prune_ineligible_saves()`, a new function replacing `expire_old_saves()` at the same daily 3am cron slot (renamed to `prune-ineligible-saves`), actually flipping `connections.saved` back to `false` for real accounting once a save becomes ineligible, rather than leaving the view as the only thing hiding it forever.
- Deleted accounts needed no new code: `profiles`/`connections` both already `references auth.users(id) on delete cascade`, confirmed live that deleting an account removes the connection (the save itself), not just the profile, before either the view or the prune function ever runs.
- `saved.tsx`: removed `SAVE_WINDOW_DAYS`, `daysLeft()`, and the countdown badge entirely; updated the empty-state copy to drop the now-false "for 14 days" claim (kept the rest of the sentence, this is a removal of a false claim, not new invented copy).

**Verified live, all four scenarios, using an isolated test save (Maria Santos saving Elena Torres, cleaned up after) plus one throwaway account for the deletion case (created and fully deleted the same way Fix #3's pending-cap test did):**
- No time-based expiry: backdated a real save's `saved_at` to 20 days ago (well past the old 14-day threshold), confirmed it still appeared in `saved_profiles`, then ran `prune_ineligible_saves()` directly and confirmed it survived that too (age of the save itself is no longer a factor anywhere).
- Age ineligibility: narrowed the viewer's own `max_friend_age` to exclude the saved person, confirmed the view immediately returned 0 rows, confirmed the underlying `saved` flag was still `true` until `prune_ineligible_saves()` ran, then confirmed it flipped to `false`. Restored and re-saved to confirm the eligible case still works.
- Gender-preference ineligibility: same two-layer confirmation (immediate view filter, then durable flag flip via the prune function), narrowing the viewer's `matching_preference` to exclude the saved person's gender.
- Deleted account: created a throwaway account, had Maria save it, confirmed `saved_profiles` returned it, deleted the throwaway account's `auth.users`/`auth.identities` rows, confirmed the save disappeared automatically with zero application code involved, purely the existing FK cascade.
- All test data and preference changes reverted afterward, independently re-verified (Maria's real `matching_preference`/`max_friend_age` restored, zero saved connections remaining for her, throwaway account fully gone).
- `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new code or copy.

**Not built, correctly flagged rather than faked:** blocked-account handling in `saved_profiles`/`prune_ineligible_saves()`, since Report/Block (Fix #13) doesn't exist yet. Both need a block-status clause added once that feature ships, noted directly in the migration's own comments so the dependency isn't lost.

**Next:** NEXT SESSION FOCUS item 4, Remember (People List, Timeline, Add Entry), the largest remaining gap, still fully unbuilt. Given this session found a THIRD distinct pattern (not "already built, docs stale" like Fix #1, not "different undocumented implementation" like Fix #2, not "genuinely unbuilt" like Fix #3, but "docs claim it was fixed, code shows it never was"), the standing advice holds: verify every remaining item directly before trusting any doc's characterization of it, including this file's own.

### Session, July 22, 2026 (bug fix: Fix #1's email verification never actually worked)

**A real gap in a previous "verified live" claim, reported by the user, not self-caught.** Fix #1 (2026-07-20) verified the account-creation schema, the age hard filter, and `tsc --noEmit`, and called it done. It never actually drove `email-verification.tsx` → `verify-email-code.tsx` end to end with a real code. That gap let a real bug ship: clicking the code-entry screen's "Verify" button never actually confirmed an email, and instead appeared to strand the user back at the first onboarding screen, losing their progress.

**Root cause, found by testing the actual auth calls directly rather than guessing from the symptom, per this session's own instruction.** `supabase.auth.verifyOtp()`'s email OTP length for this project is 8 digits, confirmed by generating four separate real codes via GoTrue's admin API, all exactly 8 digits ("08963603", "68148654", "25563972", "20013730"), while phone verification (`verify-code.tsx`) genuinely does use 6 digits, a real, different per-flow setting, not a copy-paste inconsistency. `verify-email-code.tsx`'s code input had `maxLength={6}` and truncated everything typed to 6 characters (`.slice(0, 6)`) before ever calling `verifyOtp`, and validated on an exact `code.length !== 6`. This made a successful manual confirmation mathematically impossible, every real attempt submitted a truncated, wrong code and failed with GoTrue's genuinely confusing "Token has expired or is invalid" message (confirmed live: a truncated 6-digit code fails this way; a full, untruncated 8-digit code is accepted, HTTP 200).

**Why it looked like "routing back to the first screen" rather than just a stuck error screen, reasoned through but not fully live-confirmed.** `verify-email-code.tsx`'s own error handling was already correct, a failed `verifyOtp` call shows an inline error and does not navigate anywhere (confirmed live: a wrong/garbage code returns a clean error, the user stays put). Since manual entry could never succeed, the most likely real path to an ACTUAL successful confirmation is Supabase's own default email template, which (per its standard behavior) includes a clickable confirmation link alongside any code, bypassing this app's own `handleVerify` (and the `public.users.email` upsert that only lives inside it) entirely. That link redirects wherever the project's Site URL/redirect_to points, most likely this app's own web root, and `src/app/index.tsx` is a pure, unconditional `<Redirect href="/philosophy-intro" />` with zero session-awareness, for any user in any state. This part is a reasoned, evidence-consistent explanation, not something driven through an actual email inbox (this project has no custom SMTP, a known pre-existing limitation, and the live investigation also hit Supabase's default rate limit on real email sends partway through). Flagged, not fixed: `index.tsx`'s total lack of session-aware routing is a separate, larger, pre-existing architectural gap (it would also strand a fully graduated, returning user at the philosophy intro screen again, unrelated to this specific bug), out of scope for this fix.

**Direct, concrete evidence this was really happening, not hypothetical.** The real signed-in test account's `auth.users` row showed `email_confirmed_at` set only moments before this session started, matching a real, very recent, successful confirmation, while `public.users.email` was still null, exactly the disconnect this bug produces: the auth layer genuinely confirms the email, but the app's own record of it never gets written, because the code path that writes it (`handleVerify`'s success branch) was never actually reached through the broken input field.

**Fixed:** `verify-email-code.tsx`'s input now accepts up to 10 digits (`maxLength={10}`, `.slice(0, 10)`) and validates on `code.length < 6` (a floor, not an exact count), so this can't silently truncate a valid code again if the project's OTP length setting changes in the future. Copy updated to drop the specific "6-digit" claim. No changes were needed to the error-handling path (already correct) or to `verify-code.tsx` (phone OTP genuinely is 6 digits, unaffected).

**Verified live, all against a fresh throwaway account (or the admin API directly), real seed/real accounts left untouched:**
- Confirmed the actual OTP length four separate times via GoTrue's admin `generate_link` API.
- Confirmed a 6-digit truncated code fails with "Token has expired or is invalid."
- Confirmed the full, untruncated code is accepted (`verifyOtp` returns HTTP 200).
- Confirmed the `users` upsert (the other half of `handleVerify`'s success path) works correctly in isolation when called with a valid session.
- Confirmed a genuinely wrong code still shows a clean inline error and does not reset or navigate anywhere.
- A real, separate red herring encountered and ruled out during investigation, documented here so a future session doesn't re-chase it: seed/dev-tooling accounts that have been signed in via `dev-create-session` accumulate a synthetic `@seed.dev.local` email identity as a side effect, and GoTrue's own validator rejects certain reserved-looking domains (`.local`, `example.com`) when revalidating a user's *existing* email during an update request, citing the current address as "invalid" even though the NEW address being requested is fine. This is a testing-environment artifact of this project's own dev session-minting mechanism, not a bug affecting real users (who start with no email at all, so there's nothing for that revalidation check to trip on).
- One live action was correctly blocked by the harness's own safety classifier: an attempt to sign in as the real user's own account to test the flow end to end. Respected the block, pivoted to a throwaway account instead, and did not attempt to route around it.
- All throwaway test accounts (phones `+15555599995/6/7`) fully deleted afterward, confirmed via direct count query. The real seed account touched only for read-only checks (Robert Kim, `+15555500104`) confirmed to have zero residual state from a failed test attempt. The real signed-in test account's own email (`kanawata@gmail.com`) was never modified.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean before and after. No em dashes in any new copy.

**The meta-lesson, since this is what the user specifically asked to be recorded:** Fix #1's "verified live" claim was not fabricated or careless in an obvious way, the schema and hard-filter checks it actually performed were real and correct. But "verified live" was applied to the FEATURE as a whole based on verifying only PART of it (the data layer), not the part that actually broke (a client input field's assumptions about server config). The standing advice from every fix in this file so far has been "verify before building, don't trust docs," but this session is a reminder that verification claims need the same scrutiny, including this project's own, going forward: a "verified live" note is only as trustworthy as the specific thing it actually exercised, not the feature name attached to it.

**Next:** Remember (People List, Timeline, Add Entry) remains the largest unbuilt gap per NEXT SESSION FOCUS. The `index.tsx` session-aware-routing gap flagged above is a real, separate, worth-a-dedicated-look item, not urgent enough to have blocked this fix but should be picked up deliberately rather than patched piecemeal inside an unrelated bug fix again.

### Session, July 25, 2026 (AI-assist philosophy audit, single-box + Undo rollout, Clean Up truncation fix, and documentation reconciliation)

This entry covers real, verified work found and done across this session that had no corresponding log entry, plus a closing documentation-reconciliation pass. Part A is pre-existing, undocumented work discovered already done when this session started (same recurring pattern this file has hit repeatedly, see the meta-lesson in the July 22 entry above). Part B is work actively built and live-verified this session. Part C is the reconciliation check itself.

**Part A, found already fixed and undocumented:**

**A1, auth/session root-cause fix (Site URL, `index.tsx`, email verification).** `index.tsx`'s own total lack of session-aware routing, flagged as a real, separate architectural gap at the end of the July 22 entry above, was found already fixed when this session checked it: it now checks for a real session and a `profiles.display_name` row before ever redirecting to `/philosophy-intro`, sending an already-past-onboarding user to `/home` or `/profile-basics` instead of restarting them from scratch. `verify-email-code.tsx` (the screen the July 22 fix patched) no longer exists on disk at all, confirmed by directly listing `src/app/*.tsx`. `email-verification.tsx` was rebuilt around a further root cause found beyond July 22's digit-count bug: this project's free Supabase tier uses the default mailer, which cannot embed `{{ .Token }}` in its email-change template, only `{{ .ConfirmationURL }}` (confirmed directly against the template API), meaning the code-entry screen was structurally unreachable through any real, legitimate path no matter what digit count it expected. The screen is now fully link-based, optional, and skippable, matching what "Skip" already implied. Confirmed live via this session's own reconciliation pass (Part C): the file's own header comments document this reasoning directly and match what's actually on disk.

**A2, sender reassurance line, two rounds of bugs.** The passive, client-computed reassurance line shown to whoever is waiting on a reply (20h and 72h checkpoints, no DB row, see F17) had two separate rendering bugs, both since fixed: the 20h copy was not rendering at all, and once that was fixed, the 72h checkpoint was found to be showing the 20h line's text instead of its own distinct copy. Both corrected; the two checkpoints now show their own separate lines as originally specified.

**A3, no-ghost prompt card (`ConversationFlowPromptCard`), four bugs found and fixed together.** All four were present in the same component and are documented directly in its own code comments ("AI philosophy fix," "Bug A/D fix," "Bug B fix"), found already fixed when this session's first task read the file:
- Drafted or cleaned-up text was not visible before Send, a real visibility bug, not just a design preference. Fixed with a real, visible `TextInput` that matches `UniversalTextBox`'s own contract (shown once there's content, editable, reviewed before Send).
- "Keep waiting" and "Close and make room for another" appeared visually disabled. Root cause was styling, not a real disabled state: plain-text-link styling with no border/pill made them indistinguishable from disabled controls. Restyled to match every other secondary action pill in the app.
- Receiver-side prompts (R2/R3) were missing a working reply textbox, same underlying cause as the first bug.
- Honest Exit's confirmation message ("Choosing honesty over silence...") was not rendering, because the thread screen's own realtime message handler unmounted the prompt card (clearing `noGhostPrompt`) the instant the honest-exit message landed, before the card's own local confirmation state ever got a chance to show. Fixed by lifting an `honestExitSent` flag up to the thread screen itself, so the confirmation survives the card unmounting underneath it.

**A4, forced-AI-draft philosophy violation.** Both "Draft it" (sender-side no-ghost flow) and the receiver-side "Reply" option were found to be auto-generating or auto-replacing AI text the instant an option was picked, with no real user input first, a direct violation of AGENTS.md's own anchor that the user's raw words come first and Claude only ever articulates what they already said. Fixed before this session's own further work built on top of it: the field starts blank, and generating anything requires the user to actually describe their situation first.

**Part B, actively built and live-verified this session:**

**B1, single-box + persistent Undo pattern, full rollout.** Replaced the old two-box "your words / AI version" side-by-side comparison with a single box everywhere: type or "Help me write," Clean Up replaces the box in place, Undo appears and stays available through further edits until Send. Applied across all 7 real usage sites of `UniversalTextBox`: `ReplyAssistPanel`, chat compose, no-ghost R2/R3, no-ghost S1/R1 (including renaming S1's action from "Draft it" to "Help Me Write" for consistency within that flow only, R1 unaffected), profile fields (About You, Dealbreakers), and `meetup-cancel`'s Step 3B. Two callers (`activity-suggestions-modal`, `meetup-cancel` Step 5) were confirmed not applicable, they never had a draft-from-empty entry point.

A real bug was found mid-rollout and fixed: `ReplyAssistPanel`'s "Write a reply" modal still showed two boxes at once (reported directly, with a screenshot, after the pattern was believed complete). Root cause: `UniversalTextBox` still rendered its own internal mini-textarea for the situation description, a leftover from the old design never fully removed in the earlier pass. The real, general fix was architectural: the user now types their situation directly into the caller's own already-visible field for every step, `UniversalTextBox` renders no field of its own at all anymore. This meant `conversation-flow-prompt-card.tsx`'s S1/R1 field also needed a one-off change, it had been conditionally hidden while empty, which would have left nowhere to type once the internal mini-field was removed; made unconditionally visible instead. Also corrected: Undo now applies to a fresh "Help me write" draft too, not just Clean Up, restoring the user's own typed situation description if they don't want the AI version, a real gap in the original design that this fix closed.

Verified live (Playwright, real seed accounts, real deployed backend), 4 surfaces, 31+ checks, including the exact reported surface: `ReplyAssistPanel` via F19's "Help me say it" (confirmed no second textarea appears before or after tapping Help me write, situation prompt shown as a caption not a field, draft lands in place, Undo appears and survives further edits, restores exact original text, Send works, "I'll reach out my own way" and "Dismiss" both still work), chat compose, no-ghost S1 (including confirming "Close and make room for another" and "Keep waiting" still work, unaffected), and profile-build's About You field.

**B2, Clean Up truncation bug, root-caused and fixed.** A long (500+ word) message run through Clean Up returned a single truncated word instead of a cleaned version, Undo correctly prevented data loss but Clean Up itself was badly broken for longer input. Reproduced live before fixing anything: called the deployed function directly with a 586-word input and inspected the raw Anthropic response, `stop_reason: "max_tokens"`, output capped at exactly the flat 600 tokens every caller shared, cut off mid-sentence. The model's "thinking" content block draws from that same budget at an unpredictable size (47 tokens in one call, 429 in an earlier one, both confirmed live), so on an unlucky run almost nothing is left for the real answer, which is exactly how a single stray word can come out. Fixed in `generate-reply-draft`: Clean Up's `max_tokens` now scales with input length plus a fixed thinking-token buffer instead of a flat number; any response that stops at `max_tokens` is now treated as a failure and returns a clear error instead of ever being shown as a complete result; and a defensive 12,000-character hard ceiling fails immediately with "Message too long to clean up. Try sending it as is or shortening it first." rather than spending an API call to find out. The specific server error message now surfaces to the user everywhere `generate-reply-draft` is called, instead of a flat generic message, via a `DraftServiceError` type so only known-safe backend copy is ever shown, not raw network/SDK error text.

Verified live across the full length range: the same 586-word input now returns a complete, coherent 572-word cleanup ending on the correct final sentence; a 134-word medium input and a short input both unaffected (no regression); a 15,290-character pathological input gets the clear "too long" error immediately; draft mode (unaffected by the cleanup-specific fix) still works normally. Also re-verified the full UI cycle on the same long input: types into the real compose box, Clean Up produces the full untruncated result in place, Undo restores the exact original 3057-character text.

Mic icon UI placeholders (non-functional, "coming soon" on tap) were also added to every free-text box touched by this session's audit work, a separate, smaller task folded into the same pass, not documented further here since it has no behavior beyond the placeholder itself.

**Part C, documentation reconciliation (this entry).** A separate check confirmed, via direct code, schema, and live-database queries, not by reading documentation, that: F17's no-ghost timing is correctly the blueprint's 0/20-or-36/72/120/125h/168h table, queried directly from the live deployed function, not the stale 48/72/96/120h table; F14's 14-day Saved expiry is genuinely removed, no cron job, no function, no date filter anywhere; F19 (this session's own Part B1 test subject) is genuinely built and working, correcting a separate list that had it marked as not built; and the min/max friend age hard filter is genuinely live. The same check also confirmed F8 (Guide library) and F7 (etiquette module content) are NOT built to spec, despite being easy to assume otherwise given how much else in this app is real and working, see the corrected notes in AGENTS.md and the Known Issues table above for the exact mismatches found. Full findings, including the five profile-field gaps re-verified as still open (availability, chat-first/meet-sooner, communication modes, photo-required-before-visible, radius pool warning) and confirming `communication_style_expression`/`openness` are genuinely different fields from communication mode preference, not a naming overlap, are folded into AGENTS.md's relevant sections directly rather than repeated here.

**Verified (Parts B1 and B2):** `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new code or copy (only the pre-existing, intentional em-dash-stripping instruction text and regex, unchanged). All temporary test scripts, response files, and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, never touched `package.json`/`package-lock.json`) were removed after each verification pass.

**Next:** Remember (People List, Timeline, Add Entry) remains the largest unbuilt gap. F8 (Guide library) now has a clear, scoped next step: restructure the existing `guides.tsx` placeholder into the real four-category structure rather than starting from nothing. F7 needs both new content and a format rebuild (one comprehension question, not a two-choice scenario). The five re-confirmed-open profile-field gaps (availability, chat-first/meet-sooner, communication modes, photo-required-before-visible, radius pool warning) and the still-unbuilt Public Profile Review screen remain exactly where they were, none addressed this session.

### Session, July 26, 2026 (backfill: onboarding overhaul, meetup milestone redesign, and a full reconciliation)

This entry covers two substantial batches of real, deployed work that happened outside a normally-logged session in this project's history, same recurring pattern this file has flagged repeatedly (see the July 20 and July 25 entries above), plus the reconciliation investigation that found and verified both of them line by line against live code, schema, and deployed functions rather than trusting any prior summary of what shipped. Part A is the onboarding overhaul. Part B is the meetup system replacement. Part C is the reconciliation itself, including one real discrepancy it found between an earlier summary of Part A's own AI-reflection work and what is actually live.

**Part A, onboarding overhaul, confirmed live 2026-07-26.**
- **F1 intro:** the old "It's Tuesday night..." copy, which assumed every user was lonely, isolated, divorced, or newly relocated, is gone from `philosophy-intro.tsx`. Replaced with "Meeting people is only the beginning," naming the uncertainty of new friendship directly without assuming a specific life circumstance. Same visual system, same Continue button, same dev skip link.
- **F3 regrouping:** `gender-identity.tsx` is now 3 steps instead of 4. Matching preference and the existing min/max friend age inputs now share one screen ("Who would you like to connect with?"), messaging preference stays its own separate final step. Same underlying values, selectors, validation, and persisted field names, only position changed, confirmed by re-reading the actual validation and submit logic, not just the screen titles.
- **F6 addition, "Your experience with new friendship":** a new private 5-question section (2 multi-select, 3 single-select) shown after the existing 10 reflection questions, onboarding-only, not shown on the Profile tab's retake flow. Stored in a new `profiles.friendship_experience` jsonb column, additive, nullable, confirmed via `information_schema` never exposed to `discovery_profiles`/`browse_profiles`/`compatible_candidates_for` or read by any matching code.
- **F6 AI reflection, rebuilt twice in immediate succession:** first to two tentative-language sections, then, same day, to three ("How you tend to connect," "What has helped before," "When things feel uncertain"), the second pass also adding server-side fetching of the caller's own `profiles` selections (via their own JWT, RLS-scoped) as a third grounding source alongside the 10 reflections and the 5 new answers. Every displayed sentence is now validated server-side against a per-section allowed-evidence category, not just checked for being real, a real answer id from the wrong section's group is rejected the same as an invented one, falling back to fixed neutral copy per section when there isn't enough of the right kind of evidence. A real truncation bug was found and fixed during this second pass: an empty evidence group (the edit-mode retake path, which never collects the 5 new answers) made Claude write a verbose "no answer was provided" explanation that blew through the token budget and corrupted the JSON, a 502 every time, reproduced 3 for 3 live before the fix. Fixed by telling the model those sections don't need to explain themselves (they get discarded and replaced by the fallback regardless of what it writes there) and raising `max_tokens` from 800 to 2000 with real headroom, the same class of fix this file's own July 25 entry already made once for `generate-reply-draft`'s Clean Up. Reverified 4 for 4 clean after.
- **F7 videos:** the two mandatory modules are now "Begin With Curiosity" and "How We Show Up" (`module_curiosity`/`module_show_up`), replacing "The rhythm of messaging"/"When friendship gets hard" in the mandatory onboarding slot. Those two were demoted to `mandatory: false` in `modules-data.ts`, not deleted, they keep their original content, id, and any existing user's completion history, and remain reachable through Guides. No real video was generated for either new slot, per explicit instruction, `VideoPlaceholder` now takes a `label` so each pending slot has a precise placeholder rather than a generic one.
- **F9/F10 merged into one confirmation:** `readiness-commitment.tsx` now carries both the original readiness question and the full behavioral-tracking-disclosure content on one screen, same wording for both preserved, "Yes, I'm ready" writes `readiness_status`, `paused_until`, and `behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at` in a single update instead of two screens each writing once. `behavioral-tracking-disclosure.tsx` deleted outright. "All set" (`modules-complete.tsx`) moved to after this single confirmation, now the last screen before `/home`.
- Existing users with `profiles.display_name` already set are unaffected by any of this: `index.tsx`'s resume gate reads that one field, not any of the screens or fields this overhaul touched, confirmed live that a genuinely completed seed account still lands directly on `/home`.

**Part B, meetup system replaced with a milestone/elapsed-time model, confirmed live 2026-07-26.** The prior date-based scheduling machinery (`meetups` table, `propose_meetup`/`confirm_meetup`/`decline_meetup`/`cancel_meetup`, a real `scheduled_at`) was found broken before this redesign started, `meetup_count` was defined but never written by any code path, `logged_at` was never set, nothing ever reached `status = 'completed'`, and no Graduation logic existed anywhere to consume any of it. It was removed, not patched: `meetups`, `day_of_checkins`, `cancellation_interventions`, and `calendar_optin_responses` are all confirmed gone from the live schema, `propose_meetup`/`confirm_meetup` confirmed gone from `pg_proc`, `expo-calendar` confirmed uninstalled, every associated client file confirmed deleted.
- Replaced by: a first-time private feeling check (`first_meetup_feelings`, Excited/Neutral/Nervous, with a skippable Guide nudge) the first time "Let's plan something" is ever engaged with on a connection; an hourly cron (`meetup-checkin-check`) that fires a `meetup_checkins` row for both participants once roughly a week has passed with no further planning activity, asking how it went; and mutual-confirmation reconciliation, re-verified directly against the live `resolve_meetup_checkin` function body this session, `connections.meetup_count` only increments in the exact branch where both sides' resolved outcome is `went_well`, any other combination leaves it untouched, and neither "didn't happen" nor silence has any consequence.
- Confirmed genuinely wired end to end, not just present as disconnected pieces: `handlePlanSomething` in `thread/[id].tsx` (bound to both the manual button and the automatic 7-10 message banner) calls `record_plan_activity` and then opens the real, existing 3-category `ActivitySuggestionsModal`, traced directly through the handler body, not assumed from the components existing separately.
- A real, working Guide entry point was added this same pass: the Profile tab now links to `/guides`, confirmed live end to end, landing on the existing (still flat, unstructured) Guides screen, the full F8 restructure remains a separate, larger, still-open gap.
- **F21/F24/F25 (numbering per the July 17 session that originally built them; F22/F24/F25 per this file's own numbering) are not "not yet redesigned," they are fully removed with no replacement of any kind**, confirmed live, zero references anywhere beyond the historical migrations that created and then dropped them. The new first-time milestone modal is not a substitute for the old pre-meetup curiosity prompt, it is a private self-feeling check about the user's own state, not the original "What are you genuinely curious to understand about [Name] today?" question about the other person. This is a real, currently open product decision (drop from MVP scope entirely, or redesign around the milestone model), not resolved by this session, not decided here.
- F23 (activity suggestions) is unaffected by any of this, reached through the new flow instead of the old one. Its own deliberately-deferred latency tradeoff is unchanged, reconfirmed live: `SET_COUNT = 3` and the `activity_suggestion_pool` reserve table are both still in place, so roughly 1 in 3 taps still costs a real 7-30 second Claude call. Ticketmaster/Eventbrite still have no key configured (reconfirmed via `supabase secrets list`), SeatGeek and Google Events are still fully uncoded.

**Part C, reconciliation findings.** Every item in AGENTS.md's "Missing from original F-numbering" list (Remember, Report & Block, Premium/Upgrade, Referral, Settings, Terms/Privacy acceptance, fraud controls, analytics) was individually re-checked live this session, not assumed from the list's own prior wording, all confirmed still fully absent, no partial progress on any of them. The five profile-field gaps (availability, chat-first/meet-sooner, communication modes, photo-required-before-visible, radius pool warning) were also each re-checked individually and are all still absent. The F21/F22 numbering conflict this file has carried since July 17 is now moot rather than resolved, pre-meetup curiosity (one side of the original disputed pair) no longer exists in any form, so there is no longer a competing claim on either number.

**One real discrepancy found and reported directly, not smoothed over:** the task that requested this reconciliation described Part A's AI reflection as a "revised two-section AI reflection," matching the first of the two rebuilds described above. The actual live code has three sections, from the second, same-day rebuild that added profile-selections grounding. Both rebuilds really happened, in immediate succession, undocumented until now, the three-section version is what is actually live and is what AGENTS.md's F6 entry now describes. Flagging this here so a future summary describing "the two-section reflection" is understood to be describing history, not current behavior.

**Verified:** this reconciliation session itself was documentation-only, no application code was changed here. Every claim in Parts A, B, and C above was checked directly this session via `grep`/`Read` against the actual source files, and `information_schema`/`pg_proc`/`pg_get_functiondef`/`supabase secrets list` queries against the live database and deployed functions, not inferred from AGENTS.md's own prior text or any other summary. The live Playwright click-throughs referenced within Parts A and B (the full onboarding order including the three-section reflection rendering with real grounded content, the meetup milestone flow end to end including mutual confirmation, the Guide link) were run during the earlier, undocumented build sessions this entry backfills, not re-run today, this session's own contribution was confirming what those builds actually left behind in the live code and schema, catching the one real discrepancy noted above in the process.

**Next:** the F21/F24/F25 drop-vs-redesign decision from Part B is the most concrete open question this session surfaces, unlike most items on the missing-features list it isn't a simple "still not built," it's a real removal that needs a real decision before anything gets built in that space again. Remember remains the largest single unbuilt gap. F8's full four-category restructure and F7's format rebuild (one comprehension question, not a two-choice scenario) both remain open, unaffected by either of the two title changes this session found. This entry does not itself change any application code, per its own explicit scope, both Parts A and B describe work already live before this session started.

### Session, July 26, 2026 (Report & Block, safety-critical)

Built Report & Block from genuinely nothing, confirmed first rather than assumed: grepped the whole codebase and queried the live schema for any `reports`/`blocks`/`blocked_users` table, RPC, or UI, zero matches beyond unrelated false positives (Postgres's own `pg_stat_get_blocks_*` functions, the meetup system's `other_reported_outcome` field). Chat and Other User Profile had no Report or Block affordance either, matching the standing note this session re-verified rather than trusted.

**Built (migration `20260729000000`):**
- `reports` (8 categories: fake_identity, harassment, romantic_sexual_misuse, hate, scam, unsafe_meetup, impersonation, other) and `blocks` (unilateral, one row per direction), both no-client-write, same convention as `no_ghost_prompts`/`meetup_checkins`. `file_report()` and `block_user()` are the only writers, `file_report` can optionally also block in the same call.
- `connections.status` gained a `blocked` value alongside pending/active/passed/inactive/paused.
- `discovery_profiles`, `browse_profiles`, `saved_profiles`, and `compatible_candidates_for` (the service-role batch match-refresh path) all gained a bidirectional block exclusion at the same tier as the existing gender/pause filters. `prune_ineligible_saves` gained the same clause, resolving the exact dependency F14's own entry had flagged ("both need a block-status clause added once Fix #13 ships").
- `my_connection_capacity` and `create_connection_with_capacity_check` both exclude `blocked` from active/pending counts, so blocking frees a slot.
- The messages table's INSERT RLS policy now rejects a blocked connection, enforced at the database layer, confirmed live via a direct insert attempt that failed with a real RLS violation, not just a hidden compose box client-side.
- New client lib (`src/lib/report-block.ts`), `ReportModal` (8 categories, optional plain-text detail field deliberately NOT wired to this app's AI-drafting pattern, see the file's own comment for why, real emergency guidance shown for unsafe_meetup: call 911 for immediate danger, plus the National Sexual Assault Hotline and National Domestic Violence Hotline), and `BlockConfirmModal` (deliberately lighter than Honest Exit's own multi-step flow, one screen, one confirmation, no reason required).
- Wired into `thread/[id].tsx`'s own header (always visible regardless of connection state), `candidate/[id].tsx`'s header, both no-ghost prompt card shapes (`ConversationFlowPromptCard`, R2/R3 and S1/R1 both got a Block pill, independent of their own escalation choices), and `inbox.tsx` (a new "Blocked" section, connections stay visible, not deleted).

**A real bug caught and fixed while building this, not part of the original ask:** `create_connection_with_capacity_check`'s existing-connection-reuse branch would have silently reset a blocked connection's status back to `pending` the next time either party's client called it, for example the blocked person revisiting the blocker's profile and tapping Say hello. Now raises `blocked` instead of reactivating.

**Verified live, not assumed:**
- Filed two real reports against a real seed account (David Chen reporting Marcus Alvarez) across two different categories (harassment, with a real detail string; unsafe_meetup, with the also-block default left on), confirmed both rows in `reports` with the correct category, detail, connection_id, and `also_blocked` flag.
- Confirmed the unsafe_meetup category shows real emergency guidance (911, both hotline numbers) directly in the report flow, not a silent log.
- Confirmed bidirectional block exclusion with a genuinely gender-compatible pair (Maria Santos/Aisha Bello, not David/Marcus, to isolate the new block filter from the pre-existing gender filter): both `discovery_profiles` and `browse_profiles` correctly excluded each from the other's results in both directions, reverted immediately after and reconfirmed visibility restored.
- Confirmed messaging genuinely stops at the database layer: a direct insert attempt as the blocked party failed with a real Postgres RLS violation (42501).
- Confirmed message history stays fully visible to the blocker (a real SELECT as the blocker returned both original messages), and the thread UI shows "This connection is blocked" with compose replaced, not just hidden.
- Confirmed blocking frees active-conversation capacity: a real connection's contribution to `my_connection_capacity()`'s active count dropped from 1 to 0 immediately after blocking.
- Confirmed Block is reachable directly from an active no-ghost prompt card (force-fired a real R2 prompt via the Dev tab against a fresh isolated connection, tapped Block from within the card itself, confirmed the connection flipped to `blocked`). The confirm dialog's own display name fell back to "them" rather than the real name in this specific test, the same pre-existing gender-incompatible-test-pair `display_name` gap this project's own testing has hit repeatedly for unrelated reasons (David/Marcus and this session's Robert Kim pairing are not mutually visible in `discovery_profiles`, which is where every other component's `otherName` already comes from), not a new bug in this work.
- All test connections, reports, and blocks created for verification were deleted afterward, confirmed zero remaining.

**Not decided here, flagged rather than assumed:** report data retention. Reports are stored indefinitely by default, the same standing this app already gives `no_ghost_prompts` and other behavioral records. Whether safety reports need a real retention policy (a minimum hold period, a maximum, redaction rules) is a genuine legal question this migration does not answer.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new file (checked directly, caught and fixed one in a first draft of AGENTS.md's own new copy before finalizing). All temporary test scripts and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, confirmed never touched `package.json`/`package-lock.json`) were removed after verification.

**Next:** an unblock UI was deliberately not built, out of scope for "build Report & Block," `block_user`'s effects are all real and correct but there is no way for a user to reverse a block from within the app yet, this belongs with the still-unbuilt Settings screen (blocked-users management) per AGENTS.md's own missing-features list. A "view my reports" screen was also not built, same reasoning. Report/Block data does not yet factor into F30's accountability check-in system or any fraud-control logic, both remain separately unbuilt.

### Session, July 26, 2026 (four confirmed profile gaps: availability, communication modes, photo-required-before-visible gate, radius pool warning)

**Step 1, verification before building, per this project's established discipline.** Checked all four items directly against live schema/code before touching anything: availability and communication_modes genuinely did not exist as columns anywhere (confirmed via information_schema). computeCompletionPct in profile-build.tsx was found to already check Boolean(p.photoUrl), a correction to an earlier session's claim that it didn't, but discovery_profiles/browse_profiles' WHERE clauses genuinely had no photo condition (confirmed via pg_get_viewdef), so the real, safety-relevant gap held. browse.tsx/home.tsx genuinely had no pool-size warning of any kind. The Public Profile Review screen, checked while in this area per explicit request, has zero matches anywhere in src/ for any of its likely names, confirmed definitively absent, not just unconfirmed.

**Built (migration 20260730000000_add_availability_commmodes_photogate.sql, applied via supabase db query --linked --file):**
- profiles.availability (text[]) and profiles.communication_modes (text[]) added. Two new option lists in filter-options.ts, AVAILABILITY (5 options) and COMMUNICATION_MODES (4 options), no DB-level value constraint, matching the existing convention for other uncapped multi-select fields like hangout_type_preference. Both wired into profile-build.tsx (new sections, toggle handlers, load/persist, computeCompletionPct as soft factors only, deliberately not added to getMissingRequiredFields), the Profile tab (self-view), and candidate/[id].tsx (Other User Profile).
- discovery_profiles and browse_profiles both recreated with a photo_url IS NOT NULL condition added to the WHERE clause (the actual safety fix) and the two new columns added to the SELECT list. Real Postgres limitation hit and fixed: the first attempt placed the new columns before the existing computed distance_miles column and failed, since CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW only allows appending trailing columns, not inserting them mid-list. Fixed by moving both new columns to after distance_miles in both view definitions.
- Real regression risk caught and fixed before shipping, not after: all 9 real seed accounts had photo_url = null. Shipping the photo gate as-is would have made every seed account invisible to every other one instantly, the same class of mistake as a documented prior incident (the July 16, 2026 location/radius seeding session). Backfilled distinct pravatar.cc placeholder photos for all 9 accounts in the same migration, verified via SQL that Maria/Aisha/Elena's long-standing verified pairing survived.
- browse.tsx gained a MIN_ELIGIBLE_POOL constant (3) and a warning card shown whenever the candidate count is greater than zero and below that threshold, with two message variants, an actionable "widen your radius" link when the session slider has room to grow within the account's own saved ceiling, versus a pointer to Profile settings when already at that ceiling. Deliberately not ported to home.tsx/For You, whose low counts reflect the daily AI-suggestion cap (2 free / 5 premium) working as designed, not a radius problem, documented inline so a future session doesn't copy this to the wrong screen.

**Verified live, three of four items fully, the fourth partially, honestly reported rather than assumed:**
- Items 1 and 2 (availability, communication modes): full Playwright round trip. Signed in as Jordan Blake, confirmed both sections render on profile-build.tsx, selected real options, confirmed they persisted and rendered correctly on Jordan's own Profile tab, then signed in as Sam Rivera (confirmed gender-compatible with Jordan) and confirmed both fields render correctly on Jordan's Other User Profile.
- Item 3 (photo gate), the safety-critical one: full Playwright round trip. Nulled Aisha Bello's photo, signed in as Maria Santos (her real, confirmed-compatible match), confirmed Aisha disappeared from Browse and that direct URL navigation to her candidate profile showed "isn't available" rather than her real profile, restored her photo, confirmed she reappeared in Browse. This is the strongest verification of the four, matching the safety weight of the item.
- Item 4 (radius pool warning): verified at the data and logic layers, not the browser layer. Direct SQL/RLS impersonation confirmed Maria Santos's real, full-radius compatible pool is naturally 2 people (Aisha Bello, Elena Torres, both 0 miles), below the 3-person threshold. Created one throwaway compatible test account (same sanctioned direct-SQL pattern this project has used repeatedly, for example Fix #3's pending-cap test), confirmed the pool crossed to exactly 3 (the no-warning boundary), then deleted it via cascade and confirmed the pool reverted cleanly to 2 (the warning boundary). Separately traced browse.tsx's actual render condition and the candidates state it reads directly in source, confirming the logic itself is a simple, correct boundary check consistent with this data. What could not be completed: the actual browser click-through. Partway through this session, dev-create-session (the mechanism behind every prior live-UI verification in this project) started failing consistently with an AuthRetryableFetchError from GoTrue's admin API. Root-caused as far as this environment allows: temporarily added debug output to the deployed function, confirmed the raw error, confirmed it was not caused by anything in this session's own code (the function's logic is unchanged from before), and noticed all reserved SUPABASE_* project secrets had been rotated by the platform at the exact same timestamp, strongly suggesting an external, platform-side event. Polled for roughly three minutes plus one manual recheck afterward, no recovery. Reverted the debug change and redeployed the clean function so no debug scaffolding was left behind. Flagging this plainly rather than either faking a pass or blocking the whole session on an external outage: the server-side behavior this warning depends on is proven correct, the client-side render logic is proven correct by direct reading, only the literal on-screen render step is unverified, and that gap is due to environment, not code.

**Verified (all four items):** tsc --noEmit clean across the whole app, checked both after individual edits and once more in this final state. No em dashes in any new code or copy, grepped every touched file directly. All temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (--no-save) were removed after verification, confirmed package.json/package-lock.json show no trace of it. The one throwaway test account created for Item 4's boundary test was fully deleted via cascade, confirmed by re-querying Maria's pool afterward.

**Next:** a genuine from-scratch browser click-through of Item 4's warning banner is still owed, the next session should re-attempt it once dev-create-session is confirmed working again, a quick single test call is enough to check before assuming it's still broken. The Public Profile Review screen remains fully unbuilt, now definitively confirmed rather than merely unconfirmed. Chat-first/meet-sooner preference and the friendship_type option-list reconciliation remain the only two still-open items from the original profile-field gap list this and the July 25 session have been working through.

### Session, July 27, 2026 (Remember: People List, Friendship Timeline, Add Entry, the largest remaining MVP gap)

**Step 1, verification before building.** `dev-create-session` had recovered from the prior session's platform outage (confirmed live via a direct curl call, real 200 response) before this session started. Grepped the full `src/` tree, all migrations, and `information_schema` directly for any `remember`/`care_notes`/`friendship_notes` table, route, or component: zero real matches (the handful of grep hits for the plain word "remember" were incidental copy, e.g. "keeps a group connected and remembers what matters," not a feature). `care_notes`, listed in AGENTS.md's own SUPABASE SCHEMA section as an existing table (F27), does not actually exist in the live schema either, a separate, smaller doc-vs-reality gap noticed in passing and not otherwise touched this session (F27 remains a distinct, unbuilt feature, out of scope here). Confirmed the tab bar (`(tabs)/_layout.tsx`) genuinely had no Remember tab, five real tabs plus dev.

**Step 2, reconciled against the milestone/elapsed-time meetup system (2026-07-28 redesign), not the original date-based spec**, per this session's own explicit instruction:
- "Timeline by meetup number" uses `connections.meetup_count`, the real, mutually-confirmed count that only increments when both participants resolve a `meetup_checkins` row as `went_well`, not a raw date. A new server-side trigger (`set_remember_entry_meetup_number`) stamps each entry with that count at insert time, so the ordering can't drift or be spoofed from a stale client read.
- "Pre-meetup reminder from approved notes" fires at the next real planning moment instead of a day-before notification, since there's no `scheduled_at` anywhere anymore to anchor one to: `fetchLatestRememberNote` is checked inside `handlePlanSomething` (`thread/[id].tsx`) whenever "Let's plan something" is re-engaged (not the connection's first-ever engagement, which still shows the existing `FirstMeetupMilestoneModal` unchanged). A new `RememberReminderCard` modal surfaces it before the activity suggestions modal opens.
- Voice-to-text is a placeholder only (`MicPlaceholderButton`, the same zero-wiring component used elsewhere in this app), explicitly out of scope per instruction, flagged as a known future dependency, not attempted.

**Built (migration `20260731000000_add_remember.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`):**
- `remember_entries` (`raw_text`, `organized_text`, `follow_up_note`, `meetup_number_at_entry`, `connection_id`, `user_id`). Private, own-row RLS only (`auth.uid() = user_id`), no update policy (entries are edited client-side before the one-time approved save, never after), insert also checks the caller is a real participant in `connection_id`. Same "personal record, no cross-user business rule" reasoning that made `rhythm_mismatch_dismissals`/`calendar_optin_responses` plain client-writable tables rather than RPC-mediated ones.
- `remember_people`, the People List's read source. Deliberately built as a plain view following `inbox_conversations`' own precedent exactly (not a security definer function): Postgres's default pre-`security_invoker` view semantics mean it executes as the view owner, which is how it can read the OTHER participant's `display_name`/`photo_url` despite `profiles`' own SELECT RLS being strictly `auth.uid() = user_id`. Deliberately NOT routed through `discovery_profiles`/`browse_profiles` (both hard-filter on CURRENT gender/age compatibility), since Remember is a historical record of people already met, a preference drifting after the fact should not make someone disappear from a feature whose whole purpose is remembering them, unlike Discovery/Browse where that filter is exactly correct.
- New Edge Function `organize-remember-entry`: same input-length ceiling and truncation-as-failure pattern `generate-reply-draft`'s Clean Up mode already established (2026-07-26), returns a short summary plus an optional follow-up line via a simple `SUMMARY:`/`FOLLOW_UP:` delimited format (not JSON, avoiding parse fragility on freeform prose), explicitly instructed to write `NONE` for the follow-up when nothing in the raw text warrants one rather than inventing one.
- `src/lib/remember.ts`: `fetchRememberPeople`, `fetchTimelineEntries`, `organizeRememberEntry`, `saveRememberEntry`, `deleteRememberEntry`, `deleteAllEntriesForConnection`, `fetchLatestRememberNote`, `buildRememberExport`.
- `RememberEntryComposer` (Add Entry, modal): raw text always typed first, a few optional prompt suggestions shown as plain non-forcing captions ("Not sure what to write?"), then "Organize with AI" and "Save as written" offered as an equal, un-nudged choice, a deliberate reading of the spec's "AI organizes only after submission" as describing sequencing, not making AI mandatory, matching this app's own repeated "the user decides and acts" anchor rather than forcing every entry through Claude. Reviewing the AI path shows the original raw text plus editable summary and follow-up fields, nothing is saved until an explicit "Approve and save."
- `RememberReminderCard` (modal), `src/app/(tabs)/remember.tsx` (People List, new Remember tab between Inbox and Profile, plus a real Export action), `src/app/remember/[connectionId].tsx` (Friendship Timeline: both raw and organized text shown per entry, per-entry delete, a two-tap "Delete all history with this person" confirm).
- `meetup-outcome-card.tsx`'s "keep a note of how it went" aside, previously deliberately not linking anywhere because the comment noted "Remember...is confirmed not built anywhere in this app yet...a real link would be dead," now links to the real Timeline screen.
- Export is a real function, not a UI promise: `buildRememberExport` returns JSON grouping every entry by person, the People List screen triggers a genuine file download on web (`Blob` plus a synthetic anchor click) or the native `Share` sheet elsewhere, no new dependency needed for either path.

**Verified live, 26 checks across three Playwright passes plus a dedicated export test, all against a real isolated test connection (Jordan Blake and Sam Rivera, a genuinely gender-compatible pair confirmed in an earlier session, created via direct SQL with `meetup_count = 2` and cleaned up via cascade afterward) and one real pre-existing connection (Maria Santos and Elena Torres, `meetup_count = 0`, used read-only plus one entry added and deleted, the connection itself untouched):**
- People List correctly includes a connection with `meetup_count > 0` (Sam Rivera showed for Jordan with "2 meetups") and correctly excludes a `meetup_count = 0` connection until a manual entry exists, then correctly includes it, then correctly excludes it again once that entry is deleted (Elena Torres for Maria, all three states confirmed).
- Add Entry, AI path: raw text was never auto-organized before the user explicitly requested it, the review step showed the original raw text alongside an editable, genuinely grounded AI summary and follow-up ("Had coffee with Sam for two hours. She just started a new marketing job and seemed nervous about it." / "Ask Sam how her first week at the new job went.", generated from a realistic raw entry, not fabricated), and the entry only appeared in the Timeline after an explicit "Approve and save" tap, never automatically.
- Add Entry, raw-only path: "Save as written" correctly skipped AI entirely and saved only the raw text, labeled "Before your first meetup" for the `meetup_count = 0` connection.
- Timeline ordering: the AI-organized entry correctly grouped under "After meetup 2" on the `meetup_count = 2` connection, confirming `meetup_number_at_entry` was correctly stamped from the real connection state, not a client value.
- Pre-meetup reminder: the connection's first-ever "Let's plan something" tap correctly showed the existing first-time milestone modal (unaffected by this feature), a second engagement after an entry existed correctly showed the new Remember reminder card instead, surfacing the real note content, before proceeding into activity suggestions.
- Privacy: signed in as Sam (the other participant), confirmed zero visibility into Jordan's private note anywhere, including the Timeline screen for that exact same connection, Sam's own row for it was genuinely empty, not just hidden client-side.
- Deletion: single-entry delete removed it from both the Timeline and, where applicable, the People List criteria. Full-history delete required two taps (first tap shows a "tap again to confirm" state without deleting, confirmed the entry was still present at that point), the second tap deleted everything.

No em dashes in any new file (the two matches in the new Edge Function are the same pre-existing strip-regex pattern already used in `generate-reply-draft`, not new copy). `organize-remember-entry` deployed.
