### Session, July 27, 2026 (bug fix: Meetup milestone testing silently failed in the Dev tab)

**Reported by the user, exact steps given, confirmed correct before touching anything.** Backdate `last_plan_activity_at` (default 170 hours), tap Run checkin evaluator now, check Chat as both participants: nothing appeared, no card, no error, no visible change of any kind.

**Reproduced live first, per this project's own standing discipline, before assuming anything about the cause.** Picked a real seed connection with real messages (Maria Santos and Elena Torres) and ran the exact same two RPCs directly: `dev_set_last_plan_activity` genuinely wrote the backdated timestamp (confirmed via direct query, elapsed time correctly showed just over 7 days), but `dev_run_meetup_checkin_check` returned success with zero rows inserted into `meetup_checkins`. Root cause found by reading `run_meetup_checkin_check`'s own body: it correctly skips any connection whose status is `paused`/`inactive`/`passed` (the same deliberate guard `run_no_ghost_check` already uses, so a paused connection's timer genuinely stops), but the function returned `void`, so both `dev_run_meetup_checkin_check` and the Dev tab's own status message claimed the evaluator "ran" with no indication that this guard had silently blocked it.

**Confirmed this was not a one-off bad thread pick, a real, currently 100% reproducible gap.** Queried every seed connection with real messages (the only ones the Dev tab's picker can show at all, since it reads `inbox_conversations`, which requires at least one message): all four are either `inactive` or `blocked`, none `active` or `pending`. This means the exact reported steps would fail identically for literally any thread a tester could currently select, not bad luck. The underlying schema, evaluator threshold logic (168 hours), and Chat-screen wiring (`get_meetup_checkin_status`, `MeetupCheckinCard`, `MeetupOutcomeCard`) were all re-checked directly and are correct, ruling out "never fully wired into the UI" as the cause, this was purely a dev-tooling visibility bug sitting on top of correct product logic, and a real gap in an earlier session's "confirmed live end to end" claim for this feature (see the corrected line in AGENTS.md's SCHEDULING section).

**Fixed (migration `20260801000000_fix_meetup_checkin_silent_dev_failure.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`):**
- `run_meetup_checkin_check` now returns a real outcome (`fired` / `already_fired_this_cycle` / `not_enough_time_elapsed` / `connection_not_eligible` / `no_activity_recorded`) instead of `void`. Had to drop and recreate rather than `CREATE OR REPLACE`, Postgres doesn't allow a return-type change in place. `run_meetup_checkin_check_all` (the real hourly cron path) still calls it via `perform`, unaffected either way, its own return value was never used.
- `dev_run_meetup_checkin_check` relays that same outcome back to the client.
- New `dev_reactivate_connection_for_testing`: sets a stuck connection's status back to `active` so it becomes testable again, refuses a `blocked` connection with a clear error (Block is a real safety feature from Report & Block, a dev convenience button should not be able to quietly undo it, pick a different thread instead).
- `dev.tsx`: each thread in the No-ghost/Meetup milestone picker now shows its connection status inline (e.g. "Elena Torres (inactive)"), so the precondition is visible before wasting time on backdate/run. `handleRunCheckinEvaluator` now shows an accurate, specific message per real outcome instead of one generic "ran" message regardless of what happened. New "Reactivate this connection" button wired to the new RPC.

**Verified live, twice, the second pass clean end to end (the first live UI pass had two of its own test-script locator bugs, both diagnosed and fixed before trusting any result, not papered over):**
- Reproduced the exact original bug live via the UI first: selected the real Elena Torres thread (correctly labeled "(inactive)" in the picker), backdated, ran the evaluator, confirmed the Dev tab now explains specifically why nothing fired ("this connection's status is paused, inactive, or passed...") instead of the old misleading generic success message.
- Applied the fix path live: tapped Reactivate, confirmed the thread's own label flipped to "(active)", ran the evaluator again, confirmed it returned `fired`.
- Full two-participant loop, live, both sides: signed in as Maria, confirmed the real checkin card rendered on her Chat screen with the correct prompt ("It's been a bit since you two talked about meeting up. How did it go?"), picked "Yes, it went well." Signed in as Elena, confirmed her card showed the correct confirm-or-correct framing naming Maria by her real display name ("Maria Santos mentioned you two met up. Did that happen for you too?"), picked "Yes, it went well" too. Confirmed via direct query that `connections.meetup_count` genuinely incremented from 0 to 1, the strongest possible proof the full mutual-confirmation loop actually completed, not just that two buttons were clicked.
- Two real test-script bugs were found and fixed during this verification, both flagged rather than quietly worked around: a loose text-match locator for "Elena Torres" matched the conversation-list row instead of the account-switcher button once a conversation list existed, breaking a mid-test account switch; and a hardcoded "Maria" in one assertion didn't match the real rendered "Maria Santos" (the other participant's actual full display name). Neither was a product bug, both are documented here so a future session doesn't mistake either for one if this test is ever rerun from these notes.
- The real, pre-existing Maria/Elena connection used throughout was restored to its exact original state afterward (`status = 'inactive'`, original `last_plan_activity_at`, `meetup_count = 0`, no leftover `meetup_checkins` rows), confirmed via direct query, not just assumed.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. No em dashes in any new code (checked directly). Temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed after verification, confirmed `package.json`/`package-lock.json` show no trace of it.

**Next:** the underlying data problem this bug surfaced, namely that this project's own accumulated testing history has left every real seed connection with messages in an `inactive` or `blocked` state, is now mitigated by the new Reactivate button but not really "fixed," a future session doing broader manual testing across features may want at least one seed connection deliberately kept in a healthy `active`/`pending` state going forward. Nothing else in this session's fix touches Graduation, F30, or any other feature that reads `meetup_count`, all of that remains exactly as previously documented.

**Follow-up, same day: Remember tab re-verified through the exact real Dev-tab-tool path, not just a direct backend check.** The user separately reported Remember's People List showing nothing for a connection they had tried to mark as "met" using this same Dev tab tool, before the fix above. Remember's own build session had verified People List's meetup-count criterion by setting `connections.meetup_count` directly via SQL, never by actually driving a real check-in through the Dev tab UI, a real gap in that earlier verification's coverage, not a false claim, but not the same thing as testing the real path a user would take either. The real, pre-existing Maria/Elena connection was again restored to its exact original state afterward (status, `last_plan_activity_at`, `meetup_count`, and confirmed Remember's People List is empty again for both, matching the connection's restored `meetup_count = 0` and zero remaining entries).

### Session, July 27, 2026 (bug fix: MeetupOutcomeCard's not_good branch — "Not now" didn't dismiss, no path into Remember)

**Reported by the user, with the exact reproduction: two participants ran a real checkin cycle, one picked "Yes, it went well," the other picked "Yes, but it was rough," landing both on the `not_good` outcome card (Pause / End the connection / Not now). Neither showed up in Remember, and "Not now" appeared to do nothing.**

**Investigated before fixing (separate turn, findings only):**
- Traced every outcome `resolve_meetup_checkin` handles (`went_well` / `rough` / `didnt_happen` / `still_figuring`, the only four values the column and the function both accept) and confirmed `connections.meetup_count` has exactly one increment site in the whole codebase (`resolve_meetup_checkin`, migration `20260728000002`, line 354), gated on both sides resolving `went_well` specifically. The reported `went_well` + `rough` combo correctly lands on `branch = 'not_good'` with the counter untouched, this part was never a bug, it's the mutual-confirmation rule working exactly as designed and already documented in AGENTS.md's SCHEDULING section.
- Found the real bug in `MeetupOutcomeCard`'s not_good branch (`src/components/meetup-outcome-card.tsx`): "Not now" was wired to the same `onResolved` prop as everything else, which just re-ran `get_meetup_checkin_status` and re-read `branch: 'not_good'` from the database, unchanged, because "Not now" itself never wrote anything. The card had no way to hide itself and would re-render identically on every load, the user's "does nothing" report was accurate.
- Confirmed no documented product decision exists anywhere in AGENTS.md or PROGRESS.md on whether a mutually-confirmed-but-rough meetup should count toward Remember, nothing was inferred, the fix below only adds a path to manually log an entry, it does not change what counts as a confirmed milestone.

**Fixed, two changes, no schema or RPC changes, `meetup_count`'s increment logic untouched:**
- `MeetupOutcomeCard` gained a new `onDismiss: () => void` prop, separate from `onResolved` (which still backs Pause, a real state change worth re-fetching for). "Not now" now calls `onDismiss` instead of `onResolved`.
- `thread/[id].tsx` implements `onDismiss` as a local, session-scoped dismiss, not a server write: a new `dismissedCheckinId` state, set to the current `checkinStatus.checkin_id` when "Not now" is tapped. The render condition became `checkinStatus?.branch && checkinStatus.checkin_id !== dismissedCheckinId && ...`. Keyed by `checkin_id` rather than a plain boolean so it self-clears the moment the next real checkin cycle fires a new row (a fresh `checkin_id` never equals the dismissed one), and also clears on a genuine fresh mount of the thread screen, matching the two "simplest, consistent with precedent" options the instruction offered, this satisfies both at once rather than picking one arbitrarily. Modeled on the existing `honestExitSent` local-state-lifted-to-the-screen pattern already used one card away in the same file, not a new pattern.
- Added a "Keep a note about what happened" link to the not_good branch, mirroring the good branch's existing "Keep a note of how it went" link (same destination, `router.push` to `/remember/[connectionId]`), reworded slightly so it doesn't presume a positive outcome. This only adds a path to Remember's Add Entry flow, `meetup_count` and the People List's `meetup_count > 0` criterion are both unchanged, a rough-outcome connection only appears in Remember if the user actually writes an entry, exactly as instructed.

**Verified live end to end via Playwright against the Dev panel, not assumed correct from reading the diff.** Real isolated pre-existing test connection (Jordan Blake and Sam Rivera, `ca5379b6-df62-4389-8d5a-b8c2675b8ba1`, the same pair used in the July 27 Remember build session), reset to a clean baseline first (cleared any leftover `meetup_checkins`, `last_plan_activity_at` set to now). 13/13 checks passed:
- Backdated `last_plan_activity_at` (170h) and ran the checkin evaluator via the Dev tab, confirmed `fired`.
- Jordan resolved "Yes, but it was rough" on the real checkin card; switched accounts, Sam saw the correct confirm-or-correct framing naming Jordan and resolved "Yes, it went well."
- Confirmed the `not_good` card rendered for Sam with both the new note link and "Not now" visible.
- **Fix 1:** tapped "Not now", confirmed the card was gone immediately on the same page (no navigation, no reload). Then did a full page reload (fresh mount) and confirmed the card **reappeared**, proving the dismiss is genuinely session-scoped, not a permanent server-side suppression, exactly per the "stop showing me this right now, not never resurface this" instruction.
- **Fix 2:** tapped the new note link, confirmed it navigated to `/remember/{connectionId}`, added a raw-only entry ("Save as written" → "Save entry") with a unique marker string, confirmed it saved and rendered in the Timeline.
- Confirmed via direct query afterward: `meetup_count` stayed `0`, `meetup_checkins` correctly held `outcome = 'rough'` for Jordan and `outcome = 'went_well'` for Sam, and exactly one `remember_entries` row existed for the connection, matching every claim above independently of what the UI displayed.
- Cleaned up afterward: deleted the test `remember_entries` row and the `meetup_checkins` rows, restored `last_plan_activity_at` to its exact original value (`2026-07-21 01:26:00.113263+00`), reconfirmed `status = 'active'` and `meetup_count = 0` unchanged. Temporary Playwright test script and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed, confirmed via `git diff` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` carry no trace of it (the diffs both files show are pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted changes from before this session).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean before and after. No em dashes in any new code (checked directly).

**Not touched, out of scope for this fix:** whether a rough-but-mutually-acknowledged meetup should ever count toward `meetup_count`/Remember's automatic criteria is still an undecided product question, explicitly not answered here per instruction, this fix only adds a manual path to log it. The `good` branch's own "Keep a note of how it went" link and Pause/Honest Exit's underlying mechanics were not modified.

### Session, July 27, 2026 (new feature: "Summarize for me" on the Remember Timeline)

Built a new explicit, user-tapped AI summary of a person's own Remember entries, following the same choice pattern as "Organize with AI" on a single entry (per instruction: never ambient, never auto-triggered).

**Built:**
- New Edge Function `summarize-remember-timeline` (`supabase/functions/summarize-remember-timeline/index.ts`). Reads every `remember_entries` row for the given connection scoped to the caller's own JWT (RLS, `auth.uid() = user_id`, is the actual access control, same pattern every other user-scoped function in this codebase already uses, no separate participant check needed), summarizing **only `raw_text`, never `organized_text`**, deliberate per instruction: feeding an AI's own prior paraphrase back into a second summarization pass would compound drift away from the user's actual words across repeated regenerations.
- The strict content rules (only summarize what's explicitly written, no added facts, no inferred emotional tone, preserve explicitly stated emotion faithfully, no speculation about the relationship/person/future, sparse entries get a correspondingly short summary) are enforced via the Anthropic `system` parameter, a genuine first for this codebase, every other AI-assist function here inlines its instructions into the one user message instead. Kept the rules structurally separate from the entry content specifically because they're non-negotiable and unusually strict, per instruction.
- Same truncation-as-failure and thinking-token-buffer discipline every AI-assist function in this app has followed since the 2026-07-25/26 Clean Up fix (`stop_reason: "max_tokens"` is treated as a failure, not a partial result; fixed generous budget, not scaled to input length, since summary output is meant to stay short regardless of entry count).
- `src/lib/remember.ts`: new `summarizeRememberTimeline(connectionId)`.
- `src/app/remember/[connectionId].tsx`: new "Summarize for me" pill button placed next to "Add an entry", above the entry list. Hidden entirely (not disabled) when `entries.length === 0`, matching AGENTS.md's own stated preference for feature-gating on genuine ineligibility rather than a dead disabled control with no explanation. Result renders in a visually distinct card (`border-accent-500/40 bg-accent-500/5`, the same tinted-card treatment `MeetupOutcomeCard`'s "good" branch already uses for an AI-adjacent moment in this app) with a sparkles icon and an explicit "AI summary" caption label, never blended into the plain white entry cards used for the user's own raw/organized text.
- **Regeneration: fresh every tap, nothing cached, per the instruction's own recommendation.** No new column, no stored summary. The local `timelineSummary` state is explicitly cleared (not left stale) whenever the underlying entries change under it: after a single-entry delete, after "Delete all history," and after saving a new entry, so a summary can never silently reference entries that no longer exist or leave out one that was just added.

**Verified live end to end via Playwright against the Dev panel**, real isolated test connection (Jordan Blake and Sam Rivera, `ca5379b6-df62-4389-8d5a-b8c2675b8ba1`, the same pair used in the two prior Remember-adjacent sessions), confirmed at a clean zero-entry baseline before starting. 12/12 checks passed, covering all three states asked for:
- **Zero entries:** navigated directly to the Timeline (bypassing the People List, which wouldn't list this connection yet), confirmed the empty state renders and confirmed "Summarize for me" is genuinely absent from the DOM, not just disabled.
- **One entry:** added a real entry ("We got coffee downtown and talked about her new marketing job. I felt really nervous before meeting up but it went well and I was relieved afterward."), confirmed the button appears, tapped it, confirmed a labeled "AI summary" card renders. Read the actual returned text rather than just checking for a non-empty string: it correctly referenced the coffee/job details that were actually written and correctly preserved the explicitly stated "nervous" emotion, matching content rules 1 and 3.
- **Multiple entries, including a deliberately sparse one:** added a second, much shorter entry ("Quick call, mostly logistics for next time."), tapped Summarize again, confirmed the summary regenerated (differed from the one-entry version, proving it's genuinely fresh-every-tap, not cached) and correctly folded in the sparse second entry without padding it into something more elaborate than what was written, the combined two-entry summary landed at 38 words, matching content rule 5.
- **Cleanup path:** deleted both test entries through the UI, confirmed "Summarize for me" disappears again and the empty state returns, confirming the button's visibility genuinely tracks live entry count rather than a stale flag.
- Restored afterward: connection re-confirmed at `entry_count = 0`, `meetup_count = 0`, `status = 'active'`, its exact original state. Temporary Playwright test script and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed, confirmed no trace in `package.json`/`package-lock.json`.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean. No em dashes in any new copy (the one regex match in the new function is the same pre-existing strip-em-dash pattern already used in `organize-remember-entry` and `generate-reply-draft`, not new prose). Function deployed (`supabase functions deploy summarize-remember-timeline`).

**Not built, out of scope:** no caching, by design, see the Regeneration note above. No retry/backoff beyond the existing "tap the button again" affordance if a call fails, matching how every other AI-assist surface in this app handles a failed generation (a plain inline error, re-tap to try again, no automatic retry).

### Session, July 28, 2026 (six-item punch list: one decision, one investigation plus a real bug it surfaced, three small fixes, one investigation that found nothing to fix)

**Item 1, decision: system param vs. inline pattern for AI functions. No code changed, analysis only.** `summarize-remember-timeline` (the newest AI function, prior session) is the only one of 7 AI Edge Functions using Anthropic's `system` parameter, every other one (`organize-remember-entry`, `generate-reply-draft`, `generate-connection-analysis`, `generate-personality-narrative`, `generate-activity-suggestions`, `generate-match-suggestions`) inlines instructions into the single `user` message instead. **Recommendation: adopt `system` as the standard going forward, migrated opportunistically, not as a dedicated rewrite.** Reliability case is concrete for this app specifically: every one of those six functions concatenates real, arbitrary user-typed text into the same message that carries the instructions, a real (if so-far-unobserved) instruction-confusion risk that `system`'s structural separation is the standard mitigation for. But this project's own repeated lesson (nearly every session in this log) is that even a "safe-looking" prompt change needs live re-verification before trusting it, batch-migrating six functions with no user-facing bug driving it would be exactly the speculative churn AGENTS.md's own conventions discourage. Migrate each function to `system` the next time it's touched for an unrelated reason, so the re-verification cost rides along with work already happening, rather than opened as its own project.

**Item 2, investigation: what actually triggers a dismissed not_good MeetupOutcomeCard to reappear. Found a real, unintended bug in the "Not now" fix from the July 27 session, not just an answer to the question asked.** Tested live via the Dev panel and real in-app navigation (Back button, Inbox tab, conversation row tap, zero hard reloads): a dismissed card reappears on **any fresh mount of the thread screen**, not only when a genuinely new checkin cycle fires. Root cause: `thread/[id].tsx`'s own "Back" button calls `router.replace('/home')`, not `router.back()`, matching this project's own July 16, 2026 session note that every screen transition past verify-code deliberately uses `replace()` so the navigation stack never accumulates. A real, previously undocumented consequence of that choice: the thread screen is **never preserved across a real exit and re-entry** the way a tab screen is, there is no code path in this app today where a user leaves and returns to a thread without a full remount. Since the July 27 fix's dismissal was local `useState` (keyed by `checkin_id`, cleared on remount by design, intentionally NOT a permanent server dismiss per that session's own instruction), this meant the card was resurfacing on almost every real visit, not staying dismissed "for this session" as intended.

**Fixed as a same-session follow-up once the user confirmed the direction:** migration `20260802000000` adds `meetup_outcome_dismissals` (checkin_id, user_id, dismissed_at, primary key on both), modeled on `rhythm_mismatch_dismissals`, deliberately NOT on `no_ghost_prompts`: this is a pure personal record with no cross-participant reconciliation logic (a `meetup_checkins` row already belongs to exactly one participant), so it fits the plain own-row-RLS client-writable pattern rather than needing `no_ghost_prompts`'s SECURITY DEFINER RPCs. `get_meetup_checkin_status` (dropped and recreated, return-type change) now returns an `outcome_dismissed` field. `thread/[id].tsx`'s local `dismissedCheckinId` state is gone; "Not now" now calls a new `dismissMeetupOutcome()` (a real insert) and re-fetches `checkinStatus`, and the render condition reads the persisted flag. Still not permanent in the "never resurface" sense: a genuinely new checkin cycle gets a new `checkin_id`, which was never dismissed, so a fresh not_good outcome still shows correctly.

**Verified live (13/13 checks), real isolated test connection (Jordan Blake/Sam Rivera, `ca5379b6-df62-4389-8d5a-b8c2675b8ba1`), all via real in-app navigation, no reloads:** dismissed the not_good card, did two full navigate-away-and-back cycles (Back → Inbox tab → real conversation row tap), confirmed the card **stayed dismissed both times** (the fix). Then fired a genuinely new checkin cycle via the Dev panel and confirmed a fresh not_good card correctly still showed for the new cycle, proving the fix doesn't over-suppress real future outcomes. `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. Connection restored to its exact original state (`status`, `meetup_count`, `last_plan_activity_at`, zero leftover `meetup_checkins`/`meetup_outcome_dismissals` rows) afterward.

**Item 3, fix: date picker for "Add an entry."** Checked current storage first: `created_at` (auto, save-time only) was the only date on `remember_entries`, and it's also the tiebreaker `fetchTimelineEntries` sorts by, after `meetup_number_at_entry` (the real ordering key, derived from `connections.meetup_count`, not a date, per the 2026-07-28 milestone redesign). Least-disruptive approach: migration `20260802000001` adds `meetup_date date`, nullable, purely a display field, `created_at` and the sort order are both untouched, deliberately, re-ordering by a user-editable date would directly contradict the milestone redesign's own reasoning for moving away from date-based ordering. UI is a plain `YYYY-MM-DD` text input in `RememberEntryComposer` ("When did this happen? (optional)"), following this project's own established precedent (`profile-basics.tsx`'s birthdate field, same documented stale-Metro-bundle-after-a-native-dependency reasoning, explicitly not a native date picker), same round-trip validation and future-date rejection. Timeline's `dateLabel` now prefers `meetup_date` when set, falls back to `created_at` otherwise. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Item 4, fix: visual prominence of "Keep a note."** Both `MeetupOutcomeCard` note links (good branch's "Keep a note of how it went," not_good branch's "Keep a note about what happened") changed from plain caption-style text links to bordered, accent-tinted pill buttons (`border-accent-500 bg-accent-500/10`), the same treatment already used elsewhere in this app for a positive/selected state (the Dev tab's selected-connection pill), not a new one-off style.

**Item 5, fix: "Get a fresh idea" copy.** Confirmed the exact current text and placement first: `meetup-outcome-card.tsx`, good branch only, not_good has no equivalent button. The literal requested phrase didn't fit as one pill-button label (a question plus a CTA crammed into a small button), so it was split: a plain caption "Planning another hangout?" now sits directly above the existing short "Get a fresh idea" button.

**Item 6, investigation: why "Get a fresh idea" has a noticeable delay. Neither of the two proposed diagnoses was actually correct, reported rather than forced into one.** Traced the tap handler (a synchronous state set, no async work before the modal opens) and `ActivitySuggestionsModal`, which already has a `step === 'loading'` state showing "Finding a few ideas..." with a spinner. Timed it live: the spinner appeared 203ms after tap, not a hidden delay. The actual wait afterward is real network/LLM latency, matching what the F23 session already documented: `SET_COUNT = 3` and the `activity_suggestion_pool` reserve table mean roughly 2 in 3 taps read a pre-generated set instantly and 1 in 3 costs a real 7-30 second Claude call. This test run hit the fast path (1.4s total). Conclusion: expected latency, loading indicator already exists and is responsive, no code change made, an optional smaller enhancement (reassurance copy past some wait threshold, for the slow-path taps specifically) was flagged but not built, out of scope once the premise didn't hold.

**Verified (items 3-6 combined live pass), same test connection:** set up a mutual `went_well` (good branch) to reach "Get a fresh idea," confirmed item 5's new caption and unchanged button text both render, then measured item 6's timing as described above. 7/7 checks passed. Connection restored afterward (`meetup_count` reset to 0, all test `meetup_checkins`/`remember_entries` rows removed, `last_plan_activity_at` restored to its exact original value).

**Verified (whole session):** `tsc --noEmit` clean after every edit. No em dashes in any new code (checked directly). Two new migrations applied (`20260802000000`, `20260802000001`). All temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed after each verification pass, confirmed no trace in `package.json`/`package-lock.json` beyond the pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted diff already present at the start of this session. One incidental finding: the local dev server process died mid-session (unrelated to these changes, `netstat` showed nothing listening on 8081), restarted cleanly with `npx expo start --web`, no data or code lost.

**Not built:** item 6's optional slow-path reassurance copy (flagged, not requested). Item 1's actual migration of the six inline-pattern functions to `system` (a deliberate future-opportunistic decision, not a gap).

### Session, July 28, 2026 (bug fix: AI reflection page truncation, `generate-personality-narrative`)

**Reported by the user as a regression via live onboarding: the AI reflection page always fell back to the generic client-side error copy instead of generating real sections.** Investigated as a separate discovery pass first (findings only, no changes), then fixed once the root cause was confirmed.

**Root cause, confirmed via direct calls to the deployed function (not assumed):** not a code regression, not `isValidSection` rejecting sections (that code path never even runs on a truncation failure, it's reached only after a successful, complete Anthropic response). The function's own `stop_reason === 'max_tokens'` check (added in the July 26 fix) was firing intermittently: Claude's response was getting cut off mid-JSON before finishing, causing a 502, which the client (`big-five-assessment.tsx`) surfaces as its own generic fallback text, a different string from the per-section `FALLBACK_*` constants inside the edge function itself.

**Why the July 26 fix (max_tokens 800 to 2000) didn't actually cover this, confirmed by re-reading that session's own log, not assumed:** the bug fixed that day was specifically the *empty*-evidence-group case (Profile-tab retake, which never sends `friendshipExperience`, leaving two of three sections with nothing to cite, a structurally short expected output), reproduced 3-for-3 broken and "reverified 4 for 4 clean" after the fix. That verification never appears to have been run against the *fully-populated* case, all 10 Big Five answers plus all 5 friendship-experience answers plus a full profile, which is what real fresh onboarding actually sends and asks the model to write a meaningfully longer, multi-citation response for. Confirmed live: with the fully-populated input held byte-identical across repeated calls, the old 2000-token budget truncated roughly 50% of the time (3 of 6 test calls), with every failing call's wall-clock time (17.0-19.5s) longer than every succeeding call's (11.7-15.8s), consistent with the model running up against the ceiling mid-generation specifically on this scenario. Not a regression from any code or config change: the deployed function's `updated_at` matched the local file's mtime exactly (both July 26, 23:52 UTC), confirming nothing had been edited or redeployed since that day.

**Fixed, `supabase/functions/generate-personality-narrative/index.ts`, both parts as scoped:**
1. `max_tokens` raised from 2000 to 4000, sized for the fully-populated case specifically (a much larger evidence set requiring more citations and a longer `connectionStyle` section), not a small bump on the number already shown insufficient for that case.
2. A genuine truncation-safe retry: the Anthropic call was extracted into a local `callAnthropic()` function; if the first attempt's `stop_reason` is `max_tokens`, the function retries once (fresh, independent call) before falling back to the 502 error response. Not relying on the token number alone, since Claude's output length has real variance regardless of budget (confirmed live in the original investigation: byte-identical input produced different generation times and different outcomes across repeated calls).

**Verified live against the fully-populated case specifically, not the already-known-fine empty-group case, per the explicit testing requirement:** 20 calls total (12 then 8, rotating across 4 real seed accounts, all 10 Big Five + all 5 friendship-experience answers filled every time, matching real fresh onboarding).

| | Before fix | After fix |
|---|---|---|
| Fully-populated case, failure rate | 3 of 6 (50%) | 0 of 20 (0%) |

All 20 post-fix calls returned real, non-fallback generated text for all three sections. Three of the twenty took notably longer (28-30s vs. a typical 10-18s), plausibly the retry path engaging on a first attempt that would otherwise have truncated, though this wasn't directly instrumented (no debug logging was added to the deployed function to confirm this specifically, flagged as an inference from timing rather than a confirmed mechanism).

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (this file is excluded from the project's `tsconfig.json`, `supabase/functions/**`, so it was never in that check's scope to begin with, same as every other Edge Function in this codebase). No em dashes in any new code. Function redeployed (`supabase functions deploy generate-personality-narrative`). Temporary investigation and stress-test scripts removed after use, no persistent test artifacts left in the repo (this fix required no database writes or seed-account mutations, only direct calls to the deployed function using real sessions, so there was nothing to restore afterward).

**Not built:** exact token-count instrumentation for the three slow post-fix calls (would require temporary debug logging in the deployed function, not done since the 0/20 failure rate already answers the question that mattered). No changes to `isValidSection`, the prompt, the model, or any other part of this function, this was scoped strictly to the truncation failure.

### Session, July 28, 2026 (cost fix: activity filter on the 6-hourly match-suggestions batch refresh)

**Investigated first, confirmed via direct code and live `cron.job`/schema reads before changing anything: the 6-hourly batch refresh (`refresh-match-suggestions` cron job, calling `generate-match-suggestions`'s `runBatchRefresh()`) had no activity/recency filter of any kind.** `select id, is_premium from public.users` has no `WHERE` clause, no join to any last-active signal, the only skip was a user with no `profiles` row yet (mid-onboarding, a data-completeness guard, not an activity filter). Every real account, active or not, got a full Claude regeneration attempt every 6 hours indefinitely. Separately confirmed the on-demand fallback in `home.tsx` is fully independent of this cron (reads `match_suggestions` directly, calls this same function's normal per-user path only when the cache is empty or older than 24h, no dependency on the cron ever having run for that user), making a recency filter here safe: an excluded user just gets a normal-latency real generation next time they open Discover, the same fallback that already covers a brand-new user before their first cron cycle.

**Checked for an existing activity signal before adding anything new, per instruction:** `public.users` has no last-active/last-login column of its own, but `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` (standard GoTrue-managed) already exists and is already genuinely updated by this project's real sign-in path (`devSignInAs`'s `verifyOtp` call), confirmed live against real rows, not assumed. No new tracking added.

**Fixed:**
- Migration `20260802000002` adds `public.batch_refresh_eligible_users(p_days integer default 7)`, a plain SQL `security definer` function (same shape and convention as `compatible_candidates_for`), joining `public.users` to `auth.users` and filtering on `last_sign_in_at >= now() - 7 days`. A dedicated function was necessary, not optional: `auth` is never in Supabase's exposed PostgREST schema list (service role doesn't change this, it's a schema-exposure setting, not an RLS bypass), so the batch refresh's service-role client can't join `auth.users` directly via `.from(...)`. Granted to `service_role` only, deliberately not `authenticated`/`anon`, sign-in recency isn't something any ordinary client should be able to query about any user.
- `generate-match-suggestions/index.ts`'s `runBatchRefresh()`: `supabase.from('users').select('id, is_premium')` replaced with `supabase.rpc('batch_refresh_eligible_users', { p_days: 7 })`. No other logic in the function touched.

**Verified live, both at the SQL level and end-to-end through the real deployed batch endpoint, not assumed correct from reading the migration alone:**
- Baseline: `select count(*) from batch_refresh_eligible_users(7)` returned 10 of 10 real `public.users` rows, all currently "active" within 7 days, see the honest before/after note below for why.
- Simulated a stale account (per instruction, since no naturally-stale real account currently exists, see below): backdated one real seed account's (Priya Nair, `+15555500105`) `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` to 10 days ago, snapshotting the real original value first. `batch_refresh_eligible_users(7)` count dropped from 10 to 9, and the stale account's id was confirmed absent from the result set, while all 9 other accounts remained present.
- Confirmed this holds through the actual deployed function, not just the isolated SQL function: called the live batch endpoint directly (same `x-batch-secret` header the real cron uses) and got back `{"batch":true,"usersProcessed":9}`, matching the SQL-level count exactly.
- Restored the test account's `last_sign_in_at` to its exact original snapshotted value afterward, reconfirmed the eligible count returned to 10.

**Before/after volume numbers, reported honestly rather than a convenient estimate:** in this project's real, current dataset, **0% reduction right now** — all 10 real `public.users` rows show a `last_sign_in_at` within the last day (several within the last hour), because this project's own dev-tooling (`devSignInAs`) genuinely re-authenticates real seed accounts constantly during active development and testing, which is exactly what's been happening throughout this session. This isn't a flaw in the fix, it's an accurate reflection of a codebase in heavy active testing, where "every account looks active" is actually true right now. The mechanism itself is proven correct (the 10-to-9 simulation above), and its real cost benefit will show up the moment any account genuinely goes quiet for a week, which is exactly the case this filter exists to catch and which testing activity has so far masked in the live numbers. Flagging this distinction explicitly rather than reporting a reduction percentage that the current data doesn't actually support.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (function code excluded from that check's scope like every other Edge Function here, still confirmed the rest of the app is unaffected). No em dashes in any new code. Function redeployed (`supabase functions deploy generate-match-suggestions`). Temporary test script removed after use. The one real seed account touched for the simulation was restored to its exact original `last_sign_in_at` value, confirmed via direct re-query, not just assumed.

**Not built:** no change to the 7-day window being configurable beyond the function's own `p_days` parameter (already flexible if a different window is wanted later, just requires updating the one call site). No change to the daily suggestion cap, scoring model, or per-user on-demand path, this was scoped strictly to which users the batch cron includes.

### Session, July 28, 2026 (feature: moved "Your experience with new friendship" from onboarding to a post-first-message trigger)

**Discovery first, per instruction, before any code changed.**
- `profiles.friendship_experience` was written by `big-five-assessment.tsx`'s own `handleFinishExperience()`, a plain upsert, reachable only from the onboarding-only `'experience'` phase between the 10 Big Five questions and the AI reflection.
- `generate-personality-narrative` already handles a missing/absent `friendshipExperience` gracefully, confirmed by re-reading the July 26 fix rather than assumed: `experienceAtoms()` returns nothing for the "helped"/"uncertain" categories when it's omitted, `isValidSection` requires `supportedBy.length > 0`, so both sections always fail validation and fall through to the fixed `FALLBACK_WHAT_HELPED`/`FALLBACK_WHEN_UNCERTAIN` constants, no crash, no verbose blow-up, this is the exact mechanism the July 26 truncation fix put in place. **The real gap wasn't server-side**: the server can't tell "no evidence, genuinely never will be" apart from "no evidence yet, hasn't been asked," and was never going to need to, since the client already knows which case it's in (it decides whether to send `friendshipExperience` at all). The new "coming later" copy is therefore a client-side display decision, not a server change, no edits made to the edge function's prompt, validation, or fallback constants.
- The reflection is never persisted anywhere, confirmed by reading every call site: `connectionStyle`/`whatHelped`/`whenUncertain` are always computed fresh and held only in local component state, both during onboarding and via the Profile tab's retake. "Regenerate after answering" just means calling the function again with the now-available data, there's no cache to invalidate.
- **A real, unrelated bug found while reading this exact code, fixed as part of the same pass:** `profile.tsx`'s own "View my personality reflection" button (`openReflection`) still read `data.narrative`, a field the server hasn't returned since the July 26 rebuild to `{connectionStyle, whatHelped, whenUncertain}`. This button has been silently, unconditionally falling back to the generic error text for every user, every time, since that rebuild, confirmed by direct code read, not assumed. Fixed as described below.

**Built:**
- New `src/lib/friendship-experience.ts`: the 5 `EXPERIENCE_QUESTIONS` (unchanged content, moved out of `big-five-assessment.tsx`), `FriendshipExperience` type, `saveFriendshipExperience()`, and `shouldShowFriendshipExperiencePrompt(userId)`, the eligibility check: fires when the earliest message in ANY of the user's connections (regardless of who sent it, exactly "sent or received, whichever comes first") is 4+ hours old, or, if no message exists at all yet, when 7+ days have passed since `users.behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at` (the real "onboarding fully complete" timestamp `readiness-commitment.tsx` already writes). Checked live on load, not server-pushed, no push infrastructure exists in this app, matching how every other elapsed-time prompt here without a DB-backed cron evaluator already gets surfaced ("next app open"). Confirmed the three RLS policies this needed (`users` self-row, `connections` participant-scoped, `messages` participant-scoped via connection membership) already permit exactly this query shape before writing it, no new policies needed.
- `big-five-assessment.tsx`: the `'experience'` phase is gone entirely. Both fresh onboarding and edit-mode retakes now go straight from the 10 questions to the reflection, the same path edit-mode already used. Still tracks whether `profiles.friendship_experience` is already on file (`hasStoredExperience`, loaded the same way `big_five_scores.responses` already was) so a retake correctly includes it once it exists, rather than always omitting it.
- New `src/components/friendship-experience-modal.tsx` (`FriendshipExperienceModal`): a full-screen `Modal`, same 5 questions, saves via `saveFriendshipExperience`, then immediately calls `generate-personality-narrative` with the saved answers plus the user's stored Big Five responses (reconstructed the same way the Profile tab retake already does) and shows the real, regenerated three-section reflection in place, so "what has helped"/"when uncertain" visibly go from pending copy to grounded content in the same interaction. A "Not now" link closes without answering (re-checked on the next eligible load, same as any other unanswered elapsed-time prompt in this app).
- `home.tsx`: calls `shouldShowFriendshipExperiencePrompt` once per load (the app's default landing tab), fetches the user's stored Big Five responses only when actually eligible (so an already-answered user, the common case almost immediately after this ships, never pays for the extra query), shows `FriendshipExperienceModal`.
- `profile.tsx`'s `openReflection`, fixed alongside: now reads `data.connectionStyle`/`whatHelped`/`whenUncertain` (matching what the server has actually returned since July 26), sends the caller's own `friendship_experience` when present, and the modal renders all three sections instead of one. Also added `friendship_experience` to the profile select query and `OwnProfile` type.

**New copy (item 3), deliberately not reusing the old retake-flow fallback text**, per instruction, since that text ("You may not have one consistent pattern...") was written for "this will never apply to you," a different, no-longer-accurate scenario now that this data is genuinely collected later for everyone:
- What has helped before (pending): *"Once you've connected with someone, come back here and we'll reflect on what helped it go well."*
- When things feel uncertain (pending): *"This will take shape once you've had a real conversation or two. Come back after you've connected with someone and we'll reflect on what felt uncertain."*
- "How you tend to connect" always renders normally at this stage, unaffected, it never depended on `friendship_experience`.

**Verified live (all three scenarios plus a negative control), real Dev-panel sessions, no reload shortcuts:**
- **Fresh onboarding (Marcus Alvarez, no stored Big Five data, a genuinely first-time run through the real form):** answered the 10 questions live via real clicks, confirmed the "Your experience with new friendship" phase never appears, confirmed the results screen is reached directly, confirmed "How you tend to connect" renders real grounded text, confirmed "What has helped before" and "When things feel uncertain" show the exact new pending copy above, confirmed the OLD retake fallback text never appears. 7/7 checks passed.
- **4-hours-after-first-message (Jordan Blake / Sam Rivera's real connection, `ca5379b6-df62-4389-8d5a-b8c2675b8ba1`):** backdated their existing message to 5 hours old, seeded Sam with realistic stored Big Five responses (needed for the regeneration call to have real answers to work from, matching what real onboarding would have produced, confirmed no seed account actually has this data since none of them went through the real onboarding UI), signed in as Sam, confirmed `FriendshipExperienceModal` appeared automatically on `/home` with no manual trigger. Answered all 5 questions, confirmed the modal immediately showed a real, regenerated "What has helped before" section, no longer the pending copy. 5/5 checks passed.
- **7-day no-message backstop (Priya Nair, zero messages across any connection):** backdated `users.behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at` to 8 days ago, confirmed the modal correctly appeared on `/home`. 2/2 checks passed.
- **Negative control:** set the same account's onboarding-completed timestamp to 2 days ago instead (under both the 4-hour and 7-day thresholds, still zero messages), confirmed the modal correctly stayed hidden, ruling out "it always shows" as a false-positive explanation for the passing tests above.
- A real bug in the test itself, not the feature, was caught and fixed mid-session: the first attempt at the 4-hour test used a short fixed wait after answering, which isn't long enough for a real `generate-personality-narrative` call (this session's own earlier stress testing already found these calls legitimately take 10-30 seconds); fixed by polling for the loading spinner to disappear instead of a fixed timeout, documented here so a future rerun of this test doesn't reintroduce the same flake.
- All test accounts restored to their exact original state afterward: Sam's `friendship_experience` and `big_five_scores` back to null, her message's `created_at` restored to its exact original snapshotted value, Priya's `behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at` restored to null, Marcus's `big_five_scores` restored to null. One caveat disclosed rather than glossed over: Sam's original `big_five_scores` value was never snapshotted before being seeded for the test (it was needed mid-test, not anticipated beforehand), restored to null based on the strong pattern every other checked seed account shared (none had real stored responses), not a directly verified original value.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new or edited file. AGENTS.md's onboarding order line and F6 entry updated to match (the "New order" line no longer lists the 5-question section inside onboarding, F6 documents the new trigger and the retake-flow grounding fix). Temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed after each verification pass.

**Not built:** no push notification for the trigger (this app has no push infrastructure at all, a standing, already-documented limitation, "next app open" is the same surfacing convention every other elapsed-time prompt here uses without one). No snooze/dismiss-for-N-days on "Not now," it simply re-checks eligibility (still true) on the next load, matching that this is a one-time lifetime prompt with no need for the snooze machinery `no_ghost_prompts`-style features have for their own, different, repeating-escalation use case.

### Session, July 28, 2026 (feature: referral reward system)

**Discovery first, per instruction.**
- Confirmed genuinely unbuilt, not just undocumented: grepped the entire repo for "referral"/"referred_by"/"referral_code", the only match anywhere was AGENTS.md's own long-standing "zero matching code anywhere" note. `information_schema` confirmed `public.users` had no related columns before this session.
- Confirmed how premium is currently stored before building on top of it: `users.is_premium`, a flat boolean, default `false`. **No expiry column, no subscription table, no billing/IAP integration anywhere** (re-confirmed from the Capacity System's own build session), meaning there has never been a concept of a *temporary* premium grant for anyone in this codebase, real subscriber or not. The task's own recommended behavior ("extend their existing expiry") presupposes a mechanism that didn't exist yet, flagged rather than assumed, and the minimal real gap this session had to close before the reward system could mean anything.

**Built (migration `20260803000000`):**
- `users.referral_code` (unique, 8 chars, auto-assigned by a `before insert` trigger the moment a `users` row first exists, so every account gets one regardless of which of the several existing onboarding screens happens to create that row first). Ambiguous characters (`0`/`O`, `1`/`I`) excluded on purpose, a code meant to be typed or read aloud, not just copy-pasted.
- `users.referred_by` (nullable, self-referencing), set once via `redeem_referral_code(p_code)`, a SECURITY DEFINER RPC, not a plain client upsert: `users`' own SELECT RLS is self-row only, so a client can never look up whose code matches on its own, this function is the only way to resolve a code at all. Also enforces no-self-referral and that `referred_by` can only ever be set once (redeeming a later code can't retroactively swap who gets credit).
- `users.was_referred_reward_claimed` / `users.referred_someone_reward_claimed`, two independent one-time booleans per account, not a shared pool, no cap on chain depth by construction (each account's `referred_by` points to exactly one direct referrer, evaluated independently of any other link in the chain).
- `users.premium_until` (nullable timestamptz), the new minimal piece making a time-boxed grant possible at all, added to the same `users` table `is_premium` already lives on, not a parallel system. `grant_premium_days(user_id, days)`, the shared grant logic: if already `is_premium` with a real tracked `premium_until`, extends from the later of "now" or the existing expiry (so an active grant never loses unused time); if `is_premium` is true with `premium_until` null (this app's only representation of a permanent/manually-set grant, since no real billing has ever set an expiring one), **leaves it completely untouched**, the exact "don't conflict with real billing" case the task asked to confirm; otherwise sets `is_premium = true` and a fresh 30-day `premium_until`. A new daily cron, `expire-premium-grants`, flips `is_premium` back to `false` once a tracked `premium_until` passes, so every existing reader of `is_premium` (capacity checks, `generate-match-suggestions`' `dailyCap`) stays correct unchanged, none of those call sites were touched.
- Qualification gate (`referral_reward_qualifies`): the referred account's own `behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at is not null` (onboarding fully complete, the same signal the 2026-07-28 friendship-experience trigger already uses) AND `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` (the same activity signal the same day's batch-refresh cost fix already uses) falls within the final few days of the 30-day window since real signup (`auth.users.created_at`), not just once early on. The exact grace window (3 days) isn't given a specific number by the task, a judgment call, documented in the function's own comment rather than picked silently.
- `check_and_grant_referral_reward_for(referred_user_id)`: the real evaluator, grants up to two rewards in one pass (the referred account's own, always checked first since it's what qualification is gated on; the referrer's, only if they haven't already claimed theirs from any referral). Idempotent, safe to call repeatedly. `run_referral_reward_check_all()`, scheduled daily (`referral-reward-check`), iterates every account with an unclaimed `was_referred_reward_claimed` and a real referrer.
- Client: `profile-basics.tsx` gained an optional "Referral code" field (redeemed via the RPC above, an unrecognized code doesn't block onboarding, same "optional, skippable" tone as the email step). Also now ensures a `users` row exists at this earliest point in onboarding (a minimal `{id}` upsert alongside the existing `profiles` upsert) rather than waiting for `gender-identity.tsx`'s own later upsert, since redeeming a code needs a row to write `referred_by` onto. The Profile tab gained an "Invite a friend" row showing the caller's own code with a share action (native `Share.share()` off-web, clipboard copy on web, matching the platform split `Remember`'s own export feature already established).

**Verified live, real seed accounts, real timing simulated via direct SQL (backdating `auth.users.created_at`/`last_sign_in_at`, the same technique already used for the two prior 2026-07-28 sessions), all four required scenarios plus two extra edge cases, cleaned up and restored afterward:**
- **Full chain, A (Maria Santos) refers B (David Chen):** backdated David's signup to 35 days ago with activity at day 29 (within the grace window), confirmed `referral_reward_qualifies` returned true, ran the real evaluator, confirmed `granted_both`: David's `was_referred_reward_claimed = true`, `is_premium = true`, `premium_until` ≈ 30 days out; Maria's `referred_someone_reward_claimed = true`, same premium grant.
- **B (David) refers C (Aisha Bello), the independent-reward and stacking check:** same setup for Aisha, ran the evaluator, confirmed Aisha's own `was_referred` reward granted, AND confirmed David's `referred_someone_reward_claimed` flipped to `true` **independently of his own already-`true` `was_referred_reward_claimed` from the first link** (both flags true simultaneously, neither overwrote the other), AND confirmed his `premium_until` genuinely **extended** from the first grant's expiry (Aug 28 to Sep 27, exactly 30 more days added on top, not reset from "now"), proving the extend-not-reset logic works on a real second grant, not just in isolation.
- **A referred account that never qualifies:** two sub-cases, both confirmed to grant nothing. (1) Only 10 days since signup (too soon): `referral_reward_qualifies` false, evaluator made no change. (2) 40 days since signup but last real activity was day 2, never returned (the actual point of the activity gate, not just the elapsed-time check): also correctly false, also granted nothing.
- **A real "permanent" premium user receiving a reward:** simulated by setting `is_premium = true, premium_until = null` directly (this app's only representation of a genuine non-expiring grant) on a qualifying referred account, ran the evaluator, confirmed `premium_until` **stayed null** and `is_premium` stayed `true`, completely untouched, while `was_referred_reward_claimed` still correctly flipped to `true` (the flag records the reward as claimed even though there was nothing left to grant on the premium side).
- **Extra, not explicitly required but load-bearing for the feature to mean anything:** directly tested `expire_premium_grants()` by setting a real grant's `premium_until` into the past and running the sweep, confirmed `is_premium` correctly flipped back to `false`.
- **Client UI, live click-through:** confirmed the new referral-code field renders on `profile-basics.tsx`, confirmed the "Invite a friend" row and a real, correctly-formatted 8-character code render on the Profile tab.
- All five touched seed accounts restored to their original state afterward. One honestly disclosed gap in the restoration: two accounts' (Aisha, Robert) pre-task `is_premium`/`behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at`/referral state wasn't individually snapshotted before mutating it (only discovered mid-test that they'd be needed), restored based on the strong, consistent pattern every other seed account checked this session shared (all false/null), not a directly verified original value, flagged rather than assumed silently correct.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new or edited file. AGENTS.md's "Referral system" line updated from "zero matching code anywhere" to the full resolved writeup. Temporary Playwright test script and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed after use.

**Not built, deliberately out of scope, flagged rather than silently folded in:** duplicate-account/device-fingerprint abuse detection and an annual referral cap, both named in AGENTS.md's own original spec for this feature, both still tracked separately under this file's existing, still-unbuilt "Fraud controls" line, not built as part of this pass. No dedicated Dev-tab UI section for this feature (unlike some earlier 2026-07-28 features), testing was done via direct SQL and RPC calls against the real deployed functions instead, a faster and equally rigorous path for a feature that's fundamentally backend timing/state logic rather than an interactive UI flow.

### Session, July 29, 2026 (feature: onboarding pagination, profile-build.tsx)

**Discovery first, per instruction.** `src/app/profile-build.tsx` (1686 lines) is a single component, one continuous `<ScrollView>` holding every `<Section>` in sequence, one flat `ProfileFormState`. Confirmed the save mechanism before touching anything: every field change already goes through `updateAndSave()` → `persist()`, a full-row `upsert` to Supabase on every single change, real per-field autosave, not a deferred end-of-form submit. This meant pagination didn't need any new save logic, only new rendering/navigation around a save path already fully working. Also confirmed this screen doubles as the Profile tab's edit mode (`?from=profile`, Cancel/Save top bar, no exit gate) — a genuinely different UX intent from onboarding.

Catalogued the actual `<Section>` blocks in code order (not assumed from the task's own description), confirmed 10 natural clusters matching what was asked, with one correction: `current_situation` doesn't exist anywhere in this file, dropped from the grouping since it isn't part of what's actually being restructured.

| Step | Section(s) | Required |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where you're based (location + radius) | Location (lat/lng) |
| 2 | What brings you here (life transitions) | Yes |
| 3 | About you | No |
| 4 | What matters to you (values) | No |
| 5 | What do you like doing (activity categories + nested follow-ups) | No |
| 6 | Hangout preference + hangout type | Both |
| 7 | Friendship type + check-in freq + meeting freq + response time + availability | First 4 |
| 8 | Communication style (2 scenarios) + communication modes + bar preference + dealbreakers | First 2 |
| 9 | Languages + Ethnicity + 16 Personalities | No |
| 10 | Photo | No |

**Scope decision, flagged rather than assumed:** pagination applies only to fresh onboarding (`!isEditMode`). Edit mode keeps the exact original single-scroll layout, completely untouched, same `(isEditMode || step === N)` condition wrapping every section means edit mode's render path is byte-identical to before this change (the condition is always true there). Forcing a user editing their already-complete profile through 10 sequential steps with Back/Next friction, when Cancel/Save already gives them free-form jump-to-any-field access, would have been a regression nobody asked for.

**Built:**
- `REQUIRED_FIELD_CHECKS`: the single source of truth both `getMissingRequiredFields` (unchanged output, exact same 10 checks, exact same messages) and the new `getMissingRequiredFieldsForStep` derive from, each check now tagged with which step it lives on. Guarantees restructuring couldn't silently change which fields are required, what they're called, or add/remove one as a side effect, the literal instruction.
- `step` state (0-9), `TOTAL_STEPS = 10`. Each of the 10 groups wrapped in `{(isEditMode || step === N) && (...)}`, no field-level JSX touched inside any of them.
- Top row (onboarding mode only): "Step N of 10" + Back, the exact pattern already used in `gender-identity.tsx`. Back on step 0 leaves the screen (`/social-linking`, unchanged from before pagination); Back on any later step just decrements `step`, purely client-side, no data implication (autosave already wrote everything as it was entered).
- Bottom button (onboarding mode only): same "tappable, not hard-disabled, label reflects state" pattern the original single Continue button already used (confirmed by reading it first, not assumed): "Complete required fields to continue" when the CURRENT step's own required fields are missing, "Next" otherwise, "Continue" only on the real last step once the full, unchanged `canContinue` (60% completion + zero missing fields across the whole profile) is satisfied. The 60% soft threshold stays a final-step-only check, deliberately not turned into a new per-step gate, it was never tied to any one section to begin with, an aggregate across the whole profile including optional fields.
- Final step's Continue: identical `router.replace(isEditMode ? '/profile' : '/big-five-assessment')`, unchanged.

**Verified live end to end, real seed account (Robert Kim), not a synthetic account:** `dev-create-session`'s allowlist only covers the 9 real seed phone numbers, so a genuinely new throwaway account can't be signed into for this kind of UI test, matching this project's own established alternative for that constraint: mutate a real seed account for the test, snapshot its exact original values first, restore afterward, verified via requery. Robert Kim's full profile-build row was snapshotted, reset to blank, and restored to its exact original values (byte-for-byte, re-confirmed via a final query) after testing.

25/25 checks passed:
- **Full flow completion, fields save identically:** walked all 10 steps with real inputs via real clicks (location confirm, life transitions, about-you text, values, an activity category, hangout preferences, friendship type + all three frequency/timing fields, both communication-style scenario questions, a language, a 16-Personalities type, skipped the optional photo), reached the final step, tapped Continue, confirmed navigation to `/big-five-assessment`. **Directly queried the saved row afterward and confirmed every single field matched exactly what was entered**, including `location_lat`/`location_lng` correctly geocoded, `completion_pct` correctly computed (78%) — not just a UI-level pass, a real database-level confirmation that per-field autosave through the new step structure produces byte-identical results to the old single-page form (expected, since `persist()` itself was never touched, but empirically confirmed rather than assumed).
- **Back navigation preserves answers:** two Back taps from step 10 landed on step 8, re-tapping the primary button there advanced cleanly with no revalidation block, meaning the communication-style answers entered earlier were still genuinely in state, not reset by navigating backward. Confirmed doubly by the final saved-row check above, since those same fields appear correctly in the final database row.
- **Required-field validation blocks at the right step, not just at the end:** confirmed at steps 1 (Location), 2 (What brings you here, including the pre-existing life-transitions-specific inline message, unchanged), 6 (Hangout style), and 8 (Communication style) — each showed its own step-scoped "Still need (required for matching): ..." message and did not advance until answered, rather than only surfacing a combined list after reaching the final step. Steps 3, 4, 5, 9 (all-optional) advanced immediately with no block, confirming optional fields didn't newly become gates.

**Verified:** `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new or edited code. Temporary Playwright test script and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`) removed after use. The local dev server died mid-session again (same recurring, unrelated symptom this file has now logged several times), restarted cleanly with `npx expo start --web`, no data or code lost.

**Not built:** no changes to `computeCompletionPct`, the underlying field set, or any validation condition, this was scoped strictly to presentation/navigation. No pagination for edit mode, a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight, see above.

### Session, July 29, 2026 (feature: consumable AI top-up packs)

**Discovery first, per instruction.**
- **IAP infrastructure: confirmed genuinely greenfield.** Grepped the whole repo and `package.json` for any IAP library, receipt, purchase, or product-id reference, zero real matches. `app.json` has no `ios.bundleIdentifier` and no `android.package`, a real, separate gap flagged below, real IAP products can't be registered in App Store Connect or Play Console without one.
- **Free-tier daily caps: built and live (Fix #3, 2026-07-21).** Confirmed by re-reading `generate-match-suggestions` directly rather than trusting the earlier session's own summary: `dailyCap = is_premium ? 5 : 2`, enforced via `match_suggestions.suggested_date` as the real cap boundary.
- **"Premium monthly pool": does not exist, a real mismatch with this task's own framing, flagged rather than assumed.** The only real usage cap anywhere in this codebase, for either tier, is the one daily cap above. There is no monthly counter, no monthly reset, no second gated pool of any kind on any AI function (re-confirmed against the full AI-function inventory built earlier this session, see the July 28 entries). Built this feature against the real, existing daily cap instead of a monthly pool that was never built, flagged here rather than building a second, parallel, more-elaborate cap system nobody asked for by name.

**Built:**
- Migration `20260804000000`: `users.ai_credits` (integer, default 0), `ai_credit_purchases` (one row per real or attempted purchase, `transaction_id` UNIQUE as the idempotency key, `status`: pending/validated/credited/invalid/failed), `ai_credit_ledger` (append-only audit trail, every grant and every spend). Both tables self-row SELECT only, no client write policy, matching this app's own established `no_ghost_prompts`-style convention for anything a client must never be able to forge. `credit_ai_purchase()` (grants credits, service-role only, called only after real server-side validation succeeds) and `try_consume_ai_credit()` (atomic, WHERE-guarded decrement, returns whether it actually succeeded).
- **A real security gap caught before it shipped, migration `20260804000001`:** `try_consume_ai_credit` needed to be callable by `authenticated` directly, not just `service_role`, since `generate-match-suggestions`' own per-user path deliberately runs as the calling user, not the service role (see that function's own header comment). Granting the original, unrestricted version to `authenticated` would have let any signed-in user drain any other account's credits by passing a different `p_user_id`. Added a `auth.uid() = p_user_id` check inside the function before widening the grant, caught during design, not after.
- New Edge Function `validate-ai-credit-purchase`: real server-side receipt validation, never trusts the client-reported purchase alone. Apple: POSTs to `buy.itunes.apple.com/verifyReceipt`, retries against the sandbox endpoint on Apple's own documented `status: 21007` (sandbox receipt sent to production). Google: manually constructs and RS256-signs a service-account JWT via Web Crypto, exchanges it for an OAuth2 token, calls the real Android Publisher API's purchase-verification endpoint. Both `APPLE_SHARED_SECRET` and `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` are confirmed **not configured** in this project, the function fails closed with a specific, honest reason (`"APPLE_SHARED_SECRET not configured on this function"`) rather than ever crediting on an unverifiable receipt. Idempotent: an already-`credited` `transaction_id` short-circuits to success without re-crediting.
- `generate-match-suggestions`' per-user path: checks `ai_credits` only when the daily cap is already hit, consumes exactly one credit for exactly one extra suggestion (`effectiveCap = dailyCap + 1`), reusing `generateAndCacheSuggestions`' own existing "remaining" math rather than a second, parallel generation path. Deliberately never wired into `runBatchRefresh` (the cron path), a service-role sweep with no real user in the loop to have consented to spending anything for them right now. New response fields: `capReached`, `usedCredit`, `aiCredits`.
- Client: `src/lib/ai-credits.ts` (`purchaseAiCreditPack`, using `react-native-iap@12.15.6`, deliberately the classic imperative API rather than v15's newer Nitro/hooks `useIAP`, a judgment call for fit with this codebase's consistently plain, non-hook-heavy style for library integrations elsewhere). Never trusts `RNIap`'s own purchase-success callback as authoritative, only finishes/consumes the platform transaction after the server has confirmed and credited it, so a lost network response between purchase and validation leaves the transaction recoverable rather than silently discarded. `home.tsx` gained a blocked-state card (shown only when `capReached`) with a "Get 50 AI credits for $1.99" button alongside the existing generic message, offered as an addition, not a replacement, matching "alongside, not instead of, any existing premium upsell."

**A second real bug found and fixed during testing, not part of the original build:** the credit-consumption check only looked at whether the daily cap count was reached, not whether there was any real candidate left to actually suggest. An account with zero remaining real compatible candidates (confirmed live, see below) was still being charged a credit and receiving nothing in return, a real billing-correctness defect, a paid consumable spent for zero deliverable value. Fixed by checking real candidate availability, using the exact same filter `generateAndCacheSuggestions` itself applies, before ever attempting to consume a credit. Redeployed and reverified clean (see Scenario 1 below).

**Tested, all four required scenarios, real accounts, real deployed functions, no mocking:**
- **Scenario 1, free user hits the daily cap (David Chen, real seed account):** simulated hitting the cap via 2 synthetic `match_suggestions` rows marked `passed` (David's real compatible pool is genuinely zero, a separate, pre-existing seed-data limitation already documented for other accounts in this file, not something this feature caused). Confirmed blocked: `capReached: true, usedCredit: false, aiCredits: 0`. Granted 50 credits via `credit_ai_purchase` (simulating a validated purchase). Confirmed the bug above live before fixing it: a credit was silently consumed for zero suggestions. Fixed, redeployed, reverified: with 50 credits and zero real candidates, `capReached` correctly stayed `true` and `aiCredits` stayed `50`, no waste. To prove the genuinely positive path (a real credit buying a real new suggestion, not just the mechanism's flags), temporarily relocated David's profile coordinates to Denver (he and Marcus Alvarez are real, gender-compatible man-seeking-man accounts already, only separated by real distance past his 30-mile radius), giving him one genuine real candidate. Re-ran: `capReached: false, usedCredit: true, aiCredits: 49`, and a real, newly AI-reasoned `match_suggestions` row for Marcus was inserted and returned. Confirmed via the ledger that exactly one `-1` row was written, and confirmed a follow-up call (Marcus now sitting unactioned) correctly served him back from cache with no second credit charged.
- **Scenario 2, premium user's pool (David Chen, flipped `is_premium = true`):** confirmed the block threshold is genuinely 5, not 2 (`capReached` stayed `false` at fewer than 5 synthetic suggestions, flipped `true` only at exactly 5). Granted 50 credits with the real pool now fully exhausted (Marcus's slot already used from Scenario 1): confirmed the anti-waste fix holds at the premium boundary too, `capReached: true, aiCredits: 50`, unchanged. The positive "credit delivers a real suggestion" path was not re-proven at this specific boundary since it's the identical code path already directly verified in Scenario 1, only the numeric threshold differs, and that threshold was independently confirmed correct here.
- **Scenario 3, failed/invalid purchase does not credit:** called `validate-ai-credit-purchase` directly with garbage receipt data. Confirmed `HTTP 402`, `ai_credits` unchanged, and a real `status: 'invalid'` row written to `ai_credit_purchases` with `credits_granted: null`.
- **Scenario 4, receipt validation is genuinely server-side:** with no `APPLE_SHARED_SECRET` configured, confirmed the function fails closed with an honest, specific reason rather than a generic error. Then temporarily set a placeholder `APPLE_SHARED_SECRET` (not a real one, not needed for this proof) and called again with the same garbage receipt: got back `"Apple verifyReceipt status 21002, matched=false"`, Apple's own real, documented error code for malformed receipt data, unambiguous proof of a genuine outbound network call to Apple's live servers, not a local check. Removed the placeholder secret immediately after and reconfirmed the function returned to its honest fail-closed state, `supabase secrets list` reconfirmed clean.
- All test mutations on David Chen's account (location, `is_premium`, `ai_credits`, synthetic `match_suggestions`/`connections`/ledger/purchase rows) fully reverted and independently reverified via requery; his one real pre-existing connection (Jordan Blake, active) was never touched. No throwaway auth accounts were created this session, all four scenarios were exercisable against a real seed account.

**Verified:** No `tsc --noEmit` run against the new Edge Function or `supabase/functions/**` generally, consistent with the rest of this codebase, those are Deno runtime files outside the app's own TypeScript project and were never covered by that check. `npx tsc --noEmit` against the app itself (including `src/lib/ai-credits.ts` and `home.tsx`) is clean. No em dashes in any new file, caught and fixed one in a first draft of `home.tsx`'s own button label before finalizing. `validate-ai-credit-purchase` and `generate-match-suggestions` both deployed (the latter twice, once for the initial integration, once for the anti-waste fix). `react-native-iap@12.15.6` is a real, permanent dependency addition, unlike the repeatedly-installed-then-removed Playwright pattern elsewhere in this file, confirmed present in `package.json`/`package-lock.json`.

**Not built, real gaps, flagged rather than assumed away:**
- **The native purchase sheet itself cannot be exercised in this dev environment.** `react-native-iap` requires a real native build (StoreKit/Play Billing are native modules, no web or Expo Go equivalent), the same standing limitation this file has already flagged for deepidv and voice-to-text. Everything up to and including the real purchase UI is written correctly but unexercised; everything from "a purchase happened" onward (server validation, crediting, consumption, blocking) was proven against the real deployed backend.
- **The cancelled-purchase client path (`E_USER_CANCELLED`) is inherently untestable outside a real purchase sheet**, it resolves to `{status: 'cancelled'}` before `validate-ai-credit-purchase` is ever called, there is no server-side call to intercept for this specific case. What was tested instead, and is the actually security-relevant half of "a failed purchase must not credit," is Scenario 3 (a purchase that reaches the server but fails validation).
- `app.json` has no real `ios.bundleIdentifier`/`android.package`, a real, separate blocker for ever registering the `ai_credits_50` product in App Store Connect or Play Console, out of scope for this task but a genuine dependency for shipping it.
- No real `APPLE_SHARED_SECRET`/`GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` configured, same standing gap as every other unconfigured third-party key in this project (Ticketmaster, Eventbrite).
- No refund/chargeback handling, no admin tooling to manually adjust a user's `ai_credits`, neither was asked for.

### Session, July 29, 2026 (feature: free-tier AI usage caps and the premium shared monthly dollar pool)

**Discovery first, per instruction, before writing anything.**
- Grepped the whole repo and every migration for anything resembling a usage counter or reset-logic pattern: the only real one anywhere is `generate-match-suggestions`' own daily cap (Fix #3, 2026-07-21, `match_suggestions.suggested_date` as the boundary). No other AI function had any cap, free or premium. Confirmed directly from `20260804000000_add_ai_credits.sql`'s own header comment (from the 2026-07-29 consumable-credits session, built the same day): "The 'premium monthly pool' this feature was meant to sit alongside never existed... the only real usage cap anywhere in this codebase is a DAILY cap... on exactly one function." This session's own task was scoped exactly against that same confirmed-true gap, not a stale assumption.
