- Confirmed how `is_premium`/`premium_until` are read: a flat `users.is_premium` boolean, checked with a plain `.select('is_premium')`, no special client pattern to match. `premium_until` (referral system, 2026-07-28) is an expiry on a temporary grant, extended in fixed 30-day increments by `grant_premium_days`, not a real subscription-renewal date, since no billing/IAP integration has ever existed in this codebase for any real recurring subscription. This mattered directly for sizing the pool's reset cycle, see the judgment call below.

**Built (migration `20260805000000_add_free_tier_caps_and_premium_pool.sql`):**
- `ai_function_caps` (config table, not exposed via PostgREST, read only from inside the two functions below): `generate-reply-draft` 3/day, `generate-activity-suggestions` 1/week, `generate-connection-analysis` 1/week, `organize-remember-entry` 2/week, `summarize-remember-timeline` 2/week. `generate-match-suggestions` is deliberately absent (unchanged, its own existing Fix #3 cap governs it, confirmed live still does, see Scenario 3 below) and so is `generate-personality-narrative` (handled as a special case in both functions, see below).
- `ai_usage_events` (one row per free-tier occurrence, or per personality-narrative retake regardless of tier), no client write policy, only `record_ai_usage` (SECURITY DEFINER) inserts, same convention `no_ghost_prompts`/`meetup_checkins` already established.
- `users.premium_pool_period_start` / `premium_pool_spent_usd` (numeric(10,6), real dollars not a call count), `ai_pool_ledger` (one row per pool-gated call, real audit trail, same shape as `ai_credit_ledger`).
- `get_ai_gate_status(p_user_id, p_function_name)`: the pre-flight, read-only gate every one of the 5 functions calls BEFORE ever reaching Anthropic, so a blocked call never spends real API cost. `auth.uid() = p_user_id` enforced inside the function itself (SECURITY DEFINER bypasses RLS, this check is what makes granting it directly to `authenticated` safe, same fix already applied to `try_consume_ai_credit` in `20260804000001`). `generate-personality-narrative`'s retake cap is checked first inside this same function and short-circuits before any tier/pool branch, since it applies identically to both tiers.
- `record_ai_usage(p_user_id, p_function_name, p_cost_usd)`: called only after a genuinely completed Anthropic response. For free tier and personality-narrative retakes (either tier), a plain occurrence log. For a premium pool-gated function, rolls the 30-day cycle if expired or never started, then adds the real cost.

**Judgment call, explicit rather than left implicit, per the task's own instruction: daily caps reset at UTC midnight, weekly caps use a rolling 7-day window, not a calendar week.** Daily: `date_trunc('day', now() at time zone 'utc')`, matching `generate-match-suggestions`' own existing `suggested_date` convention exactly (`new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` is also a UTC calendar day, confirmed by reading that function directly rather than assumed). Weekly: `now() - interval '7 days'`, matching this codebase's own established elapsed-time convention for "roughly a week" features (`meetup_checkins`' 168-hour threshold, `no_ghost_prompts`' hour-banded reminders) rather than inventing a new ISO-calendar-week concept that exists nowhere else in this app.

**Second judgment call, also explicit: the premium pool's "monthly" cycle is a rolling 30 days from the account's own first pool-gated call, not a calendar month or an alignment to any real billing date.** The task's own framing ("align with however subscription renewal is tracked") presupposes a real recurring-subscription anchor that doesn't exist in this codebase, confirmed in discovery: `premium_until` is a plain expiry extended in 30-day chunks by referral grants, not a subscription date, and there is still no billing/IAP integration anywhere (re-confirmed, unchanged from every prior session that's noted this). A rolling window from first real usage is the simplest scheme that also satisfies the given "mid-cycle upgrade" test case for free, by construction: a newly-premium account's first pool-gated call just starts its own fresh cycle, there's nothing to reconcile against a stale free-tier cap.

**Third judgment call: the $3.00 pool size uses Claude Sonnet 5's standard per-token rate ($3.00/1M input, $15.00/1M output), not the temporary intro rate ($2.00/$10.00) active through 2026-08-31.** The intro rate expires in about a month, using it to size a supposedly-permanent monthly quota would make the pool's real-dollar meaning silently wrong the moment the intro period ends. Documented here rather than picked silently.

**Fourth judgment call, `generate-activity-suggestions` specifically, the one function with an existing reserve-pool architecture (F23's `activity_suggestion_pool`, roughly 1-in-3 taps costs a real Claude call, 2-in-3 read an already-generated set instantly):** free tier's 1/week cap gates the WHOLE feature, checked before even the instant-reserve fast path, matching the given spec literally ("1/week" with no carve-out for the reserve mechanism). Premium's pool gate is deliberately NOT checked at that same point, only immediately before the one real Claude call this function ever makes (after a reserve-fast-path miss), because gating an instant, zero-cost reserved-batch read against real dollar spend would repeat the exact "spend for zero deliverable value" bug the 2026-07-29 consumable-credits session already found and fixed once for `generate-match-suggestions`. This is a real, flagged tension between the literal free-tier spec (which does throttle the existing "tap for another idea" browsing pattern for free users, not just protect Claude cost) and this feature's own established UX, not silently resolved either way, flagged here the same way this file has flagged prior spec-vs-precedent conflicts (F23's own parallel-hangout note) rather than picking a side.

**Client-side blocked-state integration point:** every one of the 5 functions returns a deliberate HTTP 200 `{blocked: true, reason, tier, resetsAt, poolSpentUsd, poolCapUsd, message}` on a gate rejection, matching this app's own established convention (`generate-reply-draft`'s too-long-input and truncation cases) for a clean, distinguishable branch rather than a generic error. `message` is calm, non-punitive copy naming the real reason, no shaming, no aggressive upsell language, gesturing at "upgrade" for free tier and "a top-up option will be available soon" for premium, matching the task's own instruction not to build the actual purchase mechanism here. Wired through every real client call site's own existing error-display path rather than a new UI pattern: `UniversalTextBox`'s existing `DraftServiceError` (covers chat compose, profile fields, no-ghost drafts, `ReplyAssistPanel`, `ConversationFlowPromptCard`, all real callers of `generate-reply-draft`), `src/lib/remember.ts`'s existing thrown-`Error`-with-server-message pattern (`organize-remember-entry`, `summarize-remember-timeline`), and a new `errorMessage` state in `ActivitySuggestionsModal` plus a `data?.blocked` branch in `ConnectionAnalysisSheet` (both previously only had a fixed generic failure string, now show the real reason first). `generate-personality-narrative`'s three real call sites (`big-five-assessment.tsx`, the Profile tab's `openReflection`, `FriendshipExperienceModal`) each now send an explicit `context: 'onboarding' | 'retake'` field and check `data?.blocked` before the existing generic-fallback catch.

**Tested, all required scenarios, live against the real database via RLS-impersonated RPC calls (the established pattern for backend-logic-only features in this project, e.g. Fix #3's own capacity tests), one real seed account (+15555500109), fully restored afterward, not assumed correct from reading code alone:**
- **Scenario 1, free-tier daily cap blocks and resets at the right UTC boundary:** recorded 3 `generate-reply-draft` events, confirmed the gate then returns `allowed: false, reason: "daily_cap_reached"`, `resets_at` correctly the next UTC midnight (cross-checked against the database's own real `now()`, confirming the reset boundary is genuinely UTC-anchored, not local-time or approximate).
- **Scenario 2, free-tier weekly cap uses a rolling 7-day window, not a calendar week:** recorded 2 `organize-remember-entry` events, confirmed `allowed: false, reason: "weekly_cap_reached"`. Backdated both events 8 days (past the rolling window) via a direct admin update, confirmed the gate then genuinely reopens (`allowed: true`), proving the window is real elapsed time, not a fixed calendar-week reset.
- **Scenario 3, `generate-match-suggestions` is completely unaffected:** with the same test account already blocked on two other functions' caps, confirmed `get_ai_gate_status` for `generate-match-suggestions` (absent from `ai_function_caps` by design) always returns `allowed: true`, proving this new system never touches its own separate, existing Fix #3 cap.
- **Scenario 4, premium pool tracks real cumulative cost across multiple different functions, not one function repeated:** flipped the account premium, recorded $1.00 (`organize-remember-entry`) then $1.50 (`summarize-remember-timeline`), checked the running total via a THIRD function's own gate call (`generate-connection-analysis`, confirmed `pool_spent_usd: 2.5`), then recorded $1.00 more via that third function and checked exhaustion via a FOURTH function (`generate-reply-draft`, confirmed `pool_spent_usd: 3.5, allowed: false, reason: "pool_exhausted"`). Directly queried `ai_pool_ledger` and confirmed three real rows, one per function, summing exactly to the users-row total, a genuine cross-function shared pool, not a per-function counter mistaken for one.
- **Scenario 5, a mid-cycle free-to-premium upgrade gets pool-based limits going forward, not a leftover free-tier cap:** with the account already blocked on `generate-reply-draft`'s free daily cap, flipped `is_premium = true` and re-checked the same function's gate: immediately `allowed: true, tier: "premium", pool_spent_usd: 0`, a fresh pool, zero carryover from the exhausted free-tier state.
- **Scenario 6, `generate-personality-narrative`'s retake cap is genuinely separate from the pool, for both tiers:** recorded one retake while the account was free, confirmed blocked (`tier: "free", reason: "daily_cap_reached"`). After the same account became premium (Scenario 5) and its pool was later exhausted (Scenario 4), re-checked the retake gate: still `allowed: false, reason: "daily_cap_reached", tier: "premium"`, not `"pool_exhausted"`, proving the 1/day retake cap and the dollar pool are two fully independent mechanisms, neither leaking into the other.
- **Onboarding's one-time call:** verified via direct code trace rather than a live call (no real `DEV_SESSION_SECRET` value was available in this session to mint a fresh JWT for an end-to-end HTTP call): `generate-personality-narrative`'s gate/record calls are both wrapped in `if (context === 'retake')`, structurally unreachable for `context: 'onboarding'`, confirmed by reading the deployed source directly.
- All test mutations (6 `ai_usage_events` inserts across 2 functions after backdating, 3 `ai_pool_ledger` inserts, `is_premium`, `premium_pool_period_start`, `premium_pool_spent_usd`) fully reverted afterward and independently reconfirmed via requery: `is_premium: false`, pool columns back to null/0, zero remaining `ai_usage_events`/`ai_pool_ledger` rows for the test account.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean across the whole app after every client-side edit. No em dashes in any new code (the two matches in `generate-reply-draft`'s own existing prompt text, unchanged, are the literal instruction telling Claude not to use em dashes, not new copy). All 6 touched Edge Functions (`generate-reply-draft`, `organize-remember-entry`, `summarize-remember-timeline`, `generate-connection-analysis`, `generate-activity-suggestions`, `generate-personality-narrative`) redeployed. Migration applied via `supabase db query --linked --file` and independently re-queried against `information_schema` to confirm the new tables/columns/functions and the 5 config rows all landed correctly.

**Not built, deliberately out of scope, matching the task's own instruction:** the actual top-up purchase mechanism for a premium account's exhausted pool (the blocked-state response and every client surface are wired with a clean integration point, `tier`/`reason`/`poolSpentUsd`/`poolCapUsd` all available at the point of failure for a future purchase flow to hook into, same `ai_credits`/`validate-ai-credit-purchase` infrastructure the 2026-07-29 consumable-credits session already built could plausibly extend this pool the same way it already extends `generate-match-suggestions`' own daily cap, not attempted here). Whether a free user's reserve-pool "another idea" browsing pattern on `generate-activity-suggestions` should really cost against the 1/week cap (flagged above) is a genuine open product question, not decided in this session.

### Session, July 30, 2026 (feature: consumable AI credit top-up, wired into the caps/pool system; plus a reserve-pool explainer)

**Part A, reserve-pool explainer (no code changed).** Asked separately to explain `generate-activity-suggestions`' existing reserve mechanism before deciding whether the new 1/week free-tier cap on it (built the prior session) is right. Answered directly from the code: `SET_COUNT = 3` sets are generated in one Claude call, one served immediately, two banked in `activity_suggestion_pool` as a reserve; the next 2 taps for that connection read the bank instantly (zero cost), the 4th tap triggers another real generation. So real Claude cost is genuinely amortized 1-in-3, and the whole mechanism exists specifically to make *repeated* taps (browsing a few alternate ideas in one sitting) cheap. Flagged the real tension this creates with a flat 1/week free-tier gate checked before even the reserve read (as built): a free user's first tap in a week consumes the entire week's allowance regardless of whether it hit the free reserve or the costly generation, which throttles the exact "browse a few ideas" pattern the reserve was built to make free. Not resolved either way, left for the user to decide; no code changed as part of this explainer.

**Part B, consumable credits, discovery first, per the task's own "likely greenfield, confirm don't assume" instruction, and it mattered.** This was NOT greenfield. Grepped and read directly rather than trusting the task's own framing: `users.ai_credits`, `ai_credit_purchases`, `ai_credit_ledger`, `try_consume_ai_credit()` (self-checked, already granted to `authenticated`), `credit_ai_purchase()` (service-role only, called after real validation), and a full `validate-ai-credit-purchase` Edge Function doing genuine server-side Apple `verifyReceipt` / Google Android Publisher API validation, one product (`ai_credits_50`, $1.99 for 50 credits), idempotent on `transaction_id`, fails closed with no configured platform secret, were ALL already built and deployed the same day as the caps/pool session (2026-07-29, migrations `20260804000000`/`20260804000001`). `react-native-iap@12.15.6` is already a real dependency, `src/lib/ai-credits.ts`'s `purchaseAiCreditPack()` already implements the full client-side purchase flow correctly (never trusts `RNIap`'s own success callback, only finishes the platform transaction after server confirmation), and `home.tsx` already has a real, working "Get 50 AI credits for $1.99" purchase card wired to `generate-match-suggestions`' own daily-cap block.

**The real, confirmed gap:** `try_consume_ai_credit` was never called from anywhere except `generate-match-suggestions`' own inline logic. The 5 free-tier-capped functions and the premium pool (`get_ai_gate_status`/`record_ai_usage`, built the prior session, same day as the credits system but never connected to it) and `generate-personality-narrative`'s separate retake cap had no credit fallback at all. This session's real task was closing that one gap, not rebuilding anything that already existed.

**Built (migration `20260806000000_wire_ai_credits_into_gate_status.sql`):** extended `get_ai_gate_status` itself rather than duplicating "check credits" logic into all 6 Edge Functions separately. Every branch that would otherwise return `allowed: false` (the personality-narrative retake cap, a free-tier per-function cap, the premium pool) now first attempts `try_consume_ai_credit(p_user_id, '<function>:<reason>')` for that same user, and only falls through to the real blocked response if that also fails (zero credits). On success, returns `allowed: true, usedCredit: true, aiCredits: <new balance>` instead. Because all 6 Edge Functions already call this one function and already branch on `gate.allowed === false`, this required editing exactly one SQL function and zero Edge Function logic changes for the actual unblocking behavior. A consumed credit funds exactly one extra action past whatever blocked it, the underlying cap counter or pool total is untouched (the caller's own `record_ai_usage` still runs its normal accounting afterward), so a second blocked request needs a second credit, matching `generate-match-suggestions`' own already-established "one credit buys one extra action beyond the cap" semantics exactly, not a new, different rule.

**Client-side, real purchase wiring added to one representative, highest-leverage surface, not duplicated across every UI:** `UniversalTextBox` (the shared component behind `generate-reply-draft` across chat compose, profile fields, no-ghost drafts, `ReplyAssistPanel`, and `ConversationFlowPromptCard`, by far the most call sites of any of the 6 functions) gained a real "Get 50 AI credits for $1.99" button, shown only when the block was genuinely a credit-exhausted cap/pool response (a new `CreditBlockedError` subclass of the existing `DraftServiceError`, thrown only for `data?.blocked`, distinguishing it from any other server message like "too long" or "truncated" so the button never shows for the wrong kind of error). Tapping it calls the exact same, already-built `purchaseAiCreditPack()` `home.tsx` already uses, no second purchase implementation, and on a successful purchase automatically retries whichever action (draft or cleanup) was actually blocked, rather than making the user notice and re-tap by hand, matching the given spec's "credit purchase immediately unblocks." The other 4 functions' surfaces (`ActivitySuggestionsModal`, `ConnectionAnalysisSheet`, the Remember composer/timeline via `src/lib/remember.ts`) show the same accurate, now-real "buy 50 credits" message text (updated from the prior session's "a top-up option will be available soon" placeholder, since the mechanism is real now) but do not yet have their own inline purchase button, the same kind of scoping decision this project already made once (`home.tsx` being the only built-out purchase surface for `generate-match-suggestions`, not duplicated across Browse/Say-Hi/candidate profile).

**Tested, live against the real database via RLS-impersonated RPC calls (the same test account, +15555500109, fully restored afterward), the two scenarios that are genuinely new in this session:**
- **Scenario 1, free user hits the daily cap, blocked with 0 credits, purchase immediately unblocks and decrements correctly:** recorded 3 `generate-reply-draft` events, confirmed the gate blocked (`allowed: false, reason: "daily_cap_reached"`, no `usedCredit`). Granted 50 credits via `credit_ai_purchase` (simulating a real validated purchase, the same call `validate-ai-credit-purchase` itself makes after Apple/Google confirm a receipt). Re-checked the gate: `allowed: true, tier: "free", usedCredit: true, aiCredits: 49`, and confirmed via `ai_credit_ledger` a real `-1` row with reason `"generate-reply-draft:day_cap"`, a genuine, function-specific audit trail, not a generic entry.
- **Scenario 2, premium user exhausts the monthly pool, same unblock flow:** flipped the account premium with `premium_pool_spent_usd = 3.50` (over the $3.00 cap). Gate for a different function (`organize-remember-entry`) correctly blocked at first, then, using the 49 credits still on the account from Scenario 1, correctly unblocked: `allowed: true, tier: "premium", usedCredit: true, aiCredits: 48`, `pool_spent_usd` unchanged at `3.5` (confirming a credit funds one specific extra action, it does not refill or reset the exhausted pool).
- **Zero-credits case, confirmed genuinely blocked with no fallback:** set `ai_credits = 0` directly, re-checked the same premium-pool-exhausted gate, confirmed `allowed: false, reason: "pool_exhausted"`, no `usedCredit`, proving the credit fallback only ever fires when a real credit is actually available, never silently.
- **Scenarios 3/4 (failed/cancelled purchase does not credit; receipt validation genuinely happens server-side), honestly not independently re-run this session:** both were already proven live in the 2026-07-29 session that built `validate-ai-credit-purchase` (a real HTTP 402 on garbage receipt data with `ai_credit_purchases.status = 'invalid'` and `credits_granted: null`; a real outbound call to Apple's own servers returning Apple's genuine `"status 21002"` malformed-receipt error). That function's logic was not touched by this session, only `get_ai_gate_status` and message copy in the 6 caller functions changed, so that prior verification remains valid and current. This session did not have a way to mint a fresh user JWT to independently re-exercise the actual HTTP call (no `DEV_SESSION_SECRET` value available in this context, the same standing limitation this file has hit before), flagged honestly rather than assumed re-verified.
- All test mutations (3 `ai_usage_events`, 1 `ai_credit_ledger` entry beyond the purchase grant, `is_premium`, `premium_pool_period_start`, `premium_pool_spent_usd`, `ai_credits`) fully reverted afterward and independently reconfirmed via requery.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean after every client-side edit. No em dashes in any new/edited code (all matches in the 6 redeployed functions are the pre-existing strip-regex pattern, unchanged). All 6 Edge Functions redeployed (message-copy changes only, no logic changes beyond what migration `20260806000000` already handles at the SQL layer). Migration applied via `supabase db query --linked --file` and confirmed live via the RPC tests above, not assumed correct from reading the SQL alone.

**Not built, deliberately out of scope:** a dedicated purchase button on the other 4 functions' own UI surfaces (flagged above as a scoping decision, not an oversight, the message copy alone already tells the user a top-up is real and available). No changes to `validate-ai-credit-purchase`, `credit_ai_purchase`, or the client purchase flow itself, all already correct and unchanged. The `generate-activity-suggestions` 1/week-vs-reserve-pool tension from Part A remains unresolved, a decision for the user, not for this session.

### Session, July 30, 2026 (three fixes from the code audit: batch photo-gate, first-message photo requirement, chat compose credit-block wiring; plus a `current_situation` investigation)

A full 5-part code-verified audit of the app was produced the same day (page inventory, system status, every profile field classified, AI-reflection inputs, no-ghost timing), surfacing three real, previously undocumented findings. This session fixes all three and investigates a fourth item without building anything for it.

**Fix 1, `compatible_candidates_for` was missing the photo-gate filter the rest of the app already enforces.** `discovery_profiles` and `browse_profiles` have required `photo_url is not null` since `20260730000000`, but the service-role batch function behind the 6-hourly `refresh-match-suggestions` cron (`compatible_candidates_for`) never had that condition, confirmed via `pg_get_functiondef` before touching anything. Added `and p.photo_url is not null` to its WHERE clause (migration `20260807000000`), same return columns, no signature change. **Verified live:** confirmed Aisha Bello (a real, established compatible match for Maria Santos) is present in `compatible_candidates_for(maria_id)` by default, disappears the instant her `photo_url` is nulled, and reappears the instant it's restored.

**New rule, built: a photo is required to send the first message of a brand-new conversation, not to reply.** Two layers, matching this app's own established pattern (Report & Block) of never relying on a client-side hide alone for anything consequential:
- **Real enforcement, at the RLS layer** (same migration `20260807000000`): the messages INSERT policy now additionally requires either that the connection already has at least one message, or that the sender's own `profiles.photo_url` is not null. **A real bug found and fixed while building this, not part of the original ask:** the first version wrote the "does this connection already have a message" check as a direct correlated subquery against `messages` from inside `messages`' own INSERT policy, which triggers Postgres's row-security evaluation recursively (`42P17: infinite recursion detected in policy for relation "messages"`), caught immediately while testing, before it ever reached a real account. Fixed by pulling that one check into a small `SECURITY DEFINER` function (`connection_has_any_message`), the same "bypass RLS from inside a narrow, purpose-built function" pattern this app already uses for `big_five_proximity_score` and `compatible_candidates_for` itself.
- **Proactive UI gate** (`src/app/thread/[id].tsx`): the user's own `profiles.photo_url` is fetched once on thread load alongside the other initial reads. When the connection has zero messages and the viewer has no photo, the entire compose footer, including "Let's plan something" (itself a real path to a message send via `ActivitySuggestionsModal`, not just the text field) is replaced by a plain, non-dismissible inline explanation plus an "Add a photo" link into `/profile-build?from=profile`, mirroring the exact "replace the footer, don't overlay a banner" pattern this same file already uses for the `blocked` connection state.

**Verified live, both layers, real seed accounts, all test data restored afterward:**
- **RLS backstop** (isolated temp connection, Robert Kim ↔ Priya Nair, cleaned up after): Robert (photo nulled) attempting the first message failed with a real `42501` RLS rejection; Priya (has a photo) sending that real first message succeeded; Robert, still with no photo, replying afterward succeeded, confirming the gate only ever applies to a connection's genuine first message, never to a reply.
- **UI gate**, via a real Playwright session against the actual dev server, signed in as Robert Kim through the Dev tab's own account switcher (not a shortcut): with no photo and zero messages, the thread rendered the inline "Add a profile photo before sending your first message here..." explanation with an "Add a photo" link, and confirmed "Let's plan something" and the compose field/Send button were genuinely absent from the page, not just visually hidden. Restored Robert's photo and reloaded the same thread: normal compose (Let's plan something, the message field, Send, Help me write) rendered correctly instead.

**Fix 3, the chat compose box's credit-blocked bug (surfaced in the same day's audit) is fixed.** `thread/[id].tsx`'s `requestComposeDraft` (the custom `onRequestDraft` passed to `UniversalTextBox` for the main chat compose surface) checked `data?.error` but never `data?.blocked`, so a real cap/pool block on this specific surface fell through to a generic `"No draft returned"` error instead of `UniversalTextBox`'s own `CreditBlockedError` handling (the calm blocked message plus the working "Get 50 AI credits" button, already correct on every other AI-assist surface in the app since the 2026-07-29 credits-wiring session). Fixed with a one-line addition, `if (data?.blocked) throw new CreditBlockedError(data.message as string);`, placed before the existing `data?.error` check, matching the exact order `UniversalTextBox`'s own internal `requestDraft` already uses.

**Verified live:** exhausted Aisha Bello's real `generate-reply-draft` daily cap (3 synthetic `ai_usage_events` rows), signed in as her via the Dev tab, opened her real thread with Elena Torres (an active conversation with existing messages, so the new photo gate above doesn't interfere), typed a situation into the compose field, tapped "Help me write" then "Draft it", and confirmed the real blocked message ("You've reached today's free limit for this, and you're out of extra credits too...") and the "Get 50 AI credits for $1.99" button both rendered, with no trace of the old generic "No draft returned" error. Test usage-event rows deleted afterward, confirmed zero remaining.

**Investigation only, not built: what `current_situation` was meant to capture.** Traced via the original migration comment, the seed data, and every reference in `generate-match-suggestions`/`profile.tsx`/`candidate/[id].tsx`, not guessed:
- The column's own addition comment (`20260712000002_add_current_situation.sql`) is explicit and unambiguous: *"What they are going through right now", a separate field from life_transition (a fixed 5-option category) and personal_statement (an intro/identity blurb). Meant to capture present-tense day-to-day reality.*
- The one seed-data pass that ever populated it (`seed-current-situation-and-personality.sql`) confirms that framing with real examples, all first-person and mundane rather than identity-level: *"Most weeks it is just me and a very needy cat, figuring out what my own version of a Tuesday night looks like now."* / *"Still working out how to fill an evening that used to be full without it feeling like something is missing."* / *"Rebuilding a whole new professional circle from scratch..."*
- It is displayed under the literal label **"Right now"** on both the user's own Profile tab and Other User Profile, shown only when populated, confirmed by direct code read.
- It is genuinely read into matching-adjacent logic: `generate-match-suggestions` fetches it for the Candidate side and folds it into the dealbreaker-conflict searchable text (line-confirmed this session, not assumed), so a populated value could, in principle, contribute to excluding a candidate via a viewer's stated dealbreakers, exactly like `personal_statement`.
- **What's genuinely unclear, and can't be resolved from this repo alone:** whether an input UI for it was ever built and later silently dropped during one of `profile-build.tsx`'s many rewrites (location, communication style, the 10-step pagination, etc.), or whether it was always meant to be entered by some other means (an admin/support tool, a future onboarding step) that never shipped. This repo's entire history lives in one squashed "Initial commit" (confirmed via `git log`), so there is no git blame or diff history to distinguish "built then removed" from "never built" — this is a real, honest dead end, not something worth guessing at further. Whatever the original plan, the field is real, wired into display and matching, and permanently null for every current account until some UI writes to it.

**Verified (all three fixes):** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new code. Migration `20260807000000` applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`. Temporary Playwright test scripts (run from inside the project directory so `require('playwright')` resolves, per this file's own established convention) and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) were both removed after verification; confirmed via `git diff` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` carry no trace of it (both files' diffs are the same pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted changes present before this session started).

**Not built:** any fix or decision for `current_situation` itself (explicitly investigation-only, per instruction). A dedicated Dev-tab UI section for the photo-gate rule (tested via direct RLS impersonation and a real Playwright session instead, the same rigor this file has used for every prior backend-and-UI combination fix).

### Session, July 30, 2026 (eight-item UI/content review: question overlap, etiquette format re-check, photo copy, purchase error, pets exclusivity, Clean Up consistency, vertical alignment, referral copy precision)

**Item 1, investigation only, no code changed: Big Five / profile-build / friendship-experience question overlap.** Read all three question sources directly (`big-five-assessment.tsx`'s 10 `QUESTIONS`, `profile-build.tsx`'s 2 "Required for matching" scenario questions, `friendship-experience.ts`'s 5 `EXPERIENCE_QUESTIONS`) and compared every prompt side by side.

- **The confirmed candidate holds up: Big Five Q6 ("When you disagree with someone, you usually...", 5-point scale, `agreeableness` trait) and profile-build's `communication_style_expression` ("A friend does something that bothers you. What do you most naturally do?", 3 discrete options) genuinely ask the same underlying question, conflict-avoidance vs. directness, from two different screens with different granularity.** Their backend roles differ, but not in a way that erases the redundancy: Q6's answer is one of two items averaged into an aggregate `agreeableness` trait, which feeds `big_five_proximity_score()` (a distance-tiered bonus across all 5 traits combined, max 15 pts) and is quoted verbatim as one of 10 Q&A pairs in the AI reflection's "connection" section. `communication_style_expression` is its own standalone, directly-weighted matching field (part of the 5-pt `communicationStyleScore` bucket, paired with `communication_style_openness`) and is separately quoted in the same reflection section. Functionally different (aggregate trait input vs. discrete scoring field), but a real user answering both back to back would reasonably notice they're being asked the same thing twice. **Recommendation: reword for clear distinction, don't consolidate.** Consolidating would mean restructuring either the Big Five instrument or the standalone scoring field, a bigger change than this review asked for. The lower-risk fix is rewording one of the two prompts so they read as asking about different things, for example narrowing Big Five Q6 to a broader/longer-arc framing ("Over time, when something bothers you about a friendship...") versus profile-build's already-specific single-incident framing ("A friend does something that bothers you").
- **No other pairing rose to the same level of duplication.** Big Five Q5 ("A friend is going through something hard, your instinct is to...") and friendship-experience's `afterPositiveMeetupBehavior` ("After a good first meetup, what are you most likely to do?") both touch reaching-out timing, but from genuinely different triggers (a friend in need vs. a specific positive first meetup) and different data uses (aggregate trait vs. one-time narrative-only atom) — thematically adjacent, not redundant, the same way Big Five's own Q1/Q2 (both extraversion) aren't redundant with each other despite covering similar ground. Every other pairing checked (conscientiousness/scheduling questions vs. `meeting_freq`/`response_time`; `communication_style_openness` vs. `afterPositiveMeetupBehavior`) showed the same pattern: adjacent domain, different question shape and different backend role. **Recommendation for everything else: leave as-is.**

**Item 2, re-verified directly, the earlier audit claim was correct, not the "appears to be wrong" read.** Read `etiquette-modules.tsx` and its render logic fresh. Each module does show exactly one scenario prompt (`module.scenario.prompt`) per screen, which is likely why it reads as "a single question" at a glance — but structurally this is a **two-choice judgment scenario** (`module.scenario.choices`, right answer gets brief reinforcement, wrong answer gets a perspective-shift explanation), not blueprint's required **comprehension-question** format (a question testing whether the user understood specific video content). The other half of blueprint's requirement, captions/transcript/replay controls on a real video, is also still entirely absent: `VideoPlaceholder` is a placeholder, there is no real video anywhere in this app to caption or transcribe. AGENTS.md's F7 note stands correct as written; no correction made.

**Item 3, fixed: outdated photo-requirement copy.** `profile-build.tsx`'s photo-section warning only mentioned Discover/Browse visibility, predating this session's own first-message photo requirement (see the entry above). Updated to: *"Without a photo, you will not appear in Discover or Browse for other people, and you will not be able to send the first message in a new conversation."* **Verified live:** temporarily nulled a real seed account's (David Chen) photo, confirmed the updated sentence renders in `profile-build.tsx`'s edit mode, restored his photo and real `activity_interests` afterward (see Item 5's cleanup note below, both mutations were reverted in the same restore pass).

**Item 4, investigated, confirmed expected, not a bug, no code changed.** The generic "Something went wrong with that purchase. Try again." message on tapping "Get 50 AI credits" in this dev/test environment (web) is expected, not new. `react-native-iap` ships zero web-specific files and no `browser` field in its own `package.json` (confirmed directly), it is a pure native-modules library with no web or Expo Go equivalent, a limitation `ai-credits.ts`'s own header comment already documents. `purchaseAiCreditPack()` calls `RNIap.initConnection()` before its own try/catch begins, so on web that call's failure propagates uncaught out of the function; `UniversalTextBox`'s `handleBuyCredits()` wraps the whole call in a try/catch with exactly this generic fallback message, which is what's actually firing. **Verified live, not just reasoned about:** exhausted a real account's daily cap, reached the blocked state, tapped "Get 50 AI credits for $1.99," and reproduced the exact reported message with no other visible error (confirming it's a caught exception, not an uncaught crash). Not built, out of scope for this pass: a web-specific short-circuit message (e.g. detecting `Platform.OS === 'web'` and showing "In-app purchases aren't available in this preview" before ever calling RNIap) would be more honest during testing, flagged as an optional future enhancement, not requested as a fix here.

**Item 5, fixed: pets selection mutual exclusivity.** `toggleActivityChip()` in `profile-build.tsx` is the one generic handler behind every multi-select chip question across all 40 activity categories; added a scoped special case for `categoryKey === 'pets' && field === 'types'` only: selecting "No pets" clears every other selected option and selects only itself; selecting any real pet type clears "No pets" if present. No other category has an equivalent exclusivity rule, so this was deliberately not generalized into the shared toggle logic. **Verified live:** selected Dogs and Cats, confirmed both filled; tapped "No pets," confirmed both cleared and only "No pets" remained selected; tapped "Fish," confirmed "No pets" cleared and only "Fish" remained. Real, unintended side effects of driving this through the live UI were caught and reverted: the "Pets" category chip itself and its nested `{types: [...]}` detail got written to David Chen's real `activity_interests` (this project's own real seed account, not throwaway data) across the test; comparing against his other four categories' rich, clearly seed-authored detail content made it evident "pets" was never one of his original categories, so it and its detail were fully removed from his `activity_interests.categories`/`.details` afterward, restoring the exact 4-category state his three genuine categories (reading, games, golf, fitness) show, re-verified via requery.

**Item 6, investigated, confirmed deliberate and already consistent, no code changed.** Checked every "Add your own" free-text field in `profile-build.tsx` (`lifeTransitionsOther`, `valuesOther`, `activityOther`, `languagesOther`, `ethnicityOther`): all five are plain single-line `TextInput`s with no `UniversalTextBox`, confirmed by grep (only two `UniversalTextBox` instances exist in the whole file). Both instances that do have it (`personalStatement`/"About you", `dealbreakers`) are multi-line `VoiceTextInput` fields (`numberOfLines={3-4}`, a real min-height) explicitly meant for a few sentences of composed prose, where "Help me write"/"Clean up" add real value. The five without it are all short, single-line supplements to an already-provided closed chip list ("Add your own" placeholder, meant to add one missing tag, not compose a paragraph). This split is uniform across every field checked, not inconsistent even among the five short fields themselves. **No change made:** the existing behavior already correctly reflects "prose fields get AI assistance, tag-style supplements don't," confirmed rather than assumed.

**Item 7, fixed: vertical alignment.** Before changing anything, grepped for the app's actual layout conventions rather than assuming a single sweeping fix was warranted, and the evidence didn't support one. Two separate, each internally consistent, area-specific conventions already exist: 11 onboarding screens share an identical `flex-1 justify-between px-6 py-10` structure (header / content / footer, space distributed evenly), and 6 of 7 tab screens share an identical single-`ScrollView`-with-`pt-10` structure (header and content both inside one scrollable container). Given that, a wholesale app-wide redesign to a third, new "fixed distance below header" standard would have meant touching roughly 17 already-consistent, correctly-working screens to fix a problem that, on direct inspection, only exists in exactly two places:
- **`gender-identity.tsx`'s Step 1** was the one screen in all of onboarding that broke its own screen's convention: Steps 0 and 2 use a plain `View` for their content (sizes to content, lets `justify-between` distribute space evenly, the "nice breathing room" look), Step 1 alone used a `ScrollView` for the same slot, which expands to fill the entire middle flex space and top-aligns its content by default, producing the reported "jammed to the top" look, confirmed by screenshot before touching any code. **Fixed** by restructuring the screen to wrap all three steps in one shared, always-present `ScrollView` (top-anchored with a fixed `pt-6` gap below the header row, still scrollable if content ever overflows), so every step renders identically regardless of content length, matching the rest of onboarding's own established look rather than inventing a new one.
- **`browse.tsx`'s results area** was the one tab screen that split its header (own `View`, correctly `pt-10`) from its results (a separate `ScrollView` with `pb-10` but no top padding at all), unlike every sibling tab's single unified, `pt-10`-padded container. **Fixed** with a `pt-4` addition to the results `ScrollView`'s content container, restoring comparable breathing room beneath the filter-chip row without duplicating the chip row's own existing `py-4`, a full `pt-10` there would have been redundant on top of it.

**Verified live, both fixes, via a real Playwright session:** gender-identity's three steps now start at an identical, fixed offset below the header row regardless of step, screenshotted before and after; Browse's results now show a visibly larger, consistent gap beneath the filter chips, screenshotted before and after with a real populated result set (Elena Torres, 2 real compatible candidates). **Not done:** converting the other ~15 already-consistent onboarding/tab screens to a new standard, a real, larger, unrequested-by-evidence redesign; flagged here rather than done silently, worth a deliberate decision if the two established conventions themselves are ever felt to be wrong, not just the two screens that broke their own.

**Item 8, defined precisely and fixed: referral "active" copy.** Read the live `referral_reward_qualifies(p_user_id)` function directly rather than trusting the earlier session's own prose summary. The exact logic: (1) the referred account must have completed onboarding (`behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at is not null`); (2) at least 30 full days must have passed since real signup (`auth.users.created_at`); (3) `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` must be non-null and fall on or after `signup_at + 27 days` (i.e., a real sign-in at some point in the final 3 days of the 30-day window, or any time after, not a snapshot check at exactly day 30, and not satisfied by a sign-in confined to only the first days after signup). **A real, honest gap found while implementing this: the copy given to use didn't exist anywhere in the app yet.** Neither `profile-basics.tsx`'s referral-code field nor `profile.tsx`'s "Invite a friend" row had ever had reward-explanation copy at all, confirmed by direct read, both are new additions this session, not edits to stale existing text. Added, replacing "active" with a concrete, plain-language description of what's actually checked (a real sign-in, in the final few days of the 30-day window, not just once at the start), deliberately not hardcoding the exact "3 days" figure in permanent UI copy since that window is documented in the RPC's own comment as an undecided judgment call that could be retuned later:
- Redemption field: *"Once you've both been using Limen for about 30 days, and you're each still signing in during the final few days of that window (not just when you first joined), you'll each get 30 days of Premium, free."*
- Share-code row: *"Once your friend joins and is still signing in during the final few days of their first 30 days, you'll both get 30 days of Premium, free. This applies to your first successful referral."*

**Verified (all eight items):** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout, checked after every edit and once more in the final state. No em dashes in any new code or copy. Temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily reinstalled Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) were removed after verification, confirmed via `git diff` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` show no trace of it beyond the same pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted changes present since before this session. The one real seed-account side effect from live UI testing (David Chen's photo and `activity_interests`) was identified and fully reverted, re-confirmed via requery, not just assumed clean.

**Not built:** items 1, 2, 4, and 6 were investigation-only by instruction or by finding (no genuine bug/gap to fix in 2, 4, or 6; item 1's recommendation is reword-don't-consolidate, left for a future pass to actually reword). The larger onboarding/tab-wide layout redesign flagged in Item 7 was deliberately not attempted without a separate, explicit decision to do so.

### Session, July 31, 2026 (two investigate-then-build changes: referral 14-distinct-day activity, communication_style_expression removal)

**Part 1, referral "active" logic rebuilt from a single last-sign-in snapshot to 14 distinct calendar days of real activity within the 30-day window.**

**Investigated first, as instructed, before writing anything.** The real question was whether this app has any usable login HISTORY (not just `auth.users.last_sign_in_at`, a single overwritten timestamp structurally incapable of counting distinct days). Checked both real GoTrue candidates live rather than assumed:
- `auth.audit_log_entries` (GoTrue's own audit trail, the theoretically "correct" place for this): queried directly, **zero rows exist in this project**, despite this session alone generating dozens of real sign-ins across multiple real calendar days. Whatever writes this table isn't populating it here, confirmed empirically, not just flagged as a risk.
- `auth.sessions` (one row per session grant, has a real `created_at`): genuinely populated, confirmed live against a real, heavily-reauthenticated seed account (Aisha Bello) showing distinct `created_at` timestamps across four real separate days. But it is **not durable**, GoTrue prunes expired/superseded sessions out of this table over time, a real seed account with many more real sign-ins today than the 4 rows still present proves pruning is actively happening. A day-3 sign-in's row is not guaranteed to still exist by day 30, unsuitable for a rolling 30-day count for the same underlying reason `last_sign_in_at` itself was.
- Both are also undocumented GoTrue implementation details Supabase doesn't contract to keep queryable or unpruned, not a foundation to build a real 30-day Premium grant on even where they happen to work today.

**Conclusion: neither existing option is real or accurate. Built a small, app-owned, durable log instead** (migration `20260808000000`):
- `public.referral_signin_days` (`user_id`, `active_date`, primary key on both — a natural, race-safe dedup key). Own-row SELECT RLS, **no client INSERT policy at all**: the only writer is `record_active_day()`, a SECURITY DEFINER RPC that takes zero parameters and computes the date itself (`(now() at time zone 'utc')::date`), so a client can never backdate or fast-forward its own distinct-day count, closing the obvious gaming vector for a feature that gates a real reward.
- `referral_reward_qualifies()` rewritten: onboarding-complete and the full-30-days-elapsed gates are unchanged, the single `last_sign_in_at` check is replaced entirely (not layered alongside) with `count(distinct active_date) >= 14` within `[signup_date, signup_date + 30]`.
- Client hook: `src/lib/referral.ts`'s `recordActiveDayIfSignedIn()` (checks for a real session first, no-ops if signed out), wired into `src/app/_layout.tsx`, the one component mounted for the app's entire lifetime.

**A real bug was caught live-testing the first version of the client hook, fixed before calling this done.** The first version called `recordActiveDayIfSignedIn()` once on mount with an empty dependency array. Live-tested by signing into a real seed account (Marcus Alvarez) through the actual Dev tab UI, checking the database afterward: **zero rows written.** Root cause: supabase-js restores a persisted session asynchronously, so a mount-time call can fire before that restore finishes and see no user; and because the effect never re-runs, a sign-in that happens *later* in the same JS session (finishing phone verification, switching accounts) never re-triggered it either, a real gap a one-shot mount call can't close. Fixed by driving this off `supabase.auth.onAuthStateChange` instead (fires once with the real session after startup hydration completes, and again on every real sign-in and token refresh), keeping the original `AppState` foreground listener as a second trigger for the narrower case that doesn't cover: a session already valid, sitting untouched in the background across a real calendar-day boundary with no new sign-in or refresh to fire an auth event. Re-tested the same way afterward: **one real row, correct date.**

**Verified live, the full threshold logic, real seed account (Priya Nair), fully restored afterward:** backdated her signup to 35 days ago (onboarding-complete flag set, window `[2026-06-26, 2026-07-26]`), confirmed `referral_reward_qualifies` returns `false` with 0 days recorded; inserted 13 distinct real dates scattered through the window, confirmed still `false` (one short); confirmed a duplicate-date insert attempt doesn't inflate the count past 13 (the primary-key dedup holding); added a 14th distinct date, confirmed `true`. Separately confirmed `record_active_day()` itself, called twice in the same request as Priya (RLS-impersonated), produces exactly one row for today, not two, proving the idempotency guarantee at the actual write path, not just the schema.

**Copy updated in both locations** (`profile-basics.tsx`'s redemption field, `profile.tsx`'s share-code row), replacing "active" with the concrete mechanic, deliberately not hardcoding a numeric grace-window figure that doesn't exist in this version of the logic anymore (14 is the real, load-bearing number now, so it's stated directly): *"...opened the app on at least 14 different days during that time (not all at once)..."* / *"...opens the app on at least 14 different days within their first 30 days..."*.

**Part 2, `communication_style_expression` removed entirely, per explicit request, investigated first.**

**Scoring impact, confirmed before touching anything:** `communicationStyleScore` gave 5 pts if both expression and openness matched, 2 pts if either alone matched, 0 if neither, capped at 5 pts out of the 100-point model. Dropped cleanly: rewritten to a plain binary match on openness alone (5 / 0), same 5-pt bucket weight preserved, **no rebalancing needed anywhere else in the formula**, the 100-point scale is unchanged.

**AI reflection impact, confirmed by direct read, not assumed:** `profileAtoms()` treats every field as an independent, optional `addSingle()` call; no atom-count minimum anywhere depends on any specific field being present (`isValidSection` only requires `supportedBy.length > 0`). Removing this one atom out of roughly 15-20 real candidates already available in the same category **degrades completely gracefully**, verified live (see below), not just reasoned about.

**`communication_style_openness` confirmed standing fully on its own:** its own independent `addSingle()` call, its own separate scoring consideration, its own separate `<Section>` in `profile-build.tsx` that never shared markup with expression, was never conditioned on expression's presence anywhere checked.

**Removed cleanly, no orphaned references** (migration `20260808000001` + code): dropped the check constraint and the `profiles.communication_style_expression` column itself; dropped and recreated `discovery_profiles`, `browse_profiles` (both views, column removal isn't a same-columns change `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` allows), and `compatible_candidates_for` (function, same reasoning, drop+create not replace) without it. Removed from `profile-build.tsx` (the whole question `<Section>`, its form-state field, default, `computeCompletionPct` check, `REQUIRED_FIELD_CHECKS` entry — the remaining openness question's required-field message renamed from "Communication style (question 2)" to just "Communication style" now that it's the only question in that step — load, and persist), `filter-options.ts` (both option/description exports, plus a stale comment reference), `profile.tsx` and `candidate/[id].tsx` (type, select query, and display, both rewritten from a two-field join to showing `communication_style_openness` alone), and both Edge Functions' types/select-queries/`profileAtoms()`. `generate-match-suggestions` and `generate-personality-narrative` both redeployed.

**Verified live, both functions, real seed accounts:** the Discover tab (Marcus Alvarez, via the real UI) loaded and called `generate-match-suggestions` with no error. `generate-personality-narrative` needed a real account with populated `big_five_scores` to meaningfully exercise, no seed account had one (only the real, off-limits non-seed account did), so Marcus's `big_five_scores` were temporarily populated with a full, realistic 10-question response set for this one test, restored to null immediately after: the reflection modal generated a complete, coherent "How you tend to connect" section correctly citing his remaining `communication_style_openness` data ("you tend to open up pretty early with new friends") with zero reference to the removed field and no error, confirming the graceful-degradation prediction rather than just trusting it. `profile-build.tsx`'s Communication Style step was also confirmed live to show only the openness question now, the expression question and its options fully gone from the rendered page.

**Verified (both parts):** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new code or copy. Both migrations applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`. Temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily reinstalled Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) removed after verification, confirmed via `git diff` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` carry no trace beyond the same pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted changes present since before this session. All real seed-account test mutations (Priya Nair's onboarding flag/signup date/active-day rows, Marcus Alvarez's `big_five_scores` and his own test-generated active-day row) were fully reverted afterward, re-confirmed via requery, not just assumed clean.

**Not built:** no changes to `check_and_grant_referral_reward_for`, `grant_premium_days`, or the daily `referral-reward-check`/`expire-premium-grants` crons, none of them needed to change, they call `referral_reward_qualifies` as an opaque boolean and don't know or care how it's computed internally.

### Session, July 31, 2026 (eight-item build: Settings, Premium, Public Profile Review, Report & Block gap check, Terms/Privacy, narrow fraud signals, analytics, current_situation removal)

Eight largely independent items, each with its own investigation and its own report, matching the request. All eight migrations (`20260809000000` through `20260810000003`) and the `delete-account`/`validate-premium-purchase` functions were applied/deployed live before any testing.

---

**Item 1 — Settings/Account page.** Confirmed genuinely unbuilt before this (no settings screen anywhere in `src/app/`, re-confirmed directly, matching the 2026-07-30 audit). Investigated notification preferences first, per instruction: no `expo-notifications` dependency, no push infrastructure anywhere in this codebase (a standing, repeatedly-documented gap), so the Notifications section is an honest static note rather than a fake toggle for a system that doesn't exist.

Built `src/app/settings.tsx`: Edit profile (a plain link into `/profile-build?from=profile`, the exact entry point the Profile tab's own Edit button already used, not rebuilt), a link into the new Public Profile Review, read-only Premium/billing status (`is_premium`/`premium_until`), blocked-accounts list with real unblock (new `unblock_user()` RPC and `my_blocks` view, migration `20260810000001`), "Reports you've filed" (new `my_reports` view over `reports`' own pre-existing self-row SELECT policy, confirmed live it already existed before writing anything), Terms/Privacy links, account deletion, and the referral share section relocated here from the Profile tab (removed from `profile.tsx`, which now just links to `/settings`).

`unblock_user()` only flips the underlying connection back to `'inactive'` (restoring messaging) once **neither** direction has an active block left, since `blocks` is unilateral per direction and both people could independently have blocked each other, a real correctness detail, not an oversight.

Account deletion (`delete-account`, new Edge Function): resolves the caller's own id from their own JWT via an anon-key client, never a client-supplied id, then uses a separate service-role client only for the one privileged call this needs (`admin.deleteUser`). A two-step confirm modal (warn, then a second explicit "Yes, permanently delete") gates it client-side.

**Verified live:** signed in as Aisha Bello (a real block on Elena Torres set up via `block_user()` for this test), confirmed the blocked list shows Elena, tapped Unblock through the real UI, confirmed she disappeared from the list and the underlying `blocks` row was gone. Signed in as David Chen (a real report against Marcus Alvarez filed via `file_report()` for this test), confirmed "Reports you've filed" correctly showed it. Confirmed the Premium section shows "Free" for a non-premium account and the referral share text/code renders correctly. Account deletion's `admin.deleteUser` call itself could not be driven through the real UI (`dev-create-session` is deliberately restricted to the 8 seed phone numbers by design, a documented security boundary this session did not touch), so the part that was actually at risk of being wrong — the FK cascade `delete-account`'s whole design depends on — was verified directly instead: created a real throwaway `auth.users` row with a profile and a connection, deleted the `auth.users` row directly (the same effect GoTrue's `admin.deleteUser` produces), confirmed `public.users`/`public.profiles`/`public.connections` all cascaded to zero. The function's own JWT-resolution logic (confirmed correct by direct code read, it's two lines) was not independently re-verified via a live token exchange, flagged honestly rather than claimed proven end to end. All test data (the block, the report, the throwaway account) fully reverted afterward, re-confirmed via requery.

---

**Item 2 — Premium/Upgrade screen.** Confirmed genuinely unbuilt: `users.is_premium`/`premium_until` existed already (Capacity System, Referral system) but only ever as a manually-set flag, no purchase screen, no payment webhook, re-confirmed absent this session.

Built using the exact proven pattern from the consumable AI credit packs (2026-07-29): real `react-native-iap` calls, real server-side receipt validation before ever touching `is_premium`. Checked the installed package's own type definitions before writing anything rather than assuming the one-time-purchase API and the subscription API have the same shape, they don't: iOS's subscription request is identical to a plain purchase request (`{sku}`), Android's requires a real `subscriptionOffers` array with an `offerToken` pulled from `getSubscriptions()`'s own response (Play Billing's offer system), not just a bare product id, confirmed by reading `RequestSubscriptionAndroid`'s real type definition.

New `validate-premium-purchase` Edge Function: Apple's receipt-verification endpoint is genuinely identical for a one-time purchase and a subscription (Apple's own documented behavior, already relied on correctly by the credits function), so that half is direct reuse. Google's subscription verification uses the Android Publisher API's `purchases.subscriptions` endpoint (not the `purchases.products` endpoint the credits function uses), checked via `expiryTimeMillis` being in the future rather than inferring from `paymentState`'s several documented edge cases. **Flagged honestly, not claimed proven:** the Google subscription path is structurally correct per Google's own published API shape but was not exercised against Google's real servers in this environment (no configured service account, matching the same standing gap the credits feature already has for both platforms).

New `credit_premium_purchase()` reuses `grant_premium_days()` as-is (built for the referral system) rather than duplicating its "extend from the later of now or existing expiry" logic. **Real, disclosed limitation:** no App Store Server Notifications / Play RTDN webhook receiver exists, so a renewal that happens while the user never reopens the app won't be recorded here; `restorePremiumPurchase()` (a real `getAvailablePurchases()` call, distinct from a fresh purchase) re-syncs on next app open specifically to mitigate this, not to fully solve it.

Screen shows the real $5.99/month price, the 2/4/5-free vs. 5/8/8-premium comparison (the Capacity System's real numbers), explicit "compatibility ranking never changes with Premium" copy (restating the forbidden-advantage rule as plain copy, not re-implementing it), and a real Restore purchase action.

**Verified live:** the screen loads, shows the comparison table and price correctly for a non-premium account. **Cannot be verified end to end in this environment**, same standing limitation already documented for the consumable credit packs: `react-native-iap` requires a real native dev build, no web or Expo Go equivalent exists, so the actual purchase sheet and the Google-subscription-specific validation path are both unexercised here.

Report/Block/Edit are absent by construction, not hidden conditionally: this is a preview of the viewer's own profile, there's nothing here to report or block, and Edit is one tap away via Settings instead of being duplicated inline.

**Item 6 — Narrow fraud signals.** Phone-uniqueness, investigated rather than assumed: confirmed live via `pg_indexes` that `auth.users_phone_key` is a real UNIQUE INDEX. GoTrue's phone-OTP flow already enforces one account per verified phone at the database level, nothing to build for that half.

Built exactly the two named lightweight signals, explicitly nothing broader: a new `onboarding_device_ids` table (one row per user, written client-side at the same moment `readiness-commitment.tsx`'s "Yes, I'm ready" already writes `behavioral_tracking_disclosed_at`, via a new `src/lib/device-id.ts` that generates and persists a random id in AsyncStorage — no `expo-application`/IDFV-class package is installed in this project, and adding one was out of scope for a "lightweight signal, not a hard block" ask, a known, acceptable limitation, not a control meant to resist a determined evasion) and a new `fraud_signals` table (zero client access at all, not even self-row, reachable only via direct database access for manual review). A single trigger function (`check_fraud_signals`, fires once, at the real onboarding-completion transition) checks both patterns: multiple accounts completing onboarding from the same device within 48 hours, and a referrer's other referred accounts also completing onboarding within 48 hours of each other. "48 hours," not 24, was a judgment call, documented inline: 24h risks false negatives for a real abuse pattern spanning two calendar days in different timezones.

**Verified live, both signals, in one combined real scenario, real seed accounts, fully reverted after:** set Robert Kim and Marcus Alvarez to the same test device id and the same referrer (Elena Torres); Robert completed onboarding first, confirmed zero signals yet (nothing to compare against); Marcus completed onboarding second, confirmed both a `device_clustering` row (Marcus/Robert, same device id) and a `referral_pattern` row (Elena/Marcus, Robert also referred by Elena within the window) were inserted correctly.

**Explicitly deferred, not silently dropped, per instruction:** payment fraud detection, report-abuse detection, and ML/automated catfish detection are all out of scope for this pass and not built. Logged here as real future work: a fraud-review surface would eventually need a way to actually consume `fraud_signals` (today it's direct-database-only), and if device-based signals ever need to resist a more determined evasion than "the same AsyncStorage value," that would mean adopting a real device-identifier package (`expo-application` or similar), a deliberately deferred, small, later decision.

**Item 7 — Analytics.** No existing tool, no existing preference, confirmed via `package.json`/`src` grep before recommending anything. **Recommendation: PostHog.** Reasoning: open-source/self-hostable if this app ever wants full control over its own data rather than a one-way commitment to a closed third-party platform, which sits more comfortably next to AGENTS.md's own "transparency always"/"behavioral tracking... disclosed at onboarding" stance than a closed vendor would; an EU-hosting option matters given how sensitive this app's real data actually is (life transitions like bereavement or divorce, not just generic product-usage events); and a real self-hosted or EU-hosted path exists later without a rebuild, rather than being locked into a single vendor's US-only default from day one.

Installed `posthog-react-native` (checked its `peerDependenciesMeta` before assuming anything else was needed: every peer dependency it lists is marked optional except what a minimal, non-autocapture, non-session-replay integration actually needs, all of which — `@react-native-async-storage/async-storage`, `@react-navigation/native`, `expo-device`, `react-native-safe-area-context` — were already installed; no new native rebuild required). Built `src/lib/analytics.ts`, a thin wrapper (`initAnalytics`/`track`/`identifyUser`/`resetAnalyticsIdentity`) that no-ops completely when `EXPO_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_API_KEY` is unset, matching this codebase's own established `isSupabaseConfigured` graceful-degradation convention, so this project (no key configured today) behaves identically to before this file existed. Wired `initAnalytics()` into `_layout.tsx` and added five real call sites, deliberately not a full retroactive instrumentation pass: `onboarding_completed`, `account_deleted`, `premium_purchased`, `referral_redeemed`.

**Verified:** `tsc` clean with the real PostHog type (`PostHogEventProperties`, imported from `@posthog/core` since `posthog-react-native` doesn't re-export it, confirmed by reading the installed package directly rather than guessing at the import path after an initial wrong guess failed). No API key is configured in this project, so live event delivery to a real PostHog instance was not and could not be exercised; the no-op path was verified instead by confirming the app loads and behaves identically with the integration present and unconfigured.
