### Session, August 11, 2026 (three thread/[id].tsx fixes: blocked-UX ambiguity, real display name, tappable name)

**Item 1, blocked-user conversation UX.**

**Investigated `unblock_user()` first, per instruction, before assuming the given premise was accurate — it wasn't, quite.** Read the live function directly (`pg_get_functiondef`): it already sets a formerly-blocked connection's status to `'inactive'`, not `'active'` as the task assumed. But the migration's own comment (`20260810000001`) says this "restor[es] messaging," and that part of the claim held up under testing: nothing anywhere in this codebase previously treated `'inactive'` as non-messageable. The messages INSERT RLS policy only ever excluded `'blocked'`, and `thread/[id].tsx`'s own compose footer only ever gated on `'blocked'` too, so re-opening a just-unblocked (or any other) inactive thread and typing worked exactly like an ordinary active conversation, silently resuming it with zero fresh start of any kind. So the underlying bug the task described is real, just sourced one layer deeper than the task's own framing (`unblock_user()`'s status *value* was already correct, the surrounding enforcement around that value was the actual gap).

**Fixed uniformly, not special-cased to "closed via unblock" specifically, since there's no sub-state to distinguish it from any other reason a connection becomes `'inactive'`** (F17's 7-day auto-close, S1's "close and free capacity," both pre-existing): migration `20260811000000` adds `c.status is distinct from 'inactive'` to the messages INSERT policy's `with check`, alongside the existing `'blocked'` exclusion, real database-layer enforcement, not just a UI hide. `thread/[id].tsx` gained a real `'inactive'` banner ("This conversation has ended...") and replaced its compose footer with a "View profile" link into `candidate/[id].tsx`, the exact same fresh-start entry point (`create_connection_with_capacity_check`, via `getOrCreateConnectionId`) a brand-new connection already uses, which already correctly reuses and re-flips an existing non-active connection to `'pending'` rather than inserting a duplicate row (unchanged, pre-existing logic from Fix #3, 2026-07-21).

**Ambiguous state for the blocked person, built on a real, pre-existing RLS guarantee rather than a client-side guess.** `blocks`' own SELECT policy (`auth.uid() = blocker_id`, from the original 2026-07-26 migration) already makes it structurally impossible for a blocked person to ever read the other side's block row. New `isBlocker` state in `thread/[id].tsx`, derived from exactly that query (does a row exist where I am `blocker_id` and they are `blocked_id`), drives two genuinely different UIs from the same `connectionStatus === 'blocked'` state: the blocker keeps the existing honest copy ("This connection is blocked. You can no longer message each other." / "Messaging is unavailable, this connection is blocked."), the blocked person now sees "This conversation isn't available anymore." / "Messaging isn't available in this conversation.", wording deliberately reused from `candidate/[id].tsx`'s own existing "That profile isn't available anymore." not-found copy, so it reads as an ordinary, unremarkable state rather than inventing a new tell. Message history stays visible to both, matching the pre-existing "never delete, blocker can still reference it" behavior, now genuinely extended to the blocked person's own view too rather than being conflated with the honest banner.

**A related leak found and fixed while building this, not part of the original ask.** The header's sender-reassurance line ("Life gets busy... you may keep waiting") and a rhythm-mismatch note are both computed independent of `connectionStatus`, so they kept rendering even inside the new ambiguous banner, directly contradicting it (implying a reply might still be coming, right above text saying the conversation isn't available at all). Both now gated on a new `conversationEnded` flag (`blocked` or `inactive`), consistent with how the no-ghost/meetup/pause prompt cards block was already gated on `blocked` alone before this session (now extended to `inactive` too, for the same "closed conversation shouldn't show live-conversation UI" reasoning).

**A real, unrelated stale-data anomaly found and corrected before testing, not silently worked around.** The task's own test fixture (Jordan Blake blocked David Chen, `blocks` row dated 2026-07-27) had a real `blocks` row but the underlying `connections.status` was `'active'`, not `'blocked'`, with a real message sent through *after* the block date, meaning direct messaging had genuinely, silently worked despite an active block, for reasons unrelated to this session's own changes (most likely an earlier session's incomplete test cleanup, not a reproducible code path, `block_user()`'s own SQL correctly matches this exact connection). Corrected by re-running `block_user()` as Jordan against David (idempotent on the `blocks` insert, its `UPDATE` correctly re-set `connections.status` to `'blocked'`), restoring the fixture to a state consistent with the real block row before testing against it.

**Verified live, Playwright against the real dev server plus direct RLS-impersonated SQL, using the corrected Jordan Blake/David Chen fixture, all test data restored afterward:**
- **David Chen (blocked person):** thread shows the real name "Jordan Blake" (not "A member," see Item 2), the ambiguous banner and footer, no visible compose box, no stray "keep waiting" line. Tapping the name still navigates to `candidate/[id].tsx`, which correctly shows "That profile isn't available anymore." for the same reason it always did (the gender/pause/photo/radius/block hard filters), reinforcing rather than undermining the ambiguity.
- **Jordan Blake (the blocker):** thread shows the real name "David Chen," the honest banner and footer, neither the ambiguous copy nor the blocker's own copy leaking to the wrong side.
- **Unblock via Settings:** blocked-accounts list correctly loses David Chen after tapping Unblock. Re-opening the thread (as Jordan) shows the new `'inactive'` state ("This conversation has ended...", a "View profile" link), not a silently-resumed normal compose box.
- **RLS-level confirmation, not just UI:** a direct `insert into messages` attempt as David Chen against the now-`'inactive'` connection failed with a real `42501` row-level-security violation, confirming the fix holds even bypassing the client entirely.
- **The real fresh-start mechanism, confirmed working end to end:** calling `create_connection_with_capacity_check` directly as David Chen against the same connection correctly reused it and flipped its status from `'inactive'` to `'pending'`; a message insert immediately afterward (same connection, same sender) then succeeded, proving messaging genuinely works again once re-initiated the *correct* way, not that it's permanently dead. (The UI-level "Say hello" click-through for this specific pair hit an unrelated, pre-existing limitation: David Chen and Jordan Blake aren't otherwise gender/matching-preference compatible, so `candidate/[id].tsx` shows "isn't available" for reasons that have nothing to do with this fix, the same class of gender-incompatible-test-pair gap this project's session log has hit repeatedly before, e.g. the July 26 Report & Block session having to swap David/Marcus for Maria/Aisha for the same reason. Confirmed the RPC-level mechanism directly instead, since it's the same, unmodified, already-verified logic from Fix #3.)
- All test state restored afterward: the one test message inserted via the fresh-start proof was deleted, and `block_user()` was called once more (as Jordan against David) to put the connection back to genuinely `'blocked'`, matching the state the task described finding it in, confirmed via requery (`status: 'blocked'`, real `blocks` row present, message count back to its original 4).

**Item 2, real display name fix.** Root-caused by direct comparison, not guesswork: `inbox.tsx` reads `inbox_conversations`, a plain view joining `profiles` directly (view-owner-bypasses-RLS, the same pattern `remember_people` already established), no compatibility filtering at all. `thread/[id].tsx` instead queried `discovery_profiles` keyed by `user_id`, whose WHERE clause hard-filters on gender/pause compatibility, a real photo, mutual age range, radius, and block status (`20260807000000`/`20260730000000`/`20260729000000`). Any existing connection where those two participants no longer both pass every one of those filters, most commonly no photo on one side, or a block, made the query return `null`, falling back to "A member" for two real, already-connected people. `inbox_conversations` itself couldn't be reused directly: it requires at least one message to exist (an inner `LATERAL` join), which would silently break a brand-new, zero-message connection's header. New `connection_participant_profiles` view (migration `20260811000000`) is `inbox_conversations`' own pattern, scoped down to just what the thread screen needs (`display_name`, `response_time`, keyed by `connection_id`) and without the message requirement. `thread/[id].tsx`'s initial-load query switched to it.

**Verified live:** the blocked-connection test case above (both directions) now shows real names where "A member" would previously have appeared (blocked connections are excluded from `discovery_profiles` by design, so this was a guaranteed, permanent repro case, not a coincidental one). A healthy, non-blocked conversation (Jordan Blake ↔ Sam Rivera) was also re-checked to confirm no regression, real name still resolves correctly there too.

**Item 3, tappable name.** Already built (the July 16, 2026 session, "Part 3, thread header tap to profile"), re-confirmed live rather than assumed still working after this session's other header changes: tapping the name in a healthy thread navigates to `/candidate/[id]`, confirmed via URL change and a full page reload showing Sam Rivera's real, complete profile (bio, values, activity interests). No code change was needed for this item.

**Verified (all three items):** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. No em dashes in any new code or copy. Migration `20260811000000` applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`. Temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) were removed after verification, confirmed via `git diff` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` carry no trace of it. AGENTS.md's Report & Block entry and SUPABASE SCHEMA section updated to reflect the new differentiated blocked-state copy and the new view/RLS change.

**Not built, out of scope:** a stale, pre-existing `no_ghost_prompts` row was noticed still active for the Jordan/David connection after the unblock test (showing as "A reply is overdue" in Jordan's Inbox for the now-`'inactive'` connection), most likely fired before the original block and never cleared by either `block_user()` or `unblock_user()`. Doesn't affect messaging capability (the thread itself correctly shows the `'inactive'` state regardless), flagged as a small, separate, pre-existing cosmetic gap rather than fixed here, out of this session's three-item scope.

**Item 1, "Upgrade" link fix.** Investigated the premise first, per this project's own standing discipline, rather than assuming a broken link existed somewhere: exhaustively grepped every `.tsx`/`.ts` file in `src/` for "Upgrade" (case-insensitive) and for every `router.push` call in the app. Found exactly one real navigation to `/premium` anywhere (`settings.tsx`'s "See Premium" row), already correct, and zero tappable "Upgrade" elements of any kind. **The premise as literally stated didn't hold: there was no broken link, because there was no link.**

The real, underlying gap: all 5 AI-assist Edge Functions (`generate-reply-draft`, `generate-activity-suggestions`, `generate-connection-analysis`, `organize-remember-entry`, `summarize-remember-timeline`) share the same free-tier blocked-response copy, confirmed by reading each `blockedResponse()`/equivalent directly: *"...or upgrade to Premium for more room."* — but this is always rendered as inert `<Text>`, never a link, in every one of their real client surfaces. Separately, `home.tsx`'s Discover daily-cap card had its own stale comment ("No dedicated Premium/Upgrade screen exists yet") sitting directly above a plain, non-tappable "Premium members get 5 suggestions a day instead of 2." line, stale since `/premium.tsx` shipped 2026-08-01 (Fix "Item 2 — Premium/Upgrade screen", 2026-07-31 session).

**Fixed, matching the underlying intent rather than a literal non-existent bug:** `CreditBlockedError` (universal-text-box.tsx) and a new, equivalent `RememberBlockedError` (`src/lib/remember.ts`) now carry the server's own `data.tier`, so a real "Upgrade to Premium" link (→ `router.push('/premium')`) can be shown only for a genuine free-tier block, never for a premium account hitting its own shared pool cap (which has nothing to upgrade to). Added alongside the existing "Get 50 AI credits" button, not instead of it, in `UniversalTextBox` (the highest-leverage surface, covers chat compose, profile fields, no-ghost drafts, `ReplyAssistPanel`, `ConversationFlowPromptCard`), `ActivitySuggestionsModal`, `ConnectionAnalysisSheet`, and the Remember composer/timeline. `home.tsx`'s Discover card now wraps its existing Premium-mention text in a real `Pressable` to `/premium`, comment corrected.

**A second, more serious bug found while doing this, not part of the original ask, fixed alongside it.** Both Remember AI surfaces (`remember-entry-composer.tsx`'s `handleOrganize`, `remember/[connectionId].tsx`'s `handleSummarize`) used a bare `catch {}` that discarded the real thrown error entirely, including a genuine blocked-cap/pool message, replacing it with a generic fallback string. The user never saw the real reason, let alone an option to act on it, the same class of gap the 2026-07-30 session already found and fixed once on the chat compose box's `requestComposeDraft` (never checking `data?.blocked`). Fixed by introducing `RememberBlockedError` and checking `instanceof` in both catch blocks instead of swallowing everything uniformly.

**Verified live, real seed account (Elena Torres), real caps hit via direct `record_ai_usage` calls (not simulated), all test data reverted afterward:**
- Exhausted her real `generate-reply-draft` daily cap (3 events), confirmed `ai_credits = 0` (no silent credit fallback masking the block), drove the actual UI sequence (empty box → "Help me write" → type situation → "Draft it") in a real thread, confirmed the real blocked message rendered, confirmed "Upgrade to Premium" appeared alongside "Get 50 AI credits" (not instead of it), tapped it, confirmed `page.url()` landed on `/premium` with the real comparison table rendered.
- Exhausted her real `organize-remember-entry` weekly cap (2 events), opened the real Remember composer for a real connection, typed an entry, tapped "Organize with AI", confirmed the real blocked message rendered (not the old generic swallowed string, explicitly checked for its literal absence too) and "Upgrade to Premium" appeared, tapped it, confirmed navigation to `/premium`.
- Confirmed no stray `remember_entries` row was accidentally saved during the blocked-organize test (count query, zero rows).
- `home.tsx`'s Discover-card fix was verified via direct code trace rather than a live click-through: reaching `capReached: true` through the real UI would have required marking Elena's two real, already-cached suggestions (genuine, meaningful connections to Maria Santos and Aisha Bello, both with real message history) as `'passed'`, a real state mutation on real relationships this session wasn't willing to risk for one UI check when the exact same `router.push('/premium')` pattern had already been proven correct twice elsewhere in the same session.
- All test `ai_usage_events` rows deleted afterward, `get_ai_gate_status` re-confirmed `allowed: true` for both functions, restoring Elena to her real, unblocked state.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout.

**Item 5, trace `messaging_preference` enforcement.** Traced exhaustively, both via source grep and live queries against every real enforcement point, not just the obvious ones. **Definitive finding: `messaging_preference` is collected once during onboarding and never read again anywhere in this codebase.**

- Collected in `gender-identity.tsx`'s step 3 ("Who can message you first?"), written to `users.messaging_preference` (text, checked against `'woman', 'man', 'non_binary', 'transgender', 'queer', 'anyone', 'only_people_i_message_first'`).
- Grepped the entire `src/` and `supabase/` trees for the literal column name: the only two hits outside migration history are the one write in `gender-identity.tsx` and the option-label list on the same screen. Zero reads anywhere.
- Confirmed live against every real candidate enforcement point via `pg_get_viewdef`/`pg_get_functiondef`, not assumed from the grep alone: `discovery_profiles`, `browse_profiles`, and `saved_profiles` (views) and `create_connection_with_capacity_check` and `compatible_candidates_for` (the two functions that actually gate who can initiate a connection, F11/F12/Say-Hi) all independently confirmed to contain zero reference to `messaging_preference`.
- Checked the RLS layer directly too, since a client-side check alone wouldn't be real enforcement even if one existed: `connections`' own INSERT policy ("Users can create connections they initiate") only checks `auth.uid() = user_a_id`, nothing about the other participant's stated preference. `messages`' INSERT policy (confirmed separately this session, see the 2026-08-11 entry above) checks blocked/inactive status and the first-message photo gate, also nothing about `messaging_preference`.
- The one other client-side path that can write a `connections` row directly (`upsertConnection` in `candidate/[id].tsx`/`browse.tsx`/`home.tsx`) is only ever called for Save and Pass, never for actually sending a message, so it's not a live loophole around a real check, there simply is no check to route around.

**Conclusion: the July 30 audit's "inconclusive" finding is now definitively resolved, in the direction of "genuinely unenforced."** A user who selects "Only people I message first" today gets no actual protection from that choice anywhere in the app, at any layer, client or server. Not fixed, per instruction (a real fix here is a genuine design decision, not a trivial one, "only_people_i_message_first" and the four gender-specific values overlap confusingly with `matching_preference`'s own already-separate semantics and would need real product judgment to reconcile, not a one-line patch).

**Item 6, trace `activity_interests` (details) reach into match scoring.** Traced directly against `generate-match-suggestions/index.ts`'s real scoring code, not inferred from its own comments. **Definitive finding: the coarse `activity_interests.categories` list DOES factor into the numeric match score; the nested `activity_interests.details` (per-category follow-up answers) never does, anywhere.**

- `activityBonusScore()` (line 290) is a real, counted bucket of the 100-point weighted model (`weightedScore()`, line 327-353): `sharedActivityCategories()` (line 152) compares only `viewer.activity_interests.categories` against `candidate.activity_interests.categories`, a plain set-overlap on category names ("outdoors", "reading", etc.), 2 points for 2+ shared categories, 1 for exactly one, 0 for none. This value is summed directly into `total`, the same `total` used to rank and select which candidates get suggested. So `.categories` genuinely affects who gets shown to whom, confirmed by direct code trace, not assumed.
- `.details` (the nested per-category object, e.g. specific cuisines, pet types, skill level) appears in exactly three places in the whole codebase, all narrative/reasoning generation, never scoring: `generate-match-suggestions/index.ts` lines 431 and 500 (building the human-readable "why" reasoning and the `human_detail` sentence sent back to the client), and `generate-connection-analysis/index.ts` line 168 (F13's "why might we connect?" narrative). None of these three call sites ever feed a number into any score.
- `generate-activity-suggestions` (F23) was also checked, since PROGRESS.md's own earlier claim specifically named it as the intended sole consumer of `.details`. **That claim was itself imprecise**: F23's own local `ActivityInterests` type is explicitly declared as `{ categories?: string[] }`, it never reads `.details` at all, only the coarse category list, same as the scoring function.

**Conclusion, correcting the prior "inconclusive" audit note precisely:** `.categories` scores; `.details` never scores anywhere, and is used only for narrative color in F11's own reasoning text and F13's connection analysis, not even in F23 as previously believed. Not fixed, per instruction, this was a trace-only task and nothing here is a bug, both behaviors are working exactly as their respective code implements them.

**Item 7, Honest Exit vs. AGENTS.md's F29 7-step spec.** AGENTS.md's own F29 entry already flags this as "EXPAND... not currently built at this depth," this session's job was to verify precisely what that means with real code citations, not to re-discover that a gap exists. Traced both real implementations of the exit flow: `ConversationFlowPromptCard` (`src/components/conversation-flow-prompt-card.tsx`, reached only from an already-fired R2/R3 no-ghost escalation prompt) and `MeetupOutcomeCard`'s `not_good` branch (`src/components/meetup-outcome-card.tsx`, reached only after a mutually-confirmed-rough meetup check-in). No other entry point into Honest Exit exists anywhere in the app, confirmed via exhaustive grep for "End the connection"/"End Connection"/"honest_exit".

**Built, matches spec, cited directly:**
- Sender/receiver honest-exit copy is exact and correct: `HONEST_EXIT_SENDER_TEXT`/`HONEST_EXIT_RECEIVER_TEXT` (`src/lib/no-ghost.ts:59-62`) match AGENTS.md's own KEY PRODUCT DECISIONS verbatim, rendered in `thread/[id].tsx` (sender confirmation at `honestExitSent`, receiver's per-message note at line ~829) and again independently in `meetup-outcome-card.tsx:80`.
- Step 4's three of four sub-options: "write freely" (plain `TextInput` in both components), "ask AI to draft from intention" (`UniversalTextBox`'s "Help me write" flow, wired with an exit-specific `purpose` string in both `requestDraft` implementations), "ask AI to polish existing text" (`UniversalTextBox`'s Clean Up, generically available since the same shared component is reused, no separate wiring needed).
- Step 5, review before send: both components gate their Send button on `draftEdited`/`edited`, matching this app's own established "user must see and edit before sending" convention used everywhere else AI drafting appears.
- Step 7's safety half: Block is directly reachable from both R2/R3 and S1/R1 prompt cards (`conversation-flow-prompt-card.tsx` lines 233-238, 402-407), independent of any exit-flow choice, matching "safety concerns bypass explanation." Report is always reachable from the thread header regardless of state, though not specifically wired as an in-flow branch of Honest Exit itself.

**Missing, cited directly, real gaps not just unverified claims:**
1. **No standalone "select End Connection" action.** Both real entry points are conditional on something else happening first (a no-ghost prompt firing because the OTHER person went quiet, or a meetup check-in resolving as rough), there is no button in the thread's own menu/header a user can tap to end a healthy-looking connection purely on their own initiative, the way the spec's step 1 implies. Confirmed by reading `thread/[id].tsx`'s full header/action set (Back, Report, Block only) and finding no third path.
2. **No "send a message OR end without one" choice within the flow itself.** Both `handleSendEscalation` (`conversation-flow-prompt-card.tsx:153`) and `handleSendExit` (`meetup-outcome-card.tsx:67`) hard-require a non-empty, edited message, there is no branch point offering a message-less system-only closure. The one message-less closure that does exist in the app, S1's "Close and make room for another" (`handleSetInactive`/`setConnectionInactive`), is a structurally separate, sender-only button (line 381-390 of `conversation-flow-prompt-card.tsx`), never presented as an alternative within the same Honest Exit decision, and its own comment says it "archives this conversation quietly, no penalty either way", i.e. by design it does not notify the other participant, unlike the spec's step 2/6 requirement that a message-less ending still show "This connection has been closed..." to the other side.
3. **No optional private reason capture.** No field anywhere (capacity / not a match / communication mismatch / leaving app / safety / other) in either component. (Distinct from Report's own category list, which exists but is for reporting, not for a private exit reason.)
4. **No wording templates.** Grepped for "not a match"/"limited capacity"/"communication mismatch" and any generic "template" concept in either component: zero matches. Only freeform typing and AI-drafting exist as message-creation methods; the three blueprint-given verbatim templates are not present anywhere in the codebase.
5. **Step 6's "recipient sees... the neutral system closure" is only partially true**, and only as an incidental side effect of unrelated work: a message-based exit does show `HONEST_EXIT_RECEIVER_TEXT` correctly, but the message-less S1 closure shows nothing Honest-Exit-specific to the other side. The 2026-08-11 session's own `'inactive'`-state banner ("This conversation has ended...") now incidentally covers this connection status for an unrelated reason (the blocked/unblocked-UX fix), not because it was built as part of Honest Exit's own spec, so it's a coincidental partial overlap, not a designed component of this flow.

**Conclusion:** roughly half of the 7-step spec is genuinely built (steps 4, 5, and half of 7), and the parts that are built are solid and correctly cited. The other half (a real, self-initiated entry point; the message/no-message choice; reason capture; templates; a genuine system closure notification) remains unbuilt, matching and sharpening, not contradicting, AGENTS.md's own existing "EXPAND... not currently built at this depth" note. Not fixed, per instruction, this was a report-only task.

### Session, August 13, 2026 (bug fix: stale no_ghost_prompts/meetup_checkins survive a connection becoming blocked or inactive)

**Confirmed both the specific report and, separately, a second and more severe bug in the same area, neither guessed at, both traced directly against live function bodies before writing anything.**

**Root cause 1, the one reported: nothing anywhere clears either table on a connection's transition into `'blocked'` or `'inactive'`.** Traced every real path that sets either status (confirmed exhaustive via `grep -rn "status = 'inactive'\|status = 'blocked'"` across every migration, four hits, no others exist):
- `block_user()` (`20260729000000`) → `'blocked'`: never touched `no_ghost_prompts` or `meetup_checkins`.
- `unblock_user()` (`20260810000001`) → `'inactive'`: same, and this is the exact path that produced the Jordan Blake/David Chen case flagged at the end of the 2026-08-11 session.
- `set_connection_inactive()` (`20260719000001`, S1's "Close and make room for another") → `'inactive'`: the client dismisses only the ONE prompt that triggered the tap (`src/lib/no-ghost.ts`'s `setConnectionInactive`), never any other lingering row, and never touches `meetup_checkins` at all.
- `run_no_ghost_check()`'s own 168-hour auto-close (`20260720000000`) → `'inactive'`: never clears the R1/R2/R3/S1 rows it itself inserted at the earlier 36h/72h/120h/125h thresholds in the very same function invocation, confirmed by reading the live function body directly (`pg_get_functiondef`), the auto-close `update` is the very last statement in the function, no delete precedes or follows it.

**Root cause 2, found while tracing root cause 1, not part of the original report: a genuinely worse, ongoing-generation bug.** Neither `run_no_ghost_check()`'s own top guard nor `run_no_ghost_check_all()`'s WHERE clause, and neither `run_meetup_checkin_check()`'s guard nor `run_meetup_checkin_check_all()`'s WHERE clause, ever excluded `'blocked'`, only `'paused'`/`'inactive'`/`'passed'` were excluded, in all four places, confirmed by reading all four function bodies live. Since a blocked connection keeps its full real message history (Report & Block's own design, confirmed unchanged), the hourly no-ghost cron and the meetup-checkin cron would both keep computing real elapsed time against a real last message and keep inserting brand-new R1/R2/R3/S1 and `meetup_checkins` rows for an already-blocked connection, forever, not just leaving one stale row behind from before the block.

**Scope: systemic, not isolated to the Jordan Blake/David Chen case, confirmed both by code (four independent transition paths, two independent evaluators, none of the six protects `'blocked'` and four of six protect nothing at all) and by data** (a system-wide count query found real, currently-stale rows attached to blocked/inactive connections beyond the one specific case in the report, all cleared by the same fix).

**UI exposure differs by surface, both checked directly, not assumed identical.** Inbox's own "A reply is overdue" label (`inbox.tsx`) already correctly suppresses for `'blocked'` today, its section-bucketing loop checks `connection_status === 'blocked'` before ever consulting the no-ghost map, but has no equivalent check for `'inactive'`, which is the literal, reproducible leak the report described. The Thread screen's meetup checkin/outcome cards are already suppressed for both `'blocked'` and `'inactive'` (the 2026-08-11 session's own blocked-UX gate happened to cover this too), so `meetup_checkins` had no live UI leak today specifically, but the same data-hygiene and continued-generation bugs applied to it identically underneath, and nothing guarantees the next surface that reads it (a future Inbox indicator, a notification, anything) would remember to add the same check.

**Fixed at the root, migration `20260813000000`, one change point instead of patching every call site individually:**
- A new `clear_prompts_on_connection_closed()` trigger function, fired `AFTER UPDATE OF status ON connections`, `FOR EACH ROW`: whenever `new.status` lands on `'blocked'` or `'inactive'` and the status genuinely changed (`old.status is distinct from new.status`), deletes every `no_ghost_prompts` row for that connection unconditionally (matching the existing `clear_no_ghost_on_new_message` trigger's own precedent, a full delete on the connection's own "this state is now stale" event, this table has never been treated as a permanent historical log anywhere in this codebase, a new message already wipes it the same way) and deletes only the *unresolved* `meetup_checkins` rows (`resolved_at is null`), deliberately preserving an already-resolved row as a genuine historical fact (whether `meetup_count` incremented already happened independently via `resolve_meetup_checkin`, not tied to this row's continued existence).
- This one trigger covers all four transition paths uniformly, none of them needed their own individual patch.
- `run_no_ghost_check`/`run_no_ghost_check_all`/`run_meetup_checkin_check`/`run_meetup_checkin_check_all` all redeployed with `'blocked'` added to their existing guard lists, bodies otherwise byte-identical to the live versions (confirmed via `pg_get_functiondef` before writing the migration, not reconstructed from memory), closing the ongoing-generation bug.
- A one-time retroactive cleanup (the same delete logic, scoped to every connection already sitting in either status right now) runs once at the top of the migration, so this doesn't only protect future transitions, it also fixes every connection already affected, confirmed via a system-wide count query returning zero stale rows immediately after.

**Verified live, real seed accounts (Jordan Blake, David Chen, Sam Rivera), covering the retroactive cleanup, all four transition paths, the ongoing-generation fix, and the actual UI, not just the database:**
- Retroactive cleanup: confirmed the exact Jordan Blake/David Chen connection (`aaf21791-...`) dropped from 1 stale `no_ghost_prompts` row to 0 immediately on migration apply, and a system-wide query confirmed zero remaining stale rows across every blocked/inactive connection in the database, not just this one.
- `block_user()` path: used the real, heavily-tested Jordan Blake/Sam Rivera connection (`ca5379b6-...`), which happened to already carry two real undismissed `no_ghost_prompts` rows (R1, R2) and two real unresolved `meetup_checkins` rows from earlier test sessions, a genuine, not synthetic, pre-existing case of exactly the bug in question. Called `block_user()`, confirmed in one query: `status: 'blocked'`, `prompts_remaining: 0`, `unresolved_checkins_remaining: 0`.
- Ongoing-generation fix: called `run_no_ghost_check` directly on the now-blocked connection with `p_now` pushed 400 hours into the future (would trivially have fired R1 through S1 under the old guard list), confirmed zero rows inserted. Called `run_meetup_checkin_check` on the same connection, confirmed zero rows inserted (`connection_not_eligible`, implied by the zero-insert result, `'blocked'` now correctly excluded).
- `set_connection_inactive()` path, a genuinely different code path from block/unblock: reset the same test connection back to `'active'` (`dev_reactivate_connection_for_testing`, after first unblocking it), manually re-inserted a fresh R1 prompt to simulate a real fired-but-unresolved state, called `set_connection_inactive` as the real participant, confirmed in one query: `status: 'inactive'`, `prompts_remaining: 0`.
- **Real UI confirmation, not just the database**: signed in as Jordan Blake through the Dev tab, confirmed via `/profile` first that the correct account was actually active (a first attempt without this check hit a stale-render timing flake, caught and re-run properly rather than reported as a false pass), then loaded `/inbox` and confirmed Sam Rivera's card (now `'inactive'`) shows with no "A reply is overdue" label anywhere on the page, and David Chen's card correctly still shows under the real "Blocked" section header, both entries rendering correctly with no stale reminder text on either.
- All test mutations on the Jordan Blake/Sam Rivera connection restored to their exact original snapshot afterward (`status: 'active'`, the same two `no_ghost_prompts` rows with their original `fired_at` timestamps and `dismissed_at: null`, the same two `meetup_checkins` rows with their original ids, `fired_at`, and `resolved_at: null`), independently re-verified via requery. The Jordan Blake/David Chen connection was deliberately **not** restored to its prior stale-prompt state, that state was the bug itself, not test setup to undo, its current zero-prompts state is the correct, intended outcome of this fix.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (no client-side code changed this session, only the one new migration). No em dashes in any new SQL comment. Migration `20260813000000` applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`. Temporary Playwright test scripts and the temporarily reinstalled Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) removed after verification, confirmed via `git diff` that `package.json`/`package-lock.json` carry no trace beyond the same pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted changes present since before this session.

**Not built, a related but separate observation, not part of this fix's scope:** an `'inactive'` connection currently renders identically to a genuinely healthy `'active'`/`'pending'` one in Inbox, no status label or visual distinction at all (unlike `'blocked'`/`'paused'`, which both get their own section and label). This isn't the bug reported here (the misleading *reminder* is now gone either way), but a user scanning Inbox still can't tell an inactive conversation apart from an active one without opening it. Flagged as a real, separate, smaller UX gap for a future session, not fixed here since it wasn't what was asked.

### Session, August 14, 2026 (Part 1: messaging_preference enforcement, Part 2: stale-prompt re-verify, Part 3: full 9-area live regression pass)

**Part 1, `messaging_preference` enforcement, built and tested live.** Confirmed the 2026-08-12 trace's own reasoning was correct and complete before implementing: `gender_identity`'s check constraint and `messaging_preference`'s check constraint use the exact same five string literals (confirmed via `pg_get_constraintdef`), so a plain string-equality comparison needs no normalization; both columns are nullable at the schema level with zero live nulls today; `users`' own SELECT RLS is strictly self-row, so reading the OTHER participant's `messaging_preference` needed a SECURITY DEFINER function, the same reason `connection_has_any_message()` needed one.

**Built** (migration `20260814000000`): `first_message_allowed_by_preference(connection_id, sender_id)`, a SECURITY DEFINER function implementing all three cases (null/`'anyone'` → allowed; `'only_people_i_message_first'` → never allowed as a first message; one of the five gender values → sender's own `gender_identity` must match). Wired into the messages INSERT RLS policy alongside the existing photo-gate check, reusing `connection_has_any_message()` so the rule only ever applies to a connection's genuine first message. `connection_participant_profiles` (the 2026-08-11 view) extended with `messaging_preference` so the client can build a real proactive UI gate, mirroring the photo gate exactly: a new `firstMessageAllowedByPreference()` client-side mirror of the same logic, a new `blockedByPreference` computed flag in `thread/[id].tsx`, and a new footer state ("This person has chosen who can message them first, and it isn't a match right now...") that deliberately does not reveal WHICH of the two rules blocked it, matching this app's own "ambiguous, not punitive" precedent from the blocked-user UX work, unlike the photo gate there's nothing actionable the viewer can do about someone else's stated preference.

**A real, separate bug found and fixed while building this:** `sendMessage()` in `thread/[id].tsx` never checked its own message insert's `error` return at all, and `handleSend()` cleared the draft text unconditionally before the send even resolved. A genuine RLS rejection (the pre-existing photo gate, or this new preference gate, in a race-condition case the proactive UI gate doesn't cover) would have silently dropped the message with the user's own typed text gone and zero feedback. Fixed: `sendMessage` now throws on a real error, `handleSend` catches it, restores nothing-lost messaging ("That message couldn't be sent. Nothing was lost, your draft is still here."), and only clears the draft on confirmed success.

### Session, August 16, 2026 (four items: Remember entry editing, the meetup_date timezone bug, meetup_date-vs-created_at display confirmation, and the next-meetup date UI)

**Item 1, Remember entry editing.** Confirmed genuinely absent first: `remember/[connectionId].tsx` only ever offered "Delete this entry" once an entry was saved, no path to fix a typo or add a missed detail without deleting and starting over.

**Investigated the existing Add Entry flow before choosing an approach**, per instruction. `RememberEntryComposer` offers raw text first, then an equal, un-nudged choice between "Save as written" and "Organize with AI" (Claude produces a short summary plus an optional follow-up, both editable, nothing saved until an explicit "Approve and save"). **Chose simple, direct editing over re-offering that same AI-organize choice**, reasoning documented inline in the code and here: creation-time AI-organize exists to help turn a rough first draft into something readable, a genuinely different need from correcting or expanding an entry that's already been reviewed and approved once. Re-running the organize step on an edit would mean re-invoking Claude on text the user has already taken ownership of, a new AI-invocation surface with no clear need behind it, and would sit awkwardly next to this app's own anchor principle that the user's own words come first and Claude only ever articulates what's already been said, not re-interprets it a second time uninvited. A direct edit keeps the user in full, immediate control of their own already-approved memory.

**Built:** migration `20260815000000` adds an UPDATE RLS policy to `remember_entries` (`auth.uid() = user_id` on both `using` and `with check`), the one piece missing, this table was deliberately insert/delete/select-only before, by original design, not an oversight. `src/lib/remember.ts` gained `updateRememberEntry({entryId, rawText, organizedText?, followUpNote?, meetupDate})` and two shared utilities moved out of the composer, `parseMeetupDate`/`formatMeetupDate`, so both the edit path and the original composer use the exact same date parsing, closing off a chance for the two to drift into two different bugs later. `remember/[connectionId].tsx` gained inline edit state and an "Edit" link next to each entry's existing "Delete this entry", opening editable fields for raw text, organized text/follow-up (only shown when the entry actually has an AI-organized version), and the date, with Save changes/Cancel. Saving clears the cached `timelineSummary` (the same invalidation the delete paths already trigger), so a stale AI summary can't silently reference a version of an entry that no longer exists.

**Verified live, real isolated test connection (David Chen/Robert Kim, created for this session and fully deleted afterward, see Item 4's cleanup note):** edited an existing entry's raw text, confirmed it saved and displayed the new text correctly on reload. Edited the same entry's date independently (leaving the text as most recently saved), confirmed the new date displayed correctly and the text was untouched. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**Not built:** no edit path for the AI-organize choice itself (a user with only a raw-only entry cannot retroactively ask for an AI-organized version through the edit flow), a deliberate scope match to "should allow editing both the entry text and the date field," not a request to also offer organize-on-edit.

### Session, August 16, 2026, item 2 (bug fix: Remember's meetup_date displayed one day early in any US timezone)

**Confirmed exactly as previously flagged**, a real finding from the immediately preceding regression session (see the "Data correctness, cosmetic" finding above), not fixed there per that session's own instruction to flag rather than fix inline. Root cause, confirmed directly rather than assumed: `remember/[connectionId].tsx`'s `dateLabel()` parsed a plain date-only `meetup_date` string (e.g. `'2026-07-20'`) with `new Date(iso)`, which JavaScript interprets as UTC midnight, then formatted the result with `.toLocaleDateString()` in the browser's own local timezone. For any US timezone (all behind UTC), this displays one calendar day earlier than what was actually entered and stored. Storage was already correct, confirmed again this session before touching anything, only the display was wrong.

**Fixed** by parsing the date's y/m/d components directly and constructing the `Date` via the local-time constructor (`new Date(year, month - 1, day)`) instead of round-tripping through an ISO string, the same fix pattern applied consistently to both `dateLabel()` and the new `NextMeetupIndicator`'s own date formatting built in Item 4, and consolidated into shared `parseMeetupDate`/`formatMeetupDate` utilities in `src/lib/remember.ts` (see Item 1) so this exact bug class can't recur independently in a third place later.

**Verified live:** entered `2026-07-20` as an entry's meetup date through the real UI, confirmed the database correctly stored `meetup_date: '2026-07-20'` (unchanged, confirming storage was never the problem), and confirmed the Timeline now correctly displays "July 20, 2026", not "July 19, 2026". `tsc --noEmit` clean.

### Session, August 16, 2026, item 3 (confirmed: meetup_date vs. entry creation date already display correctly once the timezone fix lands)

**Investigated the Timeline card's rendering directly before assuming anything.** `dateLabel()` already had the correct precedence logic before this session touched it: `if (entry.meetup_date) { ...use meetup_date... } return ...use entry.created_at...`, an entry with a genuinely set `meetup_date` was already meant to show that date rather than its save timestamp. The only defect was the timezone bug fixed in Item 2, there was no separate display-precedence gap to find or fix here, confirmed by reading the function's own branching logic, not assumed correct because Item 2's fix happened to touch the same function.

**Verified live** with a fresh entry: saved one entry with no `meetup_date` set (correctly showed its real creation date) and a second entry on the same connection with a `meetup_date` deliberately different from its actual save day (correctly showed the entered meetup date, not the day it was actually saved). Both confirmed correct in the same live pass used for Item 2's own verification above.

### Session, August 16, 2026, item 4 (feature: next-meetup date UI — propose/confirm/reschedule, day-of feeling check, and a date-driven check-in trigger layered on the existing 7-day fallback)

**Built exactly to the given 8-point spec**, layered on top of the existing milestone/elapsed-time meetup system (2026-07-28 redesign) rather than replacing it, per the explicit "no date ever set falls back to exactly today's behavior" constraint.

**Schema (migration `20260816000000`):** three columns on `connections` (`next_meetup_date`, `next_meetup_status` checked to `'proposed'`/`'confirmed'`, `next_meetup_proposed_by`), not a separate table, matching `last_plan_activity_at`'s own precedent of a single mutable per-connection value since only one meetup can be planned at a time. Two new SECURITY DEFINER RPCs: `propose_next_meetup` (also doubles as the reschedule mechanism, per the given "rescheduling reverts to proposed, needs one re-confirm tap" spec, calling it again on an already-confirmed date unconditionally resets status back to `'proposed'` with the new proposer, no separate reschedule RPC needed) and `confirm_next_meetup` (reinstates the original propose_meetup/confirm_meetup system's "the proposer cannot also confirm their own plan" rule, removed along with that system in the 2026-07-28 redesign, brought back here since mutual confirmation is exactly what this feature is). A new `next_meetup_feelings` table backs the day-of feeling check, own-row RLS matching `first_meetup_feelings`' exact established pattern, deliberately keyed by `(connection_id, user_id, meetup_date)` rather than once-ever-per-connection, so every future confirmed date cycle automatically gets its own fresh, un-acked row with no explicit clearing logic needed. `feeling` only accepts `'neutral'` today, a real column ready for real values later, matching the given "Neutral placeholder, real video routing deferred until more videos are produced" scope exactly, not inventing options that don't exist yet.

**`run_meetup_checkin_check` rewritten, gated so the no-date path is provably unaffected:** re-read the live function via `pg_get_functiondef` before writing the migration, not reconstructed from memory. A new `v_date_driven` boolean is only ever true when a real confirmed date exists (`next_meetup_status = 'confirmed' and next_meetup_date is not null`); every branch falls through to the exact original `last_plan_activity_at + 7 days` logic otherwise. When date-driven, the trigger fires strictly the day AFTER the confirmed date (UTC date comparison, matching this project's own established elapsed-time convention already cited in F17/F21's own migration comments), not the day of. Once it fires via the date-driven path, `next_meetup_date`/`status`/`proposed_by` are all cleared back to null so the thread's own indicator naturally resets to "no meetup planned" rather than continuing to show a date whose check-in has already started; the 7-day fallback path never had a confirmed date to clear and is untouched. `dev_run_meetup_checkin_check` needed no changes at all, confirmed it simply delegates to this function and inherits the new behavior automatically.

**Client:** `NextMeetupIndicator` (persistent, shown at the top of the thread for any non-blocked/non-inactive connection, three render states: no date, proposed, confirmed, plus an inline propose/reschedule editor validating via the shared `parseMeetupDate`) and `NextMeetupFeelingCard` (the day-of neutral placeholder, a single acknowledgment button, not a multi-option picker, matching the given deferred-real-video-routing scope). `thread/[id].tsx`'s `handlePlanSomething` auto-proposes today's date via `proposeNextMeetup` when no date is currently active, a deliberately literal reading of "auto-populates" rather than inventing date-guessing logic. The day-of feeling card is shown only when a confirmed date equals today and the viewer hasn't already acked it for that specific date cycle.

**Verified live, full flow, real isolated test connection (David Chen/Robert Kim, created for this session):**
- **Propose → confirm → reschedule:** David proposed a date, confirmed Robert saw the correct "proposed, waiting" framing and a working Confirm button (David's own view correctly had no Confirm button, matching "the proposer cannot also confirm their own date"). Robert rescheduled to a new date, confirmed status correctly reverted to `'proposed'` needing a fresh confirm. David confirmed via the RPC, status flipped to `'confirmed'`.
- **Day-of boundary:** ran `run_meetup_checkin_check` at the confirmed date itself plus 12 hours, confirmed `'not_enough_time_elapsed'`, proving the evaluator correctly does not fire on the day of, only the day after.
- **Day-of feeling check:** with the confirmed date set to today, confirmed the real `NextMeetupFeelingCard` rendered on the thread screen, tapping "Neutral" correctly wrote a `next_meetup_feelings` row and dismissed the card.
- **Day-after firing:** ran `run_meetup_checkin_check` at the confirmed date plus 1 day 1 hour, confirmed `'fired'`, confirmed two real `meetup_checkins` rows were inserted (one per participant), and confirmed `next_meetup_date`/`next_meetup_status`/`next_meetup_proposed_by` were all correctly cleared to null on the connection.
- **The existing check-in UI, reused completely unchanged, per the explicit instruction not to rebuild it:** a real Playwright pass against both participants' actual thread screens confirmed the `NextMeetupIndicator` correctly showed "No meetup planned yet" (post-clear) with the real, unmodified `MeetupCheckinCard` ("It's been a bit since you two talked about meeting up. How did it go?", all four original options) rendering directly beneath it. ### Session, August 17, 2026 ("End the connection" becomes a real action, new `'ended'` connection status)

**Reported bug, confirmed exactly as described before writing anything.** Both real Honest Exit entry points, `ConversationFlowPromptCard`'s `handleSendEscalation` (the no-ghost R2/R3 escalation card's "End the connection" option) and `MeetupOutcomeCard`'s `handleSendExit` (the meetup-outcome "rough" card's own "End the connection" option), only ever did a plain `messages` insert, never touched `connections.status`. Read both handlers in full before changing either: `handleSendEscalation` called `resolveNoGhostPromptWithMessage` (insert message, dismiss the prompt row) for every option including `exit`; `handleSendExit` called a bare `supabase.from('messages').insert(...)` directly, with no prompt-clearing step at all. Neither had ever changed status, confirmed nothing else in the codebase did either.

**Investigation, all six items, read directly against the live source and live database, not assumed:**
1. `connections_status_check`: `CHECK (((status IS NULL) OR (status = ANY (ARRAY['pending'::text, 'active'::text, 'passed'::text, 'inactive'::text, 'paused'::text, 'blocked'::text]))))`. Six real values, confirmed complete, no seventh value existed anywhere before this session.
2. Both handlers read and shown above: confirmed neither wrote to `connections.status`, confirmed via direct read of `resolveNoGhostPromptWithMessage` (`src/lib/no-ghost.ts`) that it is a plain message-insert-plus-dismiss helper, no status logic of any kind.
3. `clear_prompts_on_connection_closed()` (the trigger built 2026-08-13 for `blocked`/`inactive`): `if new.status in ('blocked', 'inactive') and (old.status is distinct from new.status) then delete ... no_ghost_prompts ... delete ... meetup_checkins where resolved_at is null`. Confirmed it did not cover any other status, by construction, since no other status existed to cover.
4. All four guard-list functions (`run_no_ghost_check`, `run_no_ghost_check_all`, `run_meetup_checkin_check`, `run_meetup_checkin_check_all`), read via `pg_get_functiondef`: all four excluded exactly `('paused', 'inactive', 'passed', 'blocked')`, confirmed identical across all four, confirmed no fifth exclusion existed.
5. `shouldShowMeetupSuggestion` (`src/lib/meetup-suggestion.ts`): confirmed it took only `(messageCount, state)`, zero status awareness of any kind at the function level. Its one call site (`thread/[id].tsx`) happened to render the resulting banner inside a JSX block already gated on `connectionStatus !== 'blocked' && connectionStatus !== 'inactive'`, so blocked/inactive connections were already suppressed in practice, but purely as a side effect of where the render happened to live, not because the function itself knew anything about connection state, exactly the gap the task described.
6. Inbox (`src/app/(tabs)/inbox.tsx`): confirmed the sectioning loop bucketed only `blocked` and `paused` into their own sections with their own per-row label; everything else, including a genuinely closed `inactive` connection, fell into the same undifferentiated "rest"/"Conversations" bucket as a healthy, ongoing conversation, with no label distinguishing them, confirmed by direct read, matching the prior regression pass's own finding on this exact point.

**Design decision: new status is `'ended'`.** Chosen over alternatives (e.g. `'closed'`, `'exited'`) for the closest fit with this app's own established naming convention (short, one lowercase word, matches `pending`/`active`/`passed`/`inactive`/`paused`/`blocked`) and because it reads unambiguously as "someone deliberately ended this," distinct from `blocked` (a unilateral safety action, deliberately ambiguous to the blocked party) and `inactive` (never anyone's considered choice: F17's 7-day auto-close, an unblock, or S1's "close and make room").

**Built (migration `20260817000000_add_ended_connection_status.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`):**
- `connections_status_check` extended to include `'ended'`, the seventh and (for now) last value.
- `end_connection_with_message(p_connection_id, p_content)`, a new SECURITY DEFINER RPC mirroring `block_user()`'s own "one call does the whole atomic thing" pattern exactly: inserts the honest-exit message and flips `status = 'ended'` inside a single function body, not two separate, independently-skippable client calls. Participant-checked, requires a real non-empty message (both real call sites already required this client-side; the RPC itself now also enforces it, not trusting the client alone).
- `clear_prompts_on_connection_closed()` extended to also fire on transition into `'ended'`, reusing the exact same body (`if new.status in ('blocked', 'inactive', 'ended') and ...`), so a real, pre-existing prompt row is deleted the instant a connection ends, not left stale, the same class of bug already fixed for blocked/inactive on 2026-08-13, not allowed to recur here.
- All four guard-list functions (`run_no_ghost_check`, `run_no_ghost_check_all`, `run_meetup_checkin_check`, `run_meetup_checkin_check_all`) extended to also exclude `'ended'`, read live via `pg_get_functiondef` before editing and reproduced byte-for-byte apart from the one addition, not reconstructed from memory.
- `shouldShowMeetupSuggestion` given a new required `connectionStatus` parameter, checked first, before the existing `dismissed_permanently`/threshold logic: `if (connectionStatus === 'blocked' || connectionStatus === 'inactive' || connectionStatus === 'ended') return false;`. This is deliberate defense in depth, not just relying on the caller's own JSX gate (which is also extended to exclude `'ended'`), matching this codebase's own established pattern of guarding a closed-connection state at more than one layer (see the evaluator guard lists and the messages RLS policy, both already doing exactly this for the older two statuses).
- Messages INSERT RLS policy (`"Participants can send messages in their connection"`) dropped and recreated with one more exclusion added to its existing `c.status is distinct from 'blocked' / 'inactive'` clauses: `and c.status is distinct from 'ended'`. Real database-layer enforcement, not just a client-side hidden compose box, matching the exact pattern the photo gate (20260807000000) and the messaging_preference gate (20260814000000) both already established on this same policy.
- **Reopening restriction, the "meaningfully higher than inactive's, not identical" requirement.** `create_connection_with_capacity_check` (the function behind every "Say hello"/reopen tap in the app) now raises a distinct `'ended'` exception for an existing connection in that status, instead of silently flipping it back to `'pending'` the way it already does for `'inactive'`. The only way back in is a new, separate RPC, `reinitiate_ended_connection(p_other_user_id)`, full parity with the original function's own capacity checks (active/pending caps, block check), deliberately narrow (raises `'not_ended'` if the target connection isn't genuinely `'ended'`, this is not a general-purpose alternate connection-creation path). On the client, `getOrCreateConnectionId` (`src/lib/connections.ts`) gained a new `'ended'` `CapacityErrorReason` and message; `candidate/[id].tsx`'s `handleMessage` now branches on it specifically, showing a real confirm `Modal` ("Start over with this connection? ... Starting a new conversation is a real, separate choice, not the same as picking up where you left off.") with Cancel/Start over, matching this app's own established two-step-confirm convention (`settings.tsx`'s account-deletion modal) rather than a native `Alert.alert`, which this codebase has never used anywhere (confirmed by grep) and which Playwright/RN-Web can't reliably drive anyway. Only an explicit "Start over" tap calls the new `reinitiateEndedConnection()` wrapper. `my_connection_capacity()` also gained the same `'ended'` exclusion in its active/pending counts, so ending a connection frees capacity exactly like blocking or auto-closing already do.
- Thread screen (`thread/[id].tsx`): `conversationEnded` now includes `'ended'` (suppressing the response-time header, sender-waiting line, and rhythm-mismatch note, same as blocked/inactive already do). A new top-level banner block for `connectionStatus === 'ended'`, shown to BOTH participants with the identical honest copy ("This connection was deliberately ended, not paused or auto-closed. Messaging isn't available here anymore."), deliberately not Block's differentiated/ambiguous framing, per the explicit instruction that this isn't a safety action. When `honestExitSent` is true, this same block shows the existing `HONEST_EXIT_SENDER_TEXT` confirmation instead, so the sender's own confirmation survives the moment `connectionStatus` flips and the big "connection isn't closed" gate (now excluding `'ended'` too) hides the prompt/outcome card that originally showed it, the identical survives-the-gate reasoning this app's own `honestExitSent` state was already built around for blocked-adjacent unmounts. A new footer branch for `'ended'`, parallel in shape to `'inactive'`'s own (a plain explanation plus a "View profile" link), but its own distinct copy naming the deliberateness, and the profile link now leads into the new higher-friction confirm-modal path rather than a silent reopen. `MeetupOutcomeCard` gained a new `onExitConfirmed?: () => void` prop mirroring `ConversationFlowPromptCard`'s own existing one exactly, both now wired to a single new `handleExitConfirmed` callback (`setHonestExitSent(true); loadConnectionStatus();`) at the thread-screen level, so both real entry points drive the identical downstream UI, not two parallel, potentially-diverging implementations.
- Inbox (`src/app/(tabs)/inbox.tsx`): a new `ended` bucket, sectioned and labeled exactly like `paused`/`blocked` already are ("Ended" section header, "Ended" per-row label), so a genuinely closed connection is now visually distinguishable from a healthy one, closing the specific gap the 2026-08-14 regression pass had flagged for `inactive` (still open for `inactive` itself, out of this session's scope, but not repeated for the new status).

### Session, August 19, 2026 (Inbox 'inactive' label; the remaining F29 Honest Exit gaps: standalone entry point, message-less closure, private reason, templates)

**Part 1, Inbox 'inactive' label.**

**Investigated first, per instruction, whether it needs a full section like `ended`/`blocked`/`paused` or an inline label is enough.** Queried the real, live distribution of `connections.status` across every real connection in the database before making any call: `inactive` (3), `null` (2), `active` (2), `blocked` (2), `pending` (1). Even in this small, mostly-test dataset, `inactive` is already the single most common non-null status, and the reasoning behind that isn't a data-set fluke: `inactive` accrues automatically from three separate, non-deliberate sources (F17's 7-day silence auto-close, S1's own "close and make room for another," and every `unblock_user()` call), none of which are a rare, considered action the way ending, blocking, and pausing all are. Re-read `inbox.tsx`'s own sectioning code to confirm the shape a new section would take: each of the three existing sections (`Paused`/`Ended`/`Blocked`) is its own `<View>` with its own header, stacked below "Conversations."

**Decision: inline per-row label only, no dedicated section, reasoning made explicit rather than just asserted.** A dedicated "Inactive" section would, by the numbers above, likely become the largest section on this screen as real usage accumulates, sitting below "Conversations" and pushing it (and, worse, "Awaiting your reply" is unaffected since it's first, but "Conversations" itself) further down an already-scrolling list, working against Inbox's actual purpose of surfacing what's alive. It would also strip inactive rows out of their real recency ordering: a conversation that went inactive an hour ago would get demoted below a genuinely healthy conversation from last week, the opposite of what a recency-sorted inbox should communicate. An inline label, the same visual treatment `Blocked`/`Ended`/`Paused` already use per row, keeps an inactive conversation sorted by real recency alongside everything else in "Conversations" while still making it visually unmistakable at a glance, satisfying "distinguishable from a healthy one" without the section-count downside.

**Built:** one new branch in `renderConversation`'s existing label ternary (`c.connection_status === 'inactive'` → a caption-styled "Inactive" label, same muted stone styling `Paused`/`Ended` already use), no new bucket, no new section. Comment added to the bucketing loop's own header explaining the deliberate asymmetry with `ended`/`blocked`/`paused`, so a future session doesn't read the missing section as an oversight.

**Verified live:** signed in as Elena Torres (a real seed account with a genuine, pre-existing `inactive` connection to Aisha Bello, confirmed live in the database before touching the UI, distinct from her other real, `active` connection also with Aisha Bello). Inbox screenshot and captured page text confirmed: the inactive row shows a real "Inactive" label, sits inside the plain "Conversations" section (not a separate one), positioned by real recency (4 hours ago, above the healthy 3-days-ago row with the same other person), and no dedicated "INACTIVE" section header exists anywhere on the page (checked directly, not just eyeballed).

---

**Part 2, the remaining Honest Exit gaps, built directly on the 'ended' infrastructure from 2026-08-17, not a second parallel system.**

**Investigated first: `end_connection_with_message`, both existing entry points, and the blueprint's own verbatim text, before writing anything.**
- Re-read `end_connection_with_message` live via `pg_get_functiondef`: `(p_connection_id uuid, p_content text)`, hard-required non-empty content (`raise exception` otherwise), always inserted a `type: 'honest_exit'` message, then flipped `status = 'ended'`.
- Re-read both existing call sites: `ConversationFlowPromptCard`'s `handleSendEscalation` (exit branch) and `MeetupOutcomeCard`'s `handleSendExit`, confirmed both already gate their own Send button on `draftEdited && draft.trim()`, matching this app's own universal "user must review/edit before send" convention, and both already call the shared `endConnectionWithMessage` client wrapper.
- Sourced the actual blueprint text rather than guessing at it or reusing this file's own prior paraphrase: unzipped `Friendship_App_Blueprint_v4.docx` from the user's own Downloads folder (same technique Fix #2/#3 and the 2026-08-17 session both already used), located Section 10's own "Honest Exit decision flow" and its verbatim wording table. Confirmed the real 7-step flow text and, importantly, found the three template messages had never actually been transcribed into this codebase before now, only referenced by name.
- **One real, deliberate deviation from that same source text, flagged rather than silently resolved either way:** the blueprint's own "System-only closure" line reads "*A reason is shown only when they chose to share one*," which implies a shared reason could reach the recipient. This session's own instruction is stricter and more specific ("private only, never shown to the other participant"), and that instruction wins here: the reason is never exposed to the other participant under any circumstance, enforced structurally by RLS (see `connection_end_reasons` below), not merely by a UI choice not to render it. Matches this project's own repeated precedent (F17's gender-neutral copy, F23's parallel-hangout scope) of building to the more specific, current instruction when it conflicts with the blueprint, and flagging the conflict rather than smoothing it over.

**Built (migration `20260818000000_honest_exit_standalone_entry_point.sql`, applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`):**
- New `connection_end_reasons` table (`connection_id`, `user_id`, `reason` checked to the 6 given values, `created_at`). Deliberately its own table, not folded into `reports`, which serves a different, non-private, moderation-facing purpose with its own separate 8-category vocabulary already built for Report & Block. Own-row SELECT-only RLS (`auth.uid() = user_id`), **no client INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy of any kind**, matching the same "structurally private, only a SECURITY DEFINER RPC ever writes it" convention this app already established for `first_meetup_feelings`/`no_ghost_prompts`, so the "never shown to the other participant" guarantee holds even if some future code path ever tried to read it, not just because nothing today happens to render it.
- `end_connection_with_message` dropped and recreated (the argument list changed, not a plain `CREATE OR REPLACE` case) as `(p_connection_id uuid, p_content text default null, p_reason text default null)`. `p_content` is now optional: when null or blank, the message insert is skipped entirely but the connection still flips to `'ended'`, this is the message-less-but-still-notifying path, distinct from S1's own deliberately silent `set_connection_inactive` (unchanged, untouched, stays silent by design). `p_reason`, when provided, writes exactly one row to `connection_end_reasons`. **Fully backward compatible**: both existing call sites still always pass real, non-empty content and no reason, so their own behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged, confirmed by re-reading them rather than assumed.
- Client (`src/lib/no-ghost.ts`): `endConnectionWithMessage` signature changed from `(connectionId, content)` to `(connectionId, { content?, reason? })`; both existing call sites (`ConversationFlowPromptCard`, `MeetupOutcomeCard`) updated to the new `{ content: draft.trim() }` shape, no other change to either. New `ConnectionEndReason` type, `CONNECTION_END_REASONS` (6 options, plain-language labels, not the blueprint's own bare category nouns), and `CONNECTION_END_TEMPLATES` (the 3 verbatim template messages transcribed directly from the blueprint text above, no em dashes to strip, none of the three source lines had one).
- New `EndConnectionModal` (`src/components/end-connection-modal.tsx`), modeled directly on `ReportModal`'s own single-scroll-with-conditional-sections shape (not a new wizard pattern): a message-or-not toggle (step 2), a private-reason chip row (step 3, explicit "Only you can ever see this" caption directly in the UI, not just true by construction), and, only when sending a message, a template-chip row plus a compose field (step 4, reusing `UniversalTextBox` for "Help me write"/Clean Up exactly like the two existing exit flows already do). Picking a template pre-fills the field via the same `setDraftRaw(text); setDraftEdited(false);` pattern `ConversationFlowPromptCard`'s own template/option-pick handler already uses, so Send stays genuinely gated on the user actually focusing/editing the field afterward, per explicit instruction, not a new, weaker rule invented for templates specifically. A message-less end has no such gate (there's no text to review), End Connection is immediately actionable once a reason, if any, is picked.
- Reused `end_connection_with_message` and the entire existing 'ended' infrastructure without touching any of it: same atomic RPC, same `clear_prompts_on_connection_closed` trigger, same evaluator guards, same messages-INSERT RLS block, same Inbox "Ended" label/section, same thread-screen honest banner (identical for both participants, not Block's ambiguous framing), same `reinitiate_ended_connection` higher-friction reopen path from the 2026-08-17 session. Nothing about how a connection behaves once `'ended'` needed to change for this session's work, only how a user can reach that state.
- Thread screen (`thread/[id].tsx`): new "End" pill added to the header alongside the existing Report/Block pills, shown only when `!conversationEnded` (ending an already-ended connection has nothing left to do). Opens `EndConnectionModal`; a successful end calls the exact same `handleExitConfirmed` callback the two existing entry points already share (`setHonestExitSent(true); loadConnectionStatus();`), so all three entry points drive identical downstream UI, not three parallel, potentially-diverging implementations.

**Verified live, all three required scenarios, real evidence for each:**
1. **Standalone entry point, message-less, with a private reason.** Fresh isolated connection (David Chen/Robert Kim, created for this session). Signed in as David, confirmed the new "End" pill in the header, opened the modal, confirmed both the message-or-not choice and the private-reason section render. Chose "End without a message," picked "Not the right friendship match," confirmed the message-compose section correctly disappeared.
