### Session, August 20, 2026 (first-time coach-mark/tooltip system, 9 tips across the app)

**Library investigation, done live before writing any product code, per explicit instruction not to repeat this same session's earlier react-native-iap lesson (a library that turned out to have zero real web support, only discovered during testing).**

`react-native-copilot` was the obvious candidate. Static analysis first, then live verification, not assumed either way:
- Read the installed package's actual source (`node_modules/react-native-copilot/dist/index.js`, no separate `.web.js` variant exists, confirmed via `find`), no `browser` field in `package.json`, no README mention of web support anywhere. Found a real, concrete risk signal: the library's own overlay-mode selection is gated on `typeof NativeModules.RNSVGSvgViewManager !== 'undefined'`, a native-bridge check that's genuinely absent on web (`NativeModules` is a stub there), which looked like strong evidence it would silently degrade or break.
- Rather than stop at that reasoning (the same kind of plausible-but-unverified inference that missed react-native-iap's real gap), built a minimal live repro (`CopilotProvider` + `CopilotStep` + `walkthroughable(View)`) and ran it against this project's real web dev server with Playwright. **Result: it genuinely works.** A full spotlight overlay, step badge, and tooltip box all rendered correctly with zero console errors, screenshotted as proof. The native-module check turned out to only affect which of two internal overlay-rendering strategies gets used, not whether it renders at all, an important, specific correction to the initial worry, not a blanket "it's fine" assumption either.
- **Despite that, not adopted, for a structural reason, not a compatibility one.** react-native-copilot is built around one ordered, linear tour bound to a single app-wide `CopilotProvider`. This app's actual requirement is the opposite shape: 9 independent, scattered, one-off tips, 4 tied to separate tab screens with no shared mount lifecycle, 2 tied to a card that may or may not be mounted on any given thread visit depending on live data state, 1 fired from any of 6 unrelated blocked-state surfaces across the app, all needing to fire or not fire independently of each other and of any fixed order. Forcing that into one global ordered tour would mean either faking step ordinality across screens that don't share a mount lifecycle or running many parallel provider instances, neither of which is what the library is for.
- Built a small custom solution instead, matching this app's own dominant, already-proven idiom for "explain something once, once, dismissibly": a plain bordered card, the exact pattern `no_ghost_prompts`/`meetup_checkins`/every blocked-state banner in this app already uses, not a spotlight/overlay walkthrough. `react-native-copilot` and the temporary throwaway test route were both fully removed after this investigation, confirmed via `grep` that neither `package.json` nor `package-lock.json` carries any trace of it.

**Built:**
- Migration `20260820000000_add_coach_marks.sql`: new `coach_marks_seen` table (`user_id`, `mark_key` checked to the 9 real keys, `seen_at`, primary key on both). Own-row RLS for select/insert/delete, plain client-writable, no RPC needed, matching this app's own established precedent for a personal-preference record with no cross-participant logic (`rhythm_mismatch_dismissals` is the closest match: client passes `user_id` explicitly since no table in this schema defaults it from `auth.uid()`, confirmed by checking directly rather than assumed).
- `src/lib/coach-marks.ts`: `getSeenCoachMarks()` (a shared, module-level cache so every `CoachMark` instance across the whole app reads the same in-memory set, this is what lets the `credits_premium` key, reachable from 6 unrelated surfaces, dismiss once and stay dismissed everywhere else in the same session without a shared React context), `markCoachMarkSeen()` (optimistic local update before the network write resolves), `resetCoachMarks()` (Settings' "Show tips again": deletes every row for the caller and clears the cache), `clearCoachMarksCache()` (called from `_layout.tsx`'s existing `onAuthStateChange` listener, the same one `recordActiveDayIfSignedIn` already uses, so a Dev-tab account switch never reuses one account's seen marks for another's).
- `src/components/coach-mark.tsx`: a single reusable `CoachMark` component (markKey, text, optional actionLabel/onAction), a plain dismissible bordered card matching `MeetupOutcomeCard`'s own tinted-card treatment, with a "Got it" dismiss and an optional second action (used only by `credits_premium`'s "See Premium") that also dismisses.
- Wired into all 9 locations: `home.tsx` (Discover), `browse.tsx`, `saved.tsx`, `inbox.tsx`, `remember.tsx`, `profile.tsx` (each a plain tab-top placement), `thread/[id].tsx` (both `ConversationFlowPromptCard`'s and `MeetupCheckinCard`'s render sites, placed directly above the real card, fired only when that specific card is actually mounted for real data), and `credits_premium` across all 6 real blocked-AI-cap surfaces identified in AGENTS.md's own existing Premium/AI-usage-caps notes: `UniversalTextBox`, `ActivitySuggestionsModal`, `ConnectionAnalysisSheet`, the Remember composer, the Remember timeline, and `home.tsx`'s Discover cap card. Each `credits_premium` instance carries the same text and a "See Premium" action routing to `/premium`.
- Settings: a new "Help" section with a "Show tips again" row, calls `resetCoachMarks()`, shows a real confirmation line, not just an optimistic assumption it worked.

**Verified live, 32/32 checks, real evidence for each, using real seed accounts, all test data fully cleaned up afterward:**
- **All 6 tab-level tooltips** (Discover, Browse, Saved, Inbox, Remember, Profile), tested as Elena Torres: each confirmed visible with its exact given text on a genuinely first visit, confirmed gone immediately after tapping "Got it," confirmed it does **not** reappear on a completely fresh page reload and revisit (a real, hard browser navigation each time, not a soft client-side route change, so this is a genuine cross-reload persistence check, not just in-memory state).
- **credits_premium**, via a real blocked AI-draft attempt: exhausted Elena's real `generate-reply-draft` daily cap (3 synthetic `ai_usage_events` rows, the same established technique from prior sessions), drove the actual chat-compose "Help me write" → "Draft it" flow in a real thread, confirmed the coach mark's exact text and "See Premium" action rendered alongside the real server blocked message, confirmed dismissing it (scoped specifically to this card, since the same thread also had its own independent, real, active no-ghost prompt with its own coach mark visible simultaneously, confirmed both could coexist correctly) made it disappear.
- **no_ghost_prompt**, tested as Jordan Blake against a real, forced R2 escalation prompt (`dev_force_no_ghost_step`) on their real long-lived test connection with Sam Rivera: visible on first encounter, gone after dismiss, confirmed does not reappear on a fresh revisit of the same thread.
- **meetup_checkin**, tested as David Chen against a real, freshly-seeded unresolved `meetup_checkins` row on an isolated test connection with Robert Kim: same three-part confirmation (visible, dismissed, does not reappear).
- **Settings' "Show tips again"**: confirmed the row renders, confirmed tapping it shows a real success message, confirmed revisiting Discover afterward shows the tooltip again, genuinely reset, not just a UI flag, since this was checked after a hard page reload each time.

**A real, pre-existing, unrelated bug found during this testing, not introduced by this session, flagged rather than silently worked around:** `FriendshipExperienceModal` can render as a full-screen overlay on the Discover tab for an eligible account (Elena Torres was eligible during this session) and **intercepts clicks for everything underneath it**, even elements that appear first in the page's own text content (confirmed directly: `document.elementFromPoint` at the coach mark's own on-screen coordinates resolved to the modal's own DOM node, not the coach mark, despite the coach mark's text being present earlier in a plain top-to-bottom text dump of the page). This is a genuine, separate, likely web-specific z-index/stacking defect in an already-shipped feature (2026-07-28), not something this session's own components caused, worked around in testing by dismissing the modal via its own "Not now" first, not fixed here, since it's out of this session's scope.

**Two real bugs in the test scripts themselves, caught and fixed before trusting any result, documented here so a future rerun of similar tests doesn't reintroduce the same flakiness:** (1) a generic `getByText('Got it').first()` dismiss is ambiguous whenever two independent coach marks are visible on the same page at once (a real, common case here: a thread can have both an active no-ghost prompt and a fresh AI-cap block simultaneously), fixed by locating the specific "Got it" positioned closest to that card's own distinguishing text/action rather than assuming only one instance exists. (2) A manual raw-SQL row inserted mid-investigation (to verify the RLS policy directly) was left in place across a later live-UI debug pass, making a real dismiss bug look like "already dismissed," caught by directly re-querying the table rather than trusting the UI symptom alone.

**Native rendering, honestly reported, not claimed beyond what was actually checked:** `CoachMark` and `coach-marks.ts` use only core cross-platform primitives (`View`/`Text`/`Pressable`, plain `supabase-js` calls), no native-only APIs, no `Modal`, no `measure()`, no new native dependency of any kind (the one library evaluated for this feature was deliberately not adopted). This is real evidence the implementation should behave identically on native, but this environment has no iOS/Android simulator, the same standing limitation already documented repeatedly in this project (deepidv, in-app purchases, voice-to-text), so a genuine on-device confirmation was not possible and is not claimed here.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout, checked after every edit and once more in the final state. No em dashes in any new code, copy, or SQL comment (checked directly across every touched file). Migration `20260820000000` applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`. `react-native-copilot`, the temporary throwaway test route, all temporary Playwright test scripts and screenshots, and the temporarily (re)installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) were all removed after verification, confirmed via `grep -i "playwright\|react-native-copilot"` across both `package.json` and `package-lock.json` (zero matches). All test data (the isolated David Chen/Robert Kim meetup-checkin connection, the R2 prompt added to Jordan Blake/Sam Rivera's real connection, Elena Torres's synthetic `ai_usage_events` rows, and every `coach_marks_seen` row created across all three test accounts) fully deleted afterward, independently reconfirmed via requery, not assumed clean from the test scripts' own exit codes.

**Not built:** the `FriendshipExperienceModal` click-interception bug found above, flagged, not fixed, out of this session's scope. No changes to the underlying free-tier cap/pool logic, Report, Block, or any of the 6 blocked-state surfaces beyond adding the one new `CoachMark` line to each, all pre-existing behavior on those surfaces is unchanged and was reconfirmed still correct as a side effect of driving a real blocked draft attempt through one of them live.

### Session, August 22, 2026 (feature: landing-tab default, Inbox vs. Discover based on real conversation activity)

**Investigated first.** `src/app/index.tsx` (the bare root, the actual post-auth landing decision point since the 2026-07-25 session's session-aware-routing rebuild) had no landing-tab logic of any kind: a fully onboarded user (`profiles.display_name` present) always got `setTarget('/home')`, a fixed target, no branching. `(tabs)/_layout.tsx` confirmed `/home` maps to the Discover tab specifically (first `Tabs.Screen`, title "Discover"), and `/inbox` is a real, separate tab route, so redirecting to either from `index.tsx` is a legitimate, already-supported destination, no new route needed.

**Checked what "active connection" actually means live before picking a signal, rather than taking the literal `status = 'active'` column at face value.** Queried the real distribution: only 2 connections in the whole database have `status = 'active'` literally, both traceable to pause-resume/dev-reactivation actions, not to ordinary connection creation (`create_connection_with_capacity_check` defaults a new connection to `pending`/null). A literal `status = 'active'` check would have badly undercounted real, ongoing conversations sitting at `pending` or null status. The app already has one tested, real definition of "a live conversation" that the Capacity System has relied on since 2026-07-21: `my_connection_capacity()`'s `active_count` (status not in the terminal/paused set, AND genuine bidirectional message exchange) and `pending_count` (one-directional, a real Say Hi sent or received with no reply yet). Reused this RPC directly rather than writing new SQL or a second, competing definition of "active."

**Built:** `index.tsx` now calls `supabase.rpc('my_connection_capacity')` once profile completeness is confirmed, and routes to `/inbox` when `active_count + pending_count > 0`, `/home` (Discover) otherwise. `pending_count` was deliberately included, not just `active_count`: a one-directional Say Hi with no reply yet is still a real conversation thread the user would want to see in their Inbox on open, not a blank slate, matching the task's own explicit invitation to use judgment on which statuses should count. `ResumeTarget`'s type extended to include `/inbox`. No changes to `(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, `my_connection_capacity()` itself, or any other onboarding-completion redirect (`modules-complete.tsx`'s own `router.replace('/home')` is untouched and doesn't need this logic, a brand-new user finishing onboarding has zero connections by construction either way).

**Verified live, both required cases, real seed accounts, via a real Playwright pass against the actual dev server (temporarily installed, fully removed after):** computed real `active_count`/`pending_count` per seed account directly against the database first to pick honest test candidates rather than assuming: David Chen (0/0) and Maria Santos (1 active/0 pending). Signed into each via the real Dev tab account switcher, then forced a genuine cold reload of `/` (account-switching itself navigates via `router.replace('/home')`, which bypasses `index.tsx` entirely, so this was a real, separate reload, not just observing the switch's own redirect). David Chen landed on `/home` (Discover); Maria Santos landed on `/inbox`. **A real test-script race was hit and fixed before trusting the first result**, the same class of bug the 2026-08-17 session already documented once: a hard `page.goto('/')` immediately after clicking a seed account in the Dev tab can fire before the sign-in session finishes writing to storage, and the first David Chen run landed on `/philosophy-intro` (read as "no session") for exactly this reason. Fixed by waiting for the real post-sign-in `/home` URL (`page.waitForURL`) before the subsequent cold reload, not a fixed sleep; re-ran clean, both cases correct.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. No em dashes in the edited file. The temporary Playwright test script and the temporarily installed Playwright dependency (`--no-save`, then `npm uninstall`) were removed after verification, confirmed via `grep -i playwright` (zero matches in `package.json`/`package-lock.json`).

**Not built:** no change to `my_connection_capacity()` itself, or to any other entry point into the tab bar (onboarding's own completion redirect, deep links, notifications-none-exist-yet), this was scoped strictly to the bare-root landing decision.

### Session, August 22, 2026 (feature: spotlight/dimming effect added to the 9 existing coach marks)

**Investigation 1, react-native-copilot reconsidered specifically for the narrower "just the spotlight visual" ask, not assumed rejected for the same reason as before.** Re-read the 2026-08-20 session's own reasoning first: it was rejected there for a *structural* reason (one ordered, linear tour bound to a single `CopilotProvider`, versus this app's 9 independent, out-of-order, sometimes-simultaneous tips), after having been *proven* to render a real spotlight overlay correctly on web. That prior finding about web rendering still stands and wasn't re-litigated. What this session actually re-investigated, per explicit instruction not to assume the old conclusion transfers: whether the library's rendering engine itself, independent of the tour/ordering question, could support this app's own confirmed requirement that **two coach marks can be simultaneously visible at once** (the Aug 20 session's own live test: an active no-ghost prompt and a credits-blocked draft attempt, both real, both up at the same time on one thread screen).

Reinstalled `react-native-copilot` temporarily and read its actual shipped type definitions (`dist/index.d.ts`), not recalled from memory: `CopilotContextType.currentStep: Step | undefined`, singular, not a set or array, and the whole `OverlayView` rendering model is driven off that one field. `start(fromStep?: string)` does let a tour begin at an arbitrary named step (addressing the ordering half of the original concern), but the library still has no notion of two simultaneously-active spotlights, by construction, confirmed directly from its own types rather than assumed. Achieving 2 simultaneous independent spotlights with this library would require nesting multiple separate `CopilotProvider` instances, one per coach mark, each hosting exactly one step, an unsupported, undocumented repurposing of a tour library as a single-spotlight primitive, not "using the library" in any real sense, and it would still need a second, parallel visibility/tracking system built alongside the already-correct `coach_marks_seen` logic to decide when each provider's own `start()`/`stop()` fires. **Conclusion: not adopted, for a new, more precise reason than before, freshly substantiated rather than inherited.** A small (~150-line) custom `View`-based spotlight, built to natively support N independent, simultaneous instances by design, is a better fit than bending a single-active-step tour library around a shape it was never built for. Removed again after this investigation (`npm uninstall react-native-copilot`; the one holdout, an `EBUSY`-locked leftover folder from a live dev-server file handle, was removed directly since npm's own rename-based uninstall couldn't touch it), confirmed via `grep`/`ls` that neither `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, nor `node_modules` carries any trace afterward.

**Investigation 2, current CoachMark anchoring, read directly rather than assumed from memory.** All 9 usages (`home.tsx`, `browse.tsx`, `saved.tsx`, `inbox.tsx`, `remember.tsx`, `profile.tsx`, `thread/[id].tsx` ×2, and `universal-text-box.tsx`/`activity-suggestions-modal.tsx`/`connection-analysis-sheet.tsx`/`remember-entry-composer.tsx`/`remember/[connectionId].tsx` all sharing the `credits_premium` key) rendered a plain, generic top-of-content `<View>` banner (`coach-mark.tsx`, unchanged since the 2026-08-20 build): no positioning logic of any kind, no relationship to any specific on-screen element, just wherever `<CoachMark .../>` happened to sit in the JSX. For the 6 tab tips this meant the tooltip rendered on the SCREEN itself, nowhere near the actual tab bar icon it was describing (that icon lives in a completely different part of the component tree, inside `(tabs)/_layout.tsx`'s `tabBarIcon` render prop, rendered by React Navigation's bottom tab bar, not by the screen at all). For `no_ghost_prompt`/`meetup_checkin`, the banner already sat directly above its own `ConversationFlowPromptCard`/`MeetupCheckinCard` (a reasonable rough proximity, but no dimming, no ring, and no structural link, just incidental JSX ordering). For `credits_premium`'s 6 surfaces, the banner sat directly beside the blocked-message text and, critically, already contained the real actionable button itself (`actionLabel="See Premium"`, rendered inside the SAME card), confirmed by reading `coach-mark.tsx`'s own render function.

**Investigation 3, web vs. native feasibility, checked directly rather than assumed identical.** `Modal` (transparent, cross-platform) is already used extensively and proven working on web throughout this app (`ReportModal`, `EndConnectionModal`, `FriendshipExperienceModal`, and 5 others), a safe, already-validated choice for the full-screen overlay. `onLayout`/`ref.measureInWindow()` (the standard RN technique for getting an element's real on-screen, window-relative position, as opposed to `onLayout`'s own parent-relative coordinates, which aren't enough for a full-screen overlay to draw a cutout in the right place) had never been used anywhere in this codebase before, confirmed via grep, genuinely new territory here, not an established pattern to lean on. `useWindowDimensions` (also never used before) is the standard cross-platform, resize/rotation-reactive way to get real screen size, used here instead of a static `Dimensions.get()` snapshot. `SVG` masking was deliberately avoided in favor of a plain rectangle-subtraction algorithm (below) specifically so nothing here depends on a masking library that might behave differently across web/native. **Real, disclosed limitation, not solved and not silently assumed fine:** on Android specifically, plain wrapper `View`s can be optimization-collapsed out of the native tree before ever being measurable (a real, documented RN behavior, not hypothetical), addressed defensively with `collapsable={false}` on every measured view, but this specific defense could not be exercised on a real Android device in this environment, the same class of native-only gap this project has repeatedly disclosed for IAP and voice-to-text. Separately, native `Modal`'s own touch-capture behavior means the "hole" in the dimming layer, where nothing is rendered, likely still blocks touches to the real underlying element beneath it on native (unlike web, where an empty area of a transparent overlay may let clicks pass through more readily). Accepted as a known limitation rather than solved, since none of the 9 flows require interacting with the spotlighted element while its own tip is still showing.

**Built:**
- `src/lib/spotlight.ts`: two small pub-sub registries. (1) Target rects, written by a new `<SpotlightTarget markKey=...>` wrapper (measures via `measureInWindow` on layout, republishes to the store) and read by `CoachMark`; this is how the 6 tab icons and the 2 prompt/checkin cards get spotlighted at their real, external position instead of the coach mark's own. (2) Active tooltips, written by `CoachMark` once it has both "am I unseen" (unchanged) and a resolved target rect, read by one shared `SpotlightHost`. A single shared host, not one Modal per `CoachMark` instance, is the direct fix for the confirmed 2-simultaneous-marks case from Investigation 1: two independent full-screen dimming layers would each dim "everything except MY OWN rect," meaning the second one to open would re-dim the first one's spotlight the instant both were open together. `computeDimmingRegions()` is a standard rectangle-subtraction algorithm (split each dimming region around each hole into up to 4 remaining pieces, repeat per hole), generalizing correctly to any number of simultaneously-active marks, not just the one or two seen in practice.
- `src/components/spotlight-target.tsx`: the wrapper described above, `collapsable={false}` + `onLayout` + `measureInWindow`.
- `src/components/spotlight-host.tsx`: mounted once at the app root (`_layout.tsx`, alongside the existing `Stack`). Renders nothing when no mark is active (same "no footprint until earned" behavior the old banner always had). When one or more are active: one dimming layer (computed via the rectangle-subtraction above), a lit-up ring per active mark (`borderWidth`/`borderColor`/a soft `boxShadow` glow, radius scaled to the target's own shorter side so a small tab icon gets a near-circular ring and a wide card gets a rectangular one), and a tooltip box per mark (same text/`actionLabel`/"Got it" content and behavior as the old banner, just repositioned: placed below the target when there's room, above it otherwise, horizontally clamped to stay on-screen), all inside one `Modal`.
- `src/components/coach-mark.tsx` rewritten internally, same external `Props` (`markKey`, `text`, `actionLabel`, `onAction`), same "seen" tracking calls (`getSeenCoachMarks`/`markCoachMarkSeen`, byte-identical, not touched). Once visible, resolves a target rect: an external `SpotlightTarget` registration for its own key if one exists, else self-measures its own rendered position. Renders only an invisible (`opacity: 0`, `pointerEvents: 'none'`) placeholder inline, reserving the exact layout space the old visible banner used to occupy so nothing shifts, and registers/unregisters with `SpotlightHost` as it becomes visible/dismissed/unmounted.
- Wired `<SpotlightTarget markKey="tab_xxx">` around each of the 6 `Ionicons` in `(tabs)/_layout.tsx`'s `tabBarIcon` render props, and around `<ConversationFlowPromptCard>`/`<MeetupCheckinCard>` in `thread/[id].tsx`. Deliberately did **not** add any `SpotlightTarget` wrap for `credits_premium`'s 6 surfaces: since its own card already contains the real "See Premium" action (confirmed in Investigation 2), self-measuring its own rendered position spotlights the actual actionable button correctly, without needing to identify one consistent "the button" across 6 structurally different surfaces (some have a distinct "Get 50 AI credits" button, others only an "Upgrade to Premium" link, confirmed by reading all 6 directly before deciding this).
- Mounted `<SpotlightHost />` once in `src/app/_layout.tsx`.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during this session's own live testing, not part of the original build, both disclosed here rather than smoothed over:**
1. The invisible self-measuring placeholder, in its first version, rendered an empty `<View style={{opacity:0}}>` with no children, an empty View has no intrinsic size, so `measureInWindow` reported 0x0 and self-measurement could never resolve a rect at all, meaning `credits_premium`'s 6 surfaces would never have shown anything. Fixed by rendering the placeholder with the same text/actionLabel/"Got it" content the old visible banner had (still invisible), giving it a real, representative size to measure.
2. **The more serious one, caught by this session's own first live screenshot, not assumed correct from reading the code.** `SpotlightHost` wrapped each active mark's ring + tooltip in a plain, unstyled `<View key={mark.markKey}>`. An unstyled View sits in the outer `flex-1` container's normal column layout flow at effectively zero height (nothing non-absolute inside it to give it size), and a `position: 'absolute', bottom: X` child measures its offset against *that* zero-height parent, not the real screen, an easy trap since `top`/`left` offsets don't have this problem (they only need the parent's top-left corner, which is correct even at zero height) but `bottom`/`right` do (they need the parent's true bottom/right edge). This meant every mark using the "place above" branch, exactly the case for a tab icon sitting at the very bottom of the screen, rendered its tooltip off-screen: the ring (top/left-positioned) showed correctly, the tooltip (bottom-positioned) did not, confirmed by a real screenshot showing a correctly-lit Discover tab icon with no tooltip anywhere in view. Fixed by replacing the wrapping `View` with a plain `Fragment`, making the ring and tooltip direct children of the real, correctly-sized `flex-1` container instead.

Also fixed, found live via a genuine browser console warning during testing rather than assumed clean: two `pointerEvents="..."` top-level props (a real, currently-deprecated RN API, moved to `style.pointerEvents` instead) and the ring's 4 separate legacy `shadow*` style props (moved to the newer, unified `boxShadow` string, `elevation` kept for Android since that one isn't deprecated).

**Verified live, per this session's own actual sequence, not a single clean pass, disclosed honestly rather than presented as smoother than it was.** A full, 9-tip, single automated Playwright sweep was attempted first and failed three separate times purely on Metro cold-compile/timing flakiness after each dev-server restart (a real sign-in click not completing before a hard page reload, `waitForURL` timeouts, one genuinely stale-bundle warning that had already been fixed in source but not yet picked up by the running server), never on an actual assertion about the feature itself. Per explicit instruction once this pattern was clear, stopped fighting the large sweep and switched to a narrow, high-confidence manual check of exactly 2 marks (one tab-level, one contextual/self-measured), with generous waits and real screenshots actually looked at, rather than continued blind retries:
- **`tab_discover` (Aisha Bello, an account confirmed via direct query to already have `profiles.friendship_experience` answered, deliberately chosen over Priya Nair specifically to sidestep a separate, real, already-documented 2026-08-20 bug where `FriendshipExperienceModal` can cover the whole Discover screen and intercept clicks to whatever's underneath it, including this spotlight's own tooltip, confirmed to reproduce again this session when Priya Nair was tried first).** Screenshot showed, unambiguously: the background dimmed, a lit orange ring around the real Discover tab icon in the bottom tab bar, and the tooltip ("Discover shows AI-suggested matches picked for you.") anchored just above the tab bar with a working "Got it". A real pixel-coordinate click on "Got it" (chosen over `getByText`, which turned out to match two elements at once, see below) made the whole spotlight disappear immediately; a full page reload afterward showed a completely clean, undimmed screen with no reappearance. Confirmed directly against the database: a real `coach_marks_seen` row (`tab_discover`, real `seen_at` timestamp) was written for Aisha Bello by this one dismiss, and none existed before it.
- **`credits_premium` (Robert Kim, on a freshly created, isolated test connection with Marcus Alvarez, deliberately not the long-lived real Elena/Maria thread originally planned: that connection turned out to carry genuine, unrelated leftover `no_ghost_prompts` rows from far earlier sessions, dated 2026-07-30 through 08-02, whose own real, correctly-firing coach mark kept confounding this specific check, a real, disclosed test-environment finding, not a product bug, resolved by moving to a connection with zero other state).** Drove the real "Help me write" then "Draft it" flow (a real test-script ordering bug was found and fixed along the way: typing text *before* tapping "Help me write" skips past the exact UI state that button only exists in) against Robert Kim's exhausted daily cap. ### Session, August 23, 2026 (deep investigation of the Dev tab's broken sign-in buttons — root cause narrowed with strong evidence, no reliable fix found, not shipped as "fixed")

**Continuing from the 2026-08-22 session's own investigation**, per explicit instruction not to re-run its already-completed diagnostics (overlay interception, manual event dispatch, touch `tap()`, stale-bundle check via a served-JS `curl`, React Compiler flagged as untested). This session ran a much deeper investigation, ruled out several strong hypotheses with real evidence each, and ultimately found the bug's real character (rare, non-deterministic, framework-level) rather than a fixable line of app code. **No fix is shipped.** Every diagnostic change made during this investigation (React Compiler toggle, `SpotlightTarget` removal, `react-native-web` version bump, a forced-re-render hook, a `key`-based remount hack, temporary debug/diagnostic elements) was reverted; the repo is byte-identical to how this session found it, confirmed via diff against a pre-investigation backup.

**New finding 1, real and separate from the Dev tab: an unauthenticated visit to `/home` leaves a permanent, app-wide click-blocking overlay.** Landing on `/home` with no session triggers the `tab_discover` coach mark (built 2026-08-20/22); its dismiss ("Got it") is present in the DOM but not genuinely clickable in this state, and because `SpotlightHost` is mounted at the true app root (`_layout.tsx`, a sibling of the entire `<Stack>`, not scoped to `(tabs)`), its full-screen `rgba(0,0,0,0.55)` dimming layer persists across every subsequent navigation, including into `/dev`, confirmed live: `elementFromPoint` at the center of `/home` resolves to a childless dimming `<div>`, not real content, and a client-side tab-bar `Link` click from `/home` into `/dev` fails with Playwright's own `"<div class="css-view-g5y9jx"></div> from <div>…</div> subtree intercepts pointer events"` error. **Ruled out as the explanation for the Dev tab's own reported bug**, though: pre-marking every `coach_marks_seen` key for a test account (removing this overlay entirely, confirmed via the coach-mark text genuinely absent from the page) did not fix the sign-in click; the failure is real independent of this overlay. Flagged as a genuine, separate bug, not fixed here (out of this session's scope), worth its own session: either the unauthenticated case needs a session-aware guard before firing `tab_discover`, or `SpotlightHost`'s dimming layer needs a way to auto-clear on navigation away from the screen that raised it.

**Systematically ruled out for the Dev tab's own bug, each with direct evidence, not assumption:**
1. **React Compiler.** Set `experiments.reactCompiler: false` in `app.json`, full `--clear` restart (confirmed via the startup log genuinely losing its "React Compiler enabled" line): identical failure. Reverted immediately.
2. **`SpotlightTarget` wrapping the 6 tab-bar icons** (`(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, built 2026-08-22). Temporarily stripped all 6 wrappers, `--clear` restart: identical failure. Reverted, confirmed byte-identical to original via diff against a pre-edit backup.
3. **Not isolated to `handleSwitch` or to the seed-account buttons specifically.** A second, unrelated `Pressable` on the same screen (`Reset my matches`) shows the identical symptom (no RPC call, no status text, confirmed via network-response logging).
4. **Not isolated to `/dev`.** A real, authenticated (session-injected, coach-marks-suppressed) click on a Discover card's "Saved" toggle also silently fails — confirmed two ways, not just DOM text: the toggle's own `connections.saved` database value was queried before and after the click and stayed unchanged.
5. **Not headless-Chromium-specific.** Launched with `headless: false` (a real, non-headless Chromium process, the closest approximation to a real browser available in this shell environment): identical failure.
6. **Not React's own event system.** A plain `<button onClick={...}>` (bypassing `Pressable` entirely), added directly into `dev.tsx`, fires correctly every time. A CDP-level check (`DOMDebugger.getEventListeners`, the real, ground-truth Chrome DevTools API, not a heuristic) confirms `#root` itself has a genuine, working `click` listener (`clickCount: 3`) attached by React's own delegation. So React's reconciler and event delegation are demonstrably alive and correctly wired on this exact page.
7. **Not `Pressable` in general, not its `disabled` prop, not being inside `.map()`.** A bare `Pressable` (no props beyond `onPress`) added to the same screen fires correctly. Read `PressResponder.js`'s own source directly: `onClick: (event) => { if (!disabled) onPress(event) }` — `disabled={false}` and no `disabled` prop at all are both falsy and behave identically, ruling this out by source, not inference. A `Pressable` reproducing the real one's exact `className`, exact `disabled` expression, AND placed inside a `.map()` still fired correctly, right next to the real, still-broken "David Chen" button on the same render pass.
8. **Not a stale-bundle/hydration-timing race in the simple sense.** Waited 15 seconds after the button became visible before clicking (far longer than this project's bundles take to finish executing): identical failure. A DOM-node "fiber ownership" check (`Object.getOwnPropertySymbols`) initially looked like a smoking gun (zero symbols on the clicked node and every ancestor) but turned out to be a **false lead**: `#root` itself — proven to be a live, listener-bearing React root via CDP — also shows zero own symbols, meaning this React/react-native-web version simply doesn't expose fiber ownership via enumerable properties at all. Flagged explicitly so a future investigation doesn't re-chase this same red herring.
9. **Not `TouchableOpacity` as an alternative** (not even empirically tested): read its source directly, it imports and calls the identical `usePressEvents` module `Pressable` uses, so it would reproduce the same underlying mechanism, not a genuinely different code path.
10. **Not this project's specific `react-native-web` patch version.** Bumped `~0.21.0` to `0.21.2` (the latest available), full `--clear` restart, fresh first click: identical failure. Reverted to `~0.21.0`, confirmed the version string and `package-lock.json`'s resolved entry both match the original exactly.

**The real, load-bearing finding: this is genuinely non-deterministic, not a consistently-reproducible bug with a single deterministic cause.** During hypothesis-testing, one specific sequence (adding two new diagnostic `Pressable`s above the real button, several Fast-Refresh cycles, then re-clicking the real "David Chen" button) appeared to fix it once. Two immediate repeat attempts against the exact same running server and the exact same account failed again. A clean, fully-reverted, freshly-`--clear`-restarted server was then tested across **8 independent trials, one fresh browser page per trial, one real seed account per trial (all 8 different accounts): 0 of 8 succeeded.** The one earlier apparent "success" is best explained as background noise from a low, non-zero baseline success rate, not evidence that any of the tested interventions (forced re-render via `useEffect`+`useState`, or a `key`-based full remount of the button list, both tried and both reverted after failing to reliably help) actually fixes anything. Both of those two candidate "fixes" were tested and are **not being shipped**, because neither showed a measurable, repeatable improvement over the untouched baseline.

**Why no fix is being shipped, stated directly per the instruction to only fix once truly root-caused:** the investigation converged on a strong *characterization* (a rare, environment/timing-sensitive failure specific to `react-native-web`'s `Pressable`/`usePressEvents`/`PressResponder` gesture-responder system, reliably reproduced under this project's own Metro/Expo Router web dev server + React 19.1.0 combination, essentially only on screens reachable via the bottom-tab navigator, and not reproduced at all on a plain Stack screen in every test run), but not a genuine root cause at the level of "this specific line of code is wrong." Every mechanism-level fix attempt (forcing a re-render, forcing a full remount, swapping the touch-handling library, bumping the dependency version, disabling the one experimental compiler flag most likely to interact badly with a legacy responder system) failed to move the needle from a near-0% baseline success rate. Shipping any of those as "the fix" would have been dishonest given the batch-tested evidence directly contradicts it working reliably.

**Recommended next steps, not attempted here, in likely order of leverage:**
- Search `react-native-web`'s own GitHub issue tracker for reports combining "React 19" and "Pressable"/"onPress not firing"/"usePressEvents" — this looks like the profile of a genuine, already-known upstream compatibility gap between React 19's reconciler and react-native-web's still-legacy responder-based touch system, not something specific to this app's own code.
- If no upstream fix exists, consider a temporary, deliberate downgrade of `react` and `react-dom` to the last React 18.x line as a real experiment (not attempted here: React version, unlike a patch-level dependency, is a much larger, riskier, cross-cutting change this session's scope didn't warrant making unilaterally) to confirm or rule out React 19 itself as the true variable, before considering it a permanent fix.
- Given `/dev` is a `__DEV__`-only tooling screen, not shipped to real users, and given this session confirmed the *workaround* from the 2026-08-22 session (minting a session directly via `dev-create-session` + `/auth/v1/verify`, then injecting it into `localStorage` before navigating) remains fully valid and unaffected by any of this — that workaround is still the right tool for any session needing to drive a signed-in Playwright test through this app, not a stopgap to be embarrassed about.
- Separately, the newly-found `/home`-unauthenticated coach-mark-overlay-persistence bug (finding 1 above) is real, scoped, and independently fixable; worth a dedicated session.

**Verified:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean in the final, fully-reverted state. No em dashes in anything kept (all diagnostic-only code was removed, not kept). `app.json`, `(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, and `dev.tsx` all confirmed reverted to their exact pre-investigation state (the layout file via a direct `diff` against a pre-edit backup; the others via direct inspection of the specific lines touched). Temporary diagnostic scripts (9 `diag*_tmp.js` files) and the temporarily reinstalled `playwright` dependency were removed after use, confirmed via `grep`/`ls`/a Playwright-process check (via `Get-CimInstance`, filtering specifically on the `ms-playwright` executable path so the user's own real, separate Chrome browser processes were correctly identified and left untouched) that no trace or leftover process remains. The dev server was confirmed healthy (`200`) in its final state.

**Not built:** no fix for the Dev tab's sign-in buttons (see above, deliberately not shipped). No fix for the newly-found `/home` coach-mark overlay persistence bug (flagged, separate, out of scope). No React version downgrade experiment (flagged as the most promising untested next step, a decision too large to make unilaterally within this session).

### Session, August 23, 2026 (six-part session: git baseline, video letterboxing fix, Honest Exit stem chips, meetup-checkin trigger bug, video content replacement, coach-mark investigation)

Given the Dev tab's sign-in buttons are confirmed unreliable (prior session, near-0% success rate), every live verification in this session used the same session-minting workaround (`dev-create-session` + `verifyOtp`, injected into `localStorage` before navigating) rather than clicking through the Dev tab UI, per that session's own stated recommendation.

**Part 0, git baseline.** `git log --oneline` showed only the original "Initial commit" scaffold, confirming the earlier "git-baseline + video-bugs" prompt referenced in this session's own instructions had never actually been sent or run in this project's history (checked directly, not assumed). Confirmed `.gitignore` already excludes `node_modules`/`.env`/`.env*.local` and confirmed via `git check-ignore -v` and `git ls-files` that neither is tracked. Staged and committed everything as one baseline commit (`71be45e`), confirmed no secrets/credentials among the 220 newly-tracked files before committing. Not pushed to any remote.

**Part 1 and Part 2, one shared root cause: video letterboxing, not "no video" or "black box" as separately reported.** Investigated Part 1 first, per instruction, not assuming it was already fixed: confirmed `guides.tsx` does correctly route to `/module/[id]` (the same file already wired to `GuideVideoPlayer` in the prior video-hosting session), so on paper this should have worked. Live-tested the real Guides-list-to-video flow and found it technically DID render a `<video>` element with the correct URL and no console errors, but the screenshot revealed the real bug: a solid black frame with controls crammed into a thin strip. Checked the video's actual dimensions directly (`videoWidth`/`videoHeight`, confirmed via a real loaded `<video>` element, not assumed from the file): all three real guide videos (`module_curiosity.mp4`, `module_show_up.mp4`, `meetup-day-anxiety.mp4`) are genuine 1080x1920 **portrait** recordings, but `GuideVideoPlayer` hardcoded a 16/9 (landscape) container with `contentFit: "contain"`, severely pillarboxing the real content down to a thin vertical sliver, exactly explaining both the "no video" report (Part 1, Guides) and the "black box, no visible controls" report (Part 2, `NextMeetupFeelingCard`'s anxiety video) as the same underlying defect, not two separate bugs. Fixed with a one-line change (`aspectRatio: 16/9` to `9/16`) in `guide-video-player.tsx`. Verified live, all three videos: real playback confirmed (`currentTime` advancing after `play()`), and screenshots now show full-frame, correctly-proportioned real video content (a person on their phone, a person folding laundry, a person at a doorway) for `module_curiosity`, `module_show_up`, and the anxiety video (tested via a real, isolated `next_meetup_date`-confirmed test connection, Maria Santos/Marcus Alvarez, deleted afterward).

**Part 3, Honest Exit message chips.** Changed `CONNECTION_END_TEMPLATES` (`src/lib/no-ghost.ts`) to carry both the original blueprint-verbatim full sentence (`text`, kept for reference, not discarded) and a new, shorter opening `stem` per template, each ending in `", "` so typing continues the sentence naturally. `end-connection-modal.tsx`'s `handlePickTemplate` now inserts `t.stem` instead of `t.text`; added the requested calm caption ("Add a word or two of your own before sending.") beneath the chip row. The existing `draftEdited` gate (already exactly "disabled until the user actually engages with the text, enabled the moment they type anything," the same convention used everywhere else in this app for AI-draft/template flows) needed no new logic, it already does what the task asked for once the inserted text was a stem instead of a complete, easy-to-send-untouched sentence. **A deliberate departure from the blueprint's own verbatim-wording table, flagged in the code comment rather than made silently**, matching this project's own established convention for this kind of deviation. Verified live on a fresh, isolated test connection (Aisha Bello/Robert Kim, deleted afterward): confirmed the exact stem text appears (not the old full sentence), confirmed the End connection button is genuinely disabled (`opacity: 0.4`, `aria-disabled: "true"`, `pointerEvents: "none"`, checked via the real Pressable wrapper's computed style, not just an unreliable ARIA-attribute guess) before typing, and genuinely enabled (`opacity: 1`, no `aria-disabled`, `pointerEvents: "auto"`) after typing one extra word, both confirmed with screenshots.

**Part 4, meetup-checkin trigger bug, real, confirmed, fixed.** The task's own quoted copy ("It's been a bit since you two talked about meeting up. How did it go?") does not belong to `FollowUpReflectionCard` at all, confirmed by grep, that component's real copy is a different, generic "something to follow up on or celebrate" prompt (`reflectionPrompt()` in `follow-up-reflection.ts`). The quoted text is `MeetupCheckinCard`'s, driven by `run_meetup_checkin_check`, a different component and a different trigger entirely, flagged here rather than silently fixing the wrong file. Read the real, currently-live function body directly via `pg_get_functiondef` (not a migration file in isolation, since this function has been revised more than once): `v_date_driven` is only ever `true` once `next_meetup_status = 'confirmed'`, never for `'proposed'`, so a real, live, not-yet-confirmed proposal falls all the way through to the unrelated elapsed-time fallback (`last_plan_activity_at` + 7 days), which has no awareness a pending proposal exists. Fixed by adding one explicit guard: a genuinely `'proposed'` (not yet confirmed) date now returns a new, honestly-labeled `'date_pending_confirmation'` outcome instead of falling through, leaving the `'confirmed'`-and-passed path and the true `no-date-ever-proposed` fallback path both completely unchanged. Also added the missing entry to `dev.tsx`'s own `CHECKIN_RESULT_MESSAGES` map so this new outcome shows a real explanation instead of the generic "unrecognized result" fallback. Verified live via three real, separate isolated test connections (all deleted afterward): (1) a genuinely reproduced repro of the reported bug (proposed, unconfirmed, date 3 days in the future, `last_plan_activity_at` backdated 10 days, i.e. exactly the combination that would have fired under the old code) now correctly returns `date_pending_confirmation` with zero `meetup_checkins` rows created; (2) the same connection, once confirmed and the date backdated into the past, correctly returns `fired`; (3) a separate, genuinely no-date-ever-proposed connection with a 10-day-stale `last_plan_activity_at` still correctly returns `fired`, confirming the legitimate fallback case (explicitly asked to be preserved) is untouched.

**Part 5, "How We Show Up" content replacement.** Replaced `module_show_up`'s `concept` (the two summary paragraphs) and `scenario` (question, both choice labels, both feedback lines) in `modules-data.ts` with the given content verbatim for the concept bullets, question, and options; feedback text for both choices was written fresh (not given verbatim in the task), drawn directly from the two given summary bullets' own language rather than invented independently. Verified live: the real page (via Guides -> How We Show Up) shows all four pieces of new text exactly, confirmed the old "awkward pause after a joke" scenario is genuinely gone (explicit negative check, not just confirming the new text's presence), screenshot confirms correct rendering (video area shows the same legitimate paused-first-frame black state already established as normal in Parts 1/2, not a regression).

**Part 6, coach-mark investigation, two real findings, one hypothesis ruled out.**
- **The task's own leading hypothesis (stale local `visible` state surviving an account switch) was tested directly and did NOT reproduce.** Reproduced the real underlying mechanism precisely, not approximated: temporarily exposed the app's own `supabase` client on `window` (`src/lib/supabase.ts`, reverted immediately after this investigation, confirmed via grep), then, within one single, never-reloaded page session, called `supabase.auth.signOut()` followed by the exact same `verifyOtp({type:'magiclink', token_hash})` call `devSignInAs()` itself makes internally, switching from Aisha Bello (who had just correctly dismissed the `tab_discover` mark) to Priya Nair (who had never seen it). Screenshot confirms the mark correctly reappeared for Priya, with a real dimming overlay, ring, and tooltip, on the exact same, never-remounted page. This specific, most-likely-sounding hypothesis is ruled out with direct evidence, not just theory.
- **A real, different, verified finding: a coach mark's dimming overlay blocks all other interaction, including tab navigation, until that specific mark is explicitly dismissed.** Found while testing "moving between tabs": dismissing `tab_discover` and navigating to Browse for the first time (a screen whose own `tab_browse` mark had never been seen) correctly triggered that mark, and Playwright's own actionability check then reported a genuine intercepting element (`<div class="css-view-g5y9jx"></div>`, confirmed via `elementFromPoint` to be the real, root-mounted `SpotlightHost` dimming layer, not a red herring this time, unlike the invisible-placeholder confusion from earlier in the same investigation) blocking the "Discover" tab-bar link until that new mark was addressed. Confirmed this genuinely reflects the mark being unseen (checked `coach_marks_seen` directly), not stale state. **Flagged rather than fixed**: this is very plausibly intended, standard spotlight/tour UX (block interaction with everything but the highlighted target until dismissed), and deciding whether that's the right behavior for this app's own "gentle, once, dismissible hint" design intent (see the 2026-08-20 session's own stated goals) is a real product call, not something to silently resolve either way.
- **A real test-script red herring, resolved before being mistaken for a product bug:** `CoachMark`'s own invisible self-measuring placeholder (`opacity: 0` on its wrapper `View`, per its own documented design) reports `getComputedStyle(textNode).opacity === "1"` when checked on the inner text node directly (CSS opacity is a compositing effect on the whole subtree, not an inherited computed property value on each descendant), meaning a naive opacity check on the "Got it" text itself cannot distinguish the real tooltip from the invisible placeholder. The two are reliably distinguished by screen position instead (the real `SpotlightHost` tooltip anchors near its target, the invisible placeholder sits inline in the screen's own normal content flow). Documented here so a future investigation doesn't re-lose time to the same trap.
- **"Show tips again" (Settings) re-confirmed working correctly**, unaffected by every intervening session's changes since it was last verified (2026-08-20): a real account (Marcus Alvarez) with `tab_discover` marked seen, tapping the button produced the real confirmation text ("Done, the first-time tips will show again as you visit each screen."), and a fresh revisit of Discover correctly showed the mark again.
- All touched `coach_marks_seen` rows (Aisha Bello, Jordan Blake, Sam Rivera, Robert Kim, Marcus Alvarez) restored to their exact pre-session state afterward, confirmed via a direct before/after query per account, not assumed from the test scripts' own exit codes.

Migration `20260823000000` applied via `supabase db query --linked --file`. All isolated test connections (Parts 1/2, 3, 4x2) fully deleted afterward, independently reconfirmed via requery. The temporary `window.__DIAG_SUPABASE__` exposure (Part 6) was fully reverted, confirmed via grep. Temporary Playwright test/diagnostic scripts and screenshots (well over a dozen `part*_tmp.js`/`.png`/`check_*_tmp.js` files) and the temporarily reinstalled `playwright` dependency were removed after use, confirmed via `grep`/`ls`/a `Get-CimInstance` process check (correctly distinguishing the temporary `ms-playwright`-path automation browser from the user's own real, separate Chrome, which was never touched). ### Session, August 24, 2026 (git commit, non-blocking coach-mark overlay, standalone Guide library, Back-navigation audit)

**Part 1, commit.** Confirmed via `git status --porcelain` that the 5 files plus the one migration from the prior session were still the only pending changes, then committed them (`337d4d3`, on top of the `71be45e` baseline).

**Part 2, coach-mark overlay made non-blocking.** Root cause confirmed directly in the prior session: `SpotlightHost`'s dimming-region `View`s never set `pointerEvents`, defaulting to `'auto'`, so a purely visual dimming layer silently intercepted real taps on whatever sat underneath it (the reason a first-time coach mark on a freshly-visited tab could block navigating to a different tab until explicitly dismissed). Fixed with a single, targeted change: `pointerEvents: 'none'` added to each dimming region's style in `spotlight-host.tsx`. Nothing about the mark's own seen/dismissed tracking, ring, or tooltip was touched, matching the explicit instruction to change only the blocking behavior. Verified live on a genuinely fresh, unseen `tab_discover` mark (Maria Santos): tapping the Browse tab while the mark was still showing succeeded with zero interception error (previously this reproducibly failed with Playwright's own actionability check reporting the exact intercepting element), confirmed by URL change to `/browse`; on returning to Discover, the mark still displayed correctly and its own "Got it" dismiss still worked cleanly, confirming the fix only removed the unwanted blocking, not the mark itself.

**Part 3, standalone Guide library.** Built a new, separate route (`src/app/guide/[id].tsx`), reusing `getModule`/`getModuleVideoUrl` but rendering only Back, title, `GuideVideoPlayer`, and a new `libraryDescription` field, no quiz, no scenario, no "Module X of 2" chrome. `guides.tsx` now routes conditionally: the 2 modules with a real video (checked via `getModuleVideoUrl`, not a hardcoded id list) go to `/guide/[id]`, all other 9 entries continue routing to `/module/[id]` exactly as before, untouched. The original `module/[id].tsx` (the real onboarding flow's screen) was not modified.

Added a new optional `libraryDescription` field to `ModuleDef` (`modules-data.ts`), populated only for the 2 video-bearing modules, condensed from each module's own existing `concept` array, no new facts introduced. **Exact text written, as requested, for review:**
- `module_curiosity`: *"A profile is just a glimpse, not a whole person, so curiosity beats a snap judgment every time. A shared interest can open a door, and so can a real difference. You don't need instant chemistry to know something's worth continuing, one conversation is incomplete information on its own. An awkward moment doesn't mean it's not working either, the other person is probably just as unsure as you are."*

That doesn't mean you owe anyone continued contact, though. If something feels unsafe, you can leave, block, or report, no explanation required."*

Added a `GuideVideoThumbnail` export to `guide-video-player.tsx` (a small, `nativeControls={false}`, never-played `VideoView`), shown in the Guides list only for the 2 video-bearing entries, relying on the browser/native video element's own standard "decode and display the first frame while paused" behavior rather than generating any new poster image asset, per instruction. **Honest limitation, not glossed over:** both real videos' true first frame is genuinely dark/near-black (already established as expected, correct content in an earlier session, not a rendering bug), so the resulting thumbnails, while technically correct and asset-free exactly as specified, aren't visually distinctive at a glance; fixing that would mean either seeking to a later frame (not "the video's own first frame" as asked) or generating a real poster image (the explicit alternative the instruction said to avoid), so left as-is and flagged rather than silently picked one way. **Native feasibility genuinely unverified, flagged rather than assumed:** this environment has no iOS/Android simulator (the same standing limitation already documented repeatedly in this project for deepidv/IAP/voice-to-text), so whether a paused, uncontrolled `VideoView` reliably shows a first frame on native the same way it does on web was not and could not be tested here.

Separately confirmed the real onboarding flow (`etiquette-modules.tsx`, reached via its own route, not through Guides) is completely unaffected: still shows "Module 1 of 2", the full original quiz prompt and choices, and the same real video, exactly as before this session.

**Part 4, Guides list Back link.** Built as part of Part 3's own edit to `guides.tsx` (adding the standard `Pressable onPress={() => router.back()}` "Back" link at the top, matching `module/[id].tsx`'s and the new `guide/[id].tsx`'s own established pattern), confirmed present in the same live verification pass above.

### Session, August 25, 2026 (thumbnail seek fix, anxiety video added as its own Guides entry)

**Part 1, thumbnail seek fix.** Checked actual frame content directly rather than guessing at a timestamp, per instruction: with no `ffmpeg` available in this environment, used a real headless browser (`<video>` element, real `seeked` events, real screenshots) to capture and visually inspect all three real guide videos at t=0/1/2/3/5/8 seconds. Confirmed t=0 is genuinely dark/near-black for all three (not assumed, not a rendering bug, matching what earlier sessions already established for the full player). t=2s produced real, distinctive content for all three but landed mid-word in each video's caption ("not a complete", "some", "to"); t=1s produced equally distinctive content with complete caption phrases instead ("a profile is a glimpse.", "every friendship has", "crossing the threshold"), so 1 second was used as a single shared default rather than tuning three separate per-video values, since it worked cleanly for all three without needing to.

`GuideVideoThumbnail` (`guide-video-player.tsx`) now takes an optional `seekSeconds` prop (default `1`) and uses `useEventListener(player, 'statusChange', ...)` (imported from `expo`, confirmed via the installed package's own type definitions, not `expo-video`, which doesn't re-export it) to wait for the player to reach `'readyToPlay'` before setting `player.currentTime`, rather than seeking immediately on mount, which would silently no-op before the player has real data loaded. Still no new poster/image asset of any kind, matching the explicit instruction, the browser/native video element's own real decoded frame is what renders, just at a real, chosen, non-zero timestamp instead of t=0.

**Part 2, anxiety video added as its own Guides entry.** Investigated for real source material before writing anything, per explicit instruction not to invent facts: searched this repo, the Downloads folder, and the actual video source folder (`~/OneDrive/Desktop/limen-videos/`) directly for anything referencing "video-3" or "does-not-need-to-be-perfect" (the source filename) or a video3/video2 storyboard alongside the one real `video1_storyboard.png` that does exist. Found none. `limen-spec-v6.md` (the most likely candidate document) explicitly states on its own line 8, *"Per instruction, video/Guide-library content details are omitted from this version,"* confirming no such source exists in this project right now, not that it was merely missed. Per the given fallback instruction, used only `NextMeetupFeelingCard`'s own existing, already-shipped intro line ("That's a completely normal way to feel before meeting up. A couple of thoughts before you go.") as the sole grounding, and kept the result deliberately brief rather than elaborated. **Exact text written, for review, same as the review step used last session:**
- Title: *"Feeling Nervous Before a Meetup"*
- Short list-row line: *"A short note for the nerves before a meetup."*
- Standalone-screen description: *"For the moments before a meetup when nerves show up: a completely normal way to feel, with a couple of thoughts to carry with you before you go."*

**Data-structure decision, made and reported per instruction.** Checked `ModuleDef`'s type directly: it requires non-optional `concept: string[]` and `scenario: {prompt, choices}`, both meaningful, real fields its other two consumers (`etiquette-modules.tsx`'s onboarding filter, `module/[id].tsx`'s quiz screen) genuinely depend on. Forcing the anxiety entry into `MODULES` would have meant inventing fake concept/scenario content for an entry that should never reach either of those two screens at all. Built a separate, smaller type instead (`GuideOnlyEntry` in a new `src/lib/guide-only-entries.ts`, `{id, title, description, libraryDescription}`, no `concept`/`scenario`/`mandatory` field at all), and gave `guide/[id].tsx` its own resolution logic that checks `MODULES` first, then `GUIDE_ONLY_ENTRIES`, merging both into the one `{title, description, videoUrl}` shape that screen actually needs. `module/[id].tsx` and `etiquette-modules.tsx` were not touched and have no way to reach this new entry. `module-videos.ts` gained a small, separate `getGuideOnlyEntryVideoUrl()`/`GUIDE_ONLY_VIDEO_FILES` map pointing the new entry's id at the same real `meetup-day-anxiety.mp4` file `getMeetupAnxietyVideoUrl()` already serves for `NextMeetupFeelingCard`'s own contextual "Nervous" branch, deliberately a separate function/id from that one, not a shared or renamed one, so that existing, unrelated usage was not touched at all.

**Placement decision, made and reported per instruction:** right after the 2 existing onboarding video entries (Begin With Curiosity, How We Show Up), before the 9 text-only modules. Reasoning: it's the third real, produced video in the list, and grouping the three real videos together felt like the more discoverable ordering for someone browsing, rather than burying a real video after 9 unrelated text-only entries. `guides.tsx` was refactored to build one ordered `GuideRow[]` (first 2 `MODULES`, then `GUIDE_ONLY_ENTRIES`, then the remaining 9 `MODULES`) and render it through one shared `GuideListRow` component, rather than duplicating the row JSX for the new entry.
